​Why is my pledge still not going through after I confirmed the 3DS authentication challenge?

This can happen for a few reasons, such as a decline from your bank, card limits or restrictions, or the 3DS verification step timing out before it was completed.

Please also be aware that your mobile device may not support the 3DS technology from your bank so you’ll want to try to pledge and complete the authentication challenge on your desktop.

If your bank has given you the greenlight that the pledge transaction should go through, you will need to try again. We do not try to re-authenticate automatically.

If you continue to experience issues, please re-attempt your pledge using a different payment method.

What is a Superbacker?

A Superbacker is a user who has supported more than 25 Kickstarter projects with pledges of at least $10 in the past year.

Superbackers are at the heart of our backer community, with many offering advice to creators, guiding other backers, and helping to introduce projects to new audiences.

How do I know how close I am to Superbacker status?

You will be able to check your Superbacker status on your profile page. If you are already a Superbacker, you will see a Superbacker badge underneath your profile image. You can choose whether or not you’d like this badge to display to other users on your Account settings page.

If you’re not yet a Superbacker, you will see a status progress tracker there instead. This is only visible to you, regardless of your privacy settings. On this tracker, you can see how many projects you have backed that count toward Superbacker status. From here, you can also easily discover projects to back, so that you can unlock Superbacker status and all its perks.

How will people know I’m a Superbacker?

When you become a Superbacker, a badge will be added to the “About” tab of your profile page. This badge will also show up next to your profile name when leaving a comment on a project page. Additionally, you’ll receive an email from Kickstarter congratulating you on becoming a Superbacker.

What special features do Superbackers have access to?

Superbackers will receive occasional email updates with inside news from Kickstarter (make sure you’re subscribed to receive these), as well as the opportunity to test new features and provide feedback to our team. Superbackers may also receive invitations to Kickstarter events and have opportunities to be featured in Kickstarter’s marketing channels.

Quoted Comments

As a Superbacker, you will also have access to the Quoted Comments feature. Using this feature, a Superbacker can highlight a specific area of text on a project’s campaign, up to 300 characters, and start a comment.

This feature is intended to spark a conversation between backers and the creator, and to ask questions about a specific element of the project. This may include asking for clarification where needed, or for the creator to substantiate a claim.

To use this feature, simply highlight a line of text in the project’s description. When a section of text has been highlighted, a box will pop up asking if you would like to quote this text. Clicking “Start a comment” will take you to the comments section, where the selected text will appear as a quote at the top. Write your comment or question beneath the quoted text.

Note: This feature is only available on projects that you’ve backed.

Please note that at this time Quoted Comments is only available via desktop browsers.

What is Apple Pay and how do I set it up?

Please be informed that Apple Pay will be temporarily unavailable on Kickstarter.com until otherwise stated. Apple Pay will still be available in the iOS Kickstarter app, and pledges can be completed with a credit or debit card.

Apple Pay is Apple’s mobile payment service, it allows you to use your iPhone’s stored credit or debit cards to make payments easily using your phone. To use it to pledge on Kickstarter you’ll need to make sure that you’re using one of the following devices: iPhone 6 and later models, Apple Watch-compatible devices (iPhone 5 and later models), iPad Air 2, iPad Pro and iPad Mini 3 or later.

You can find detailed instructions for how to install Apple Pay on your device here.

You can also use Apple Pay on the Web to pledge to projects on your computer. In order to do so you will need to make sure that you have Apple Pay set up on your iPhone, that you are using the latest version of Safari, and that your computer and mobile device are connected to the same Wi-Fi and Bluetooth networks.

If you meet the requirements for Apple Pay on the Web, you’ll see an Apple Pay link in the rewards section of a project. Once you’ve selected your reward, you’ll need to authenticate your pledge using your iPhone.

Check here to see if your bank or credit card supports Apple Pay, and here to see if Apple Pay is supported in your country.

What is the minimum and maximum amount I can pledge to a project?

The maximum amount you can pledge to a project varies based on a project’s country of origin. It is also not possible for a project creator to set a reward tier higher than the maximum pledge amount allowed for their project’s location.

The maximum amount you can pledge to a project is:

  • US-based projects: $10,000
  • UK-based projects: £8,000
  • Canada-based projects: $13,000 CAD
  • Australia-based projects: $13,000 AUD
  • New Zealand-based projects: $14,000 NZD
  • Mexico-based projects: $200,000 MXN
  • Japan-based projects: ¥1,200,000 JPY
  • Netherlands, Germany, Italy, Austria, Belgium, Luxembourg, France, Spain, Ireland, Greece, and Slovenia-based projects: €8,500
  • Sweden-based projects: 85000 SEK
  • Denmark-based projects: 65000 DKK
  • Norway-based projects: 80000 NOK
  • Switzerland-based projects: 9500 CHF
  • Hong Kong-based projects: 75000 HKD
  • Singapore-based projects: 13000 SGD
  • Poland-based projects: 37,250 PLN

Additionally, there is a minimum amount that you can pledge to a project. This amount also varies based on a project’s country of origin.

The minimum amount you can pledge to a project is:

  • US-based projects: $1
  • UK-based projects: £1
  • Canada-based projects: $1 CAD
  • Australia-based projects: $1 AUD
  • New Zealand-based projects: $1 NZD
  • Mexico-based projects: $10 MXN
  • Japan-based projects: ¥100 JPY
  • Netherlands, Germany, Italy, Austria, Belgium, Luxembourg, France, Spain, Ireland, Greece, and Slovenia-based projects: €1
  • Sweden-based projects: 5 SEK
  • Denmark-based projects: 5 DKK
  • Norway-based projects: 5 NOK
  • Switzerland-based projects: 1 CHF
  • Hong Kong-based projects: 10 HKD
  • Singapore-based projects: 2 SGD
  • Poland-based projects: 5 PLN

When is my card charged?

If the project you’re backing is successfully funded, your card will be charged when the campaign reaches its funding deadline. When a campaign ends without reaching its funding goal, your card will never be charged.

Similarly, if a project is canceled before it reaches its campaign deadline, then your pledge will not be collected and no additional action will be required.

If you are Late Pledging to a project after its campaign has ended successfully, your card will be charged immediately.

Some backers may see a temporary authorization of funds at the time of pledging, before a project has successfully funded. Authorizations generally drop after a few days, but this process can vary depending on your financial institution. If you notice an authorization that doesn’t drop, we recommend contacting your bank or card issuer for more details about their authorization procedure.

Getting involved

Does Kickstarter have a responsible security disclosure procedure?

We sure do. If you’ve discovered a potential bug or vulnerability with Kickstarter.com, please contact us here and we’ll help make sure the information gets shared with our engineering team.

Is there a place I can download the Kickstarter logo?

Yes! The Kickstarter Brand Assets page has hi-resolution versions of the Kickstarter logo and other assets for creators, backers, and members of the press to download.

I’ve received an email from someone claiming to work at Kickstarter about a job offer. Is it real?

We’ve received reports of employment scams operating under the guise of being a job recruiter for Kickstarter.

It’s important for anyone on the receiving end of these messages to note that Kickstarter does not do the following:

  • Communicate job offers via email addresses not affiliated with @kickstarter.com
  • Conduct interviews via instant messenger or google hangouts
  • Require you to spend money for purchase of equipment or services
  • Offer to send a check to cover costs related to equipment purchases

We recommend checking our jobs page to see if the job description offered matches our available positions.

If you receive a message that appears suspicious, feel free to contact us via employment-scams [at] kickstarter.com. Per the FBI’s recommendations, we also encourage you to file a complaint with the Federal Trade Commission’s Consumer Sentinel or the Internet Crime Complaint Center.

I’m interested in working for Kickstarter, how do I apply?

When a role opens up at Kickstarter this will be posted on our Jobs page. You can find out more about each open role by clicking on the position, and if one sounds like something you’d be interested in, you can apply via the “Apply for this job” button.

Someone is offering to hire or pay me to help with a Kickstarter campaign

Before creating a campaign, companies may post advertisements requesting marketing, design, and other content assistance on contracting sites, such as Upwork and Fiverr.

Kickstarter is not affiliated with any of these parties and we do not have any insight or control into contracts made off platform. We strongly encourage creators to do their due diligence in researching the party who wishes to hire you, before sharing any personal information they are asking for. As a general reminder, your full name, address, social security number and/or tax information, should only be shared with trusted employers. Additionally, we encourage you to monitor the Kickstarter project throughout the stage of the campaign.

Getting rewards

Can I change my shipping address after I complete a backer survey or pledge manager?

If the creator is using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager or Backer Survey tool to collect backers’ addresses, and they haven’t locked addresses, you can update your shipping address by following the steps below. Please note that changing your submitted address is currently only possible on a web browser.

  • Log in to your Kickstarter account and navigate to the Backed projects page.
  • Next, locate the specific project under the ‘Successful pledges’ section and click the round “+” button to the right-hand side of the project name.
  • On the Pledge summary page, if the creator has sent their Pledge manager you’ll be able to select the ‘Pledge manager’ tab to see your current shipping address.
  • If the creator hasn’t yet locked addresses for the project, you’ll see an option here to change your address. Please note that changing the country from the one selected during pledging or completion of the pledge manager will no longer be possible at this point.

If you have multiple projects with open surveys or pledge managers, and you’d like to update your shipping information on all of them at once, you can update all eligible pledges with the same address from the Shipping address tab, found under the Settings page of your Kickstarter account. To do so:

  • Make sure you’ve saved the new address to your ‘Saved addresses’ list. If you have saved addresses stored already, you should see them listed on the right hand side of your screen.
  • Click on “Apply to all” on the desired address to apply this across all eligible surveys. Each address will indicate the number of eligible surveys and pledge managers available for updating with the selected address.
  • If the new address is in a different country than what was originally selected when pledging, you will not be able to update the address as shipping costs may change. In this case, you’ll need to reach out to the creator directly to discuss alternative delivery options.

Please note that, once a creator is ready to begin shipping rewards, they may lock addresses and you will have 48 hours to finalize any changes.

If the creator has locked addresses you should message the creator directly to see if an alternative arrangement can be made.

If the creator used a third-party pledge management service to collect information and you need to make any changes, you will need to contact the creator directly to request their help with this.

​I need to change the shipping address but I can’t see an option to do so

If the backer survey or pledge manager has closed and the creator has locked addresses, it’s likely that they are now finalizing plans to begin shipping rewards. If there are any changes to your shipping address we recommend reaching out to the creator as soon as possible, before shipping begins, by sending them a message through Kickstarter.

You could also post a comment on their project page to remind them to check their Kickstarter message inbox for your new address.

​How do I update the email address associated with my pledge?

If you’ve recently updated the email address linked to your Kickstarter account, this will now be associated with your pledge in each project’s backer report. However, if a creator has already downloaded the backer report for their project, or is using a third-party service for their pledge management, the creator will need to manually update your email address on their end.

We recommend reaching out to a creator directly to let them know that the email address associated with your pledge may need to be updated. In the event that they are using a third-party pledge management service, they may also need to re-send the survey to your new email address.

​Never post your full name, shipping address, telephone number, or email address in a comment - always send this privately as a direct message. All comments are public and can not be deleted.

Can I edit my pledge manager order?

After a pledge manager order has been completed, how much you can edit will depend on the permissions set by the creator in their pledge manager.

Changing your survey response

If a creator has allowed for edits to be made to survey responses, you will be able to do this on the Pledge summary page for the project. To see if this is available for your pledge, follow the steps below:

  • Head to your Backed projects page, locate the specific project under the ‘Successful pledges’ section, and click the round “+” button to the right-hand side of the project name.
  • Next, select the ‘Pledge manager’ tab. If the creator is currently allowing edits, you will see an “Edit order” button at the top of this tab.
  • Clicking this button will take you to a page where you can choose to edit your survey response.

The window for editing survey responses will be determined by the creator. Once they have stopped allowing edits through Kickstarter, the “Edit order” button will no longer be available. If you need to make any further changes after the window has closed, we recommend contacting the creator directly to see if this is possible.

Please note: This is only available for surveys sent as part of the Kickstarter Pledge Manager. It will not be possible to change a response if the creator used the backer survey only.

Changing your shipping address

You will be able to change your shipping address to one within the same country, as long as the creator has not yet locked addresses. You can find the steps to do that here.

Changing your reward selection

Once you have submitted your response for the pledge manager and made any applicable payments, the creator will be able to change your reward selection by making an order edit for you. Note that a reward can be exchanged for one of equal or higher value, but not downgraded.

Once a reward tier order edit is submitted by a creator, you will see the new charges for the reward, and any applicable shipping and tax. You will also see shipping and tax from the original reward, and will be automatically refunded those costs once you confirm charges.

Increasing your order

If a creator has allowed for backers to increase their order with additional add-ons, you will be able to do this on the Pledge summary page for the project. To see if this is available for your pledge, follow the steps below:

  • Head to your Backed projects page, locate the specific project under the ‘Successful pledges’ section, and click the round “+” button to the right-hand side of the project name.
  • Next, select the ‘Pledge manager’ tab. If the creator is currently allowing edits, you will see an “Edit order” button at the top of this tab.
  • Clicking this button will take you to a page where you can choose to select optional add-ons.
    • Please note that it will not be possible to change or remove existing add-ons from your order.
  • For any new add-ons added to your order, you may be asked to answer any relevant survey questions before proceeding to the checkout page. After payment is complete your order will be updated.

The window for increasing your order will be determined by the creator. Once they have stopped allowing edits through Kickstarter, the “Edit order” button will no longer be available. If you need to make any further changes after the window has closed, we recommend contacting the creator directly to see if this is possible.

If the creator has locked addresses, or is no longer allowing edits to orders, you should message the creator directly to see if an alternative arrangement can be made.

The creator is offering rewards elsewhere before shipping to backers

As Kickstarter’s mission is to help creators bring their new creative ideas to life, they must be creating something new. We don’t permit offering something that’s already available somewhere else as a project’s end goal. If you see a live project offering an existing item as the main reward, you can report this to our Trust & Safety team by clicking “Report this project to Kickstarter” at the bottom of the project page.

In instances where a creator ran a successful campaign and is currently at the fulfillment stage, we sometimes see that they are able to get their product to the public via mass production faster than individually packaging and shipping rewards.

We understand that this is frustrating and expect creators to be honest and upfront with their backers when this is the case. It’s important to remember that backing a project on Kickstarter is not like buying or pre-ordering an existing item, and as a backer you helped the creator to bring their idea to life.

How do I know the project I backed wasn’t a scam?

In order to launch a project on Kickstarter, a creator must first go through steps to verify their identity, this ensures that they are a real person. The creator’s verified name is then displayed publicly in the creator bio.

We encourage backers to thoroughly check a project and its creator before deciding to pledge, we share some suggestions in our “How do I know a project creator is who they claim they are?” article.

Often when a project is experiencing delays we see that backers start to worry that the creator has abandoned their project. However, it’s important to understand that project delays are a normal part of the creative process. After all, it’s easy to underestimate how much time is needed to complete a project, which is why we ask creators for an estimate on when they hope to begin delivering rewards. The estimated delivery date is just that, an estimate, and is not a guarantee.

Kickstarter will reach out to creators when it’s evident that they have not been as communicative with their backers as we’d expect. For example, if we receive user reports that a creator has stopped communicating, we may investigate and reach out to the creator directly. We’ll share resources that may help them, and will encourage them to post a new update and reply to backer’s messages. Unfortunately, it’s not uncommon for a creator to feel overwhelmed when they are receiving a lot of comments and messages, and often are hesitant to post a new update when they see that their backer community is unhappy with their project’s progress.

If a creator hasn’t posted a new update in over 4 weeks, we recommend requesting an update via the project page as a first step.

In instances where a creator has become overwhelmed we ask that you are supportive and understanding, and avoid sending anything unkind. And most of all, be patient. Remember that there is a human being behind every project.

How do I provide a creator with my shipping address and reward details?

After a project has successfully funded and the creator is ready to begin fulfilling your order, they will start to collect any details they need in order to produce and ship rewards. To gather this information, a creator may use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool, our Backer Survey tool, or a third-party service.

For creators using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager this will include a survey where they can request the details they need. This may include questions about size, color preference, flavor choice, etc, as well as your shipping address. Kickstarter does not collect or store your shipping information during the pledging process.

​How do I find the backer survey or pledge manager for a project?

If the creator is using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager or Backer Survey tool to collect information, and you’re viewing the site through a desktop browser, you’ll see a banner at the top of the page for any projects awaiting a response. On our iOS and Android apps, you can access any pending surveys or pledge managers via your Activity feed.

You’ll also receive an email with a direct link to the backer survey or pledge manager when the creator launches it.

If you think you might have missed an email, don’t panic, simply log in to your Kickstarter account to check. If you’re having trouble locating a project’s backer survey or pledge manager within Kickstarter, please feel free to reach out to us and we’ll be happy to help.

​The link I received for a project’s backer survey or pledge manager isn’t working

If the link you were emailed isn’t working, there’s a good chance that you’re clicking the link while signed in to the wrong Kickstarter account.

To resolve, we recommend first fully logging out of Kickstarter on your device. Next, make sure to sign in with the email address associated with your pledge before clicking on the link in the email again.

If you’re still seeing an error message, please reach out to us from the account you used to pledge.

​I haven’t received a backer survey or pledge manager for a project I backed. When will I get it?

As with all things, creators will approach pledge management in a way that works best for them. Some may send a survey or launch their pledge manager as soon as the project ends, while others may wait until they’re ready to start preparing rewards for shipping. This could be a few weeks after the project ended, or six months later.

If you haven’t been asked to complete a backer survey or pledge manager via Kickstarter, it’s possible that the creator is still preparing this and will send it out when ready. Remember, each project is different and will have its own distinct timeline for production, pledge management, and shipping.

Alternatively, the creator may be using a third-party pledge management service, such as PledgeManager, to collect responses from backers. We recommend checking the project’s Update section for any information on this, or reaching out to the creator directly.

Once you complete a project’s backer survey or pledge manager through Kickstarter, you’ll receive an email confirmation that includes a copy of your response.

​How can I save my shipping address on Kickstarter prior to completing a backer survey or pledge manager?

You can save your preferred shipping address to your account to have it ready to add to future backer surveys or pledge managers sent via Kickstarter.

To save your shipping address, log into your account, and visit the Shipping address tab on the Settings page of your account. Here you’ll be able to add your address by filling out the required fields and clicking “Save”.

When completing a backer survey or pledge manager, you’ll be able to auto-populate your preferred shipping information by choosing from a list of your saved addresses. Please note: We will create a new saved shipping address for you any time you add an address that isn’t already saved. A saved address can be deleted at any time via the Shipping address tab.

​Can I change the shipping country for my reward?

If the creator is using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, and is collecting shipping costs through the pledge manager only, you will be able to change your shipping country. This is provided the new country is one within the creator’s shipping zone. The amount you’ll pay for shipping will be determined by the country you select.

However, if a creator is using Kickstarter’s survey tool only, a third-party pledge management service, or they charged shipping while their campaign was live, it won’t be possible to change the shipping country through Kickstarter. If this is the case and you need to change your address to a different country, please contact the creator to see if this is possible.

​Never post your full name, shipping address, telephone number, or email address in a comment - always send this privately as a direct message. All comments are public and can not be deleted.

I haven’t received my reward yet

When a creator is building their project on Kickstarter, setting an estimated delivery date for each reward is a requirement. However, this is intended to give the creator a date to aim towards, rather than an assurance of when backers can expect to start receiving their rewards.

Remember, the estimated delivery date is an estimate, not a guarantee.

As a first step, we’d recommend checking the estimated delivery date for the specific reward that you selected, to see what month the creator had hoped to begin shipping. You can also check the pledge details to see if the creator has set a status for your reward.

Next, take some time to check the Updates section of the project page. This is the best place to check on the current status of the project. If the creator hasn’t posted a new update in a month, you’ll see the option to anonymously request a new update.

Every creator will approach their project differently, and from time to time they may hit an unexpected bump in the road or face an unavoidable delay. It’s also not uncommon for a creator to underestimate how much time it will actually take to bring their project to life. Unlike an online shop, there’s a chance something could prevent the creator from completing their project by the date they had initially aimed for.

When a creator is facing a delay to their estimated delivery date we expect them to communicate these setbacks and keep backers in the loop.

If after requesting a new update via the project page the creator still hasn’t posted an update, the next step should be to send them a message through Kickstarter to check in and see how things are going. You could also post a comment on their project page.

A project I backed isn’t able to fulfill, what does this mean for the backers?

Creators launch projects on Kickstarter to bring their creative idea to life, but in order to do that they need the support of backers. When you choose to back a project on Kickstarter, you are supporting that creator in their endeavour to try and create something new.

By launching a project on Kickstarter, a creator is inviting people to form a contract with them. Anyone who backs a project is accepting that creator’s offer, and forming that contract. More information about this can be found in our Terms of Use*.

We do our best to support creators when it doesn’t look like they will be able to finish their project as they had initially hoped, and we expect them to bring it to a satisfying conclusion for their backers. This includes letting backers know what it is that is preventing them from fulfilling, and how the funds they raised on Kickstarter were used.

​Can I get a refund?

The funds that you pledged to a project went directly to the creator to help them with bringing their creative idea to life. If the creator has already used the funds to pay for project costs, they may not be in a position to offer refunds.

Remember, backing a project on Kickstarter is not the same as buying an existing item in a store, and as such receiving a reward in return for pledging is not guaranteed.

​*Note: These terms went into effect on January 1, 2025, and apply to all projects launched on or after that date. At the bottom of the Terms of Use page you can find versions of our Terms of Use from 2012 to the present. The October 2012 terms only apply to projects launched on or before October 18th, 2014.

Recent Changes to EU Import Duties (Getting rewards)

Changes to EU customs rules came into effect in July 2026. These changes will affect low-value shipments (under €150) sent from outside the EU to backers in the EU. You can read more about these changes below.

What is changing?

From 1 July, 2026, the EU is removing the existing customs duty exemption on goods valued under €150. A flat duty of €3 per item type will apply to these shipments. This is a transitional rate that will remain in place until 1 July, 2028.

Separately, some EU member states have introduced their own national handling fees on top of this duty:

  • France: €2 per goods type, effective 1 March, 2026
  • Italy: €2 per goods type, effective 1 June, 2026

Other EU member states are expected to follow with their own fees. Full details have not yet been published in all countries.

These charges apply per item type, not per parcel. A package containing two different product categories would attract €6 in EU customs duty, plus any applicable national fee.

Who pays these charges?

These costs are collected by customs authorities at the point of importation, not at checkout. Who bears the cost depends on the shipping terms agreed between the creator and their fulfilment partner:

  • If the creator ships on a duties paid basis (DDP), they or their fulfillment partner cover the charges.
  • If the creator ships on a duties unpaid basis (DDU), the backer may be asked to pay before or at delivery.

What does this mean for creators?

If you are shipping physical rewards to EU backers, we recommend:

  • Reviewing your shipping costs and reward pricing to account for these additional charges.
  • Checking how your fulfilment partner handles customs duties and declarations.
  • Ensuring your shipments use accurate product classifications, as duties are assessed per item type.

What does this mean for backers?

If you are an EU-based backer receiving rewards shipped from outside the EU, you may see additional charges applied at the point of delivery, depending on how the creator has arranged shipping. If you have questions about a specific order, we recommend reaching out to the project creator or your shipping carrier.

What about VAT?

Kickstarter collects VAT at checkout for eligible shipments under the IOSS scheme. These new customs duties and national fees are entirely separate from VAT and are not collected or remitted by Kickstarter.

We will continue to monitor developments and share further guidance as more details are confirmed by EU authorities. If you have questions, please reach out to your fulfillment partner or shipping carrier.

The reward I received is not what I expected

Kickstarter is a place for creators to bring their creative ideas to life, and we do our best to give them the support and freedom necessary to do so. We expect creators to make every effort to complete their project as promised. However, we also understand that creating something new can be a long and complicated process, with twists and turns along the way.

It’s not unusual for a reward to have gone through some changes, or new iterations, since the prototype stage, and it’s important to be prepared that what you receive may be a little different from what was initially presented. The creator may have had to source alternative materials, move production to another factory, or just simply had to revise their original plans.

Can I return or exchange my reward?

Kickstarter is not a store, and when you backed a creator’s project you weren’t buying or pre-ordering an existing product from them. For many creators they may not have the means to offer a return or exchange of the reward you received. However, as each creator will have their own policies in place we recommend checking with them directly.

There is an issue with my reward

I received a tracking number for my reward but it hasn’t updated

When creators are preparing their rewards, they may generate all shipping labels with their chosen shipping partner in one go to have everything ready. They may also send the information to backers ahead of shipping to help them keep track. Please keep in mind that receiving the tracking number does not mean that your reward is ready to ship. As a first step, make sure to check the creator’s updates to see where they are in their shipping plan.

If the creator hasn’t shared information about their shipping plan yet, and they haven’t posted a new update in a month, you’ll see the option to anonymously request a new update. If after two weeks you’re still unsure of the status of your reward, we’d recommend reaching out to the creator to check in. It’s important to be patient during this time, especially if the creator is in the middle of dispatching potentially hundreds of rewards.

My reward is stuck at customs

When a reward is being shipped to another country, the creator will have needed to fill in a customs form to declare the items being shipped and the value of the goods. Each country will handle imports differently, and it’s not uncommon for items to get held up. In these cases the only option is to be patient and wait for customs to clear your reward for delivery.

If there are any duties, taxes, or customs clearance fees levied on your reward, you should receive more information, alongside steps for paying this, from the shipping partner handling your shipment (e.g. FedEx, UPS, Royal Mail, etc).

Our article “Why am I being asked to pay customs fees for my reward?” shares more information on this.

In instances where details are missing from the customs form, or additional information is being requested, you will need to reach out to the creator directly. The creator is responsible for the fulfillment and shipping of rewards and will be best placed to help.

I only received part of my pledge

If you received your reward but there was something missing from your pledge, we recommend first checking the estimated delivery date for all items within your pledge. If everything was expected to ship together you should reach out to the creator.

In cases where a pledge included multiple items, and the project offered add-ons, the creator may have chosen to ship in stages. It’s possible that some rewards had a different estimated delivery date. If further information about the creator’s shipping plan can’t be located on their project page or in an update, we recommend getting in touch with them directly to clarify.

My reward has broken or stopped working

Each creator will have their own policies in place here, and some may have included a warranty with their rewards. If your reward has stopped working or broken within this timeframe you should reach out to the creator.

What can Kickstarter do when a creator does not fulfill their project rewards?

It’s not uncommon for a project’s expected timeline to change, and it’s important to remember that when a creator is behind on fulfillment that this does not mean a project is over. In instances where a project is experiencing delays we recommend taking a look at our “What happens if a project is delayed?” article for more information on next steps.

However, unfortunately, not every project will go as planned. When this happens we expect all members of the Kickstarter community to act with honesty and openness. For creators who have acknowledged that they are unable to finish their project as they had initially hoped, we ask that they bring their project to a satisfying conclusion by sharing an update that details how the funds were used, and what it is that’s preventing them from completing their project.

Our Community Support and Trust & Safety teams monitor Kickstarter’s system and review reports received from users. In cases where a creator isn’t fulfilling their rewards, and are not keeping their backers informed on the status of their project, some actions we may take include:

  • Sending an email to the creator, using the contact information they provided when they launched their project, urging them to post a new project update, as well as reminding them to take some time to respond to concerns from their backers.
  • Revoking creator privileges, including the ability to run another campaign on Kickstarter, until they’ve made a good faith effort to bring their project to a satisfying conclusion for their backers.
  • Suspending or restricting account privileges, if there is sufficient evidence of abuse against our system or trust of others. That being said, please note that fully funded projects and creator accounts are never completely deleted from the site for transparency reasons.

How does Kickstarter monitor the health of its system?

Our Trust & Safety team is always watching over the Kickstarter platform. They review reports from the community, proactively screen for potential problems, and use complex algorithms and automated tools to identify and investigate suspicious activity.

We also track creators’ fulfillment histories as comprehensively as possible - from snags to more serious complications - to help ensure creator accountability.

While we can’t speak to the nature of all actions taken by Kickstarter, we are actively reaching out to creators when they are falling short of our expectations, and, when necessary, placing restrictions on their account.

What do I do if there are additional shipping costs?

When planning a project, one of the more complicated steps the creator may face will be calculating the costs of shipping their rewards.

As a backer, it’s important to keep in mind that the creator is making something new. For many creators this will be their first time managing a project with production and shipping costs, and they may have limited experience in anticipating changes that could translate into unexpected fees. We do urge creators to research and plan all costs involved in completing their project, and ask that they communicate realistic expectations for backers, but sometimes unavoidable issues (and unforeseen costs) are inevitable.

Even with a thoroughly detailed shipping plan a creator can’t control all external factors. It could be that the shipping partner they were planning to use has increased their prices, or the packaging they had intended to use is no longer viable.

It’s not uncommon, or against our rules, for creators to wait until after the project ends to charge shipping. This way they can ensure that they are charging the correct amount needed to cover costs. If at checkout the shipping fee was zero, or an unusually low amount, we recommend double-checking the project page for any information the creator may have shared about their shipping plan while their project was live.

If you encounter a situation where a creator charged shipping while their campaign was live, but is now announcing additional costs, we recommend reaching out to the creator directly with any questions or concerns. Since it’s up to each creator to determine costs for their project, and Kickstarter is not involved in this process, they are the best person to address any concerns. While we know these kinds of obstacles are not optimal, we hope that backers and creators can work together to understand and overcome them.

We expect creators to bring their project to a satisfying conclusion, and understand that in some cases the costs involved with shipping may be what’s holding things up. Kickstarter isn’t able to resolve disputes between backers and creators, so in instances where there are additional shipping costs you will need to work with the creator directly to find a resolution.

What happens if a project is delayed?

We know that backing a project, and the anticipation of receiving your reward, can be exciting. However, it’s important to remember that when you decide to pledge towards a project on Kickstarter you are not purchasing, or pre-ordering, an existing item—you are helping to create something new.

​The estimated delivery date has passed

When a creator is building their project on Kickstarter, they are required to set an estimated delivery date. This date is intended as a goal for when the creator hopes to start shipping rewards, and should not be treated as a guarantee of when backers can expect to start receiving rewards. Remember, this date is an estimate.

Project delays can happen for a number of reasons and are to be expected when something new is being created.

Many projects on Kickstarter are in the early stages of development, which means as a backer you get to be a part of the creative process. It also means that a project can shift along the way, and unanticipated challenges may arise that delay the initial timeline laid out by the creator. This may be just a few weeks, but it is not uncommon for delays to go over six months. A delayed project does not mean it’s a failed project.

Typically, a creator is running a project from beginning to end all by themselves, or with just a small team. It’s also possible that they underestimated how long it would take to actually produce the rewards and get everything finished within a certain timeframe. They may have encountered an unavoidable bump in the road that means more time is needed.

Remember: backing is not buying, and delays do not mean a project is over.

Our article “I haven’t received my reward yet” shares some next steps we recommend when the estimated delivery date has passed.

The creator isn’t responding to messages

Our biggest expectation of creators, especially when a project is delayed, is that they communicate with backers through regular updates or messages, however each creator will approach how they handle this communication differently.

Oftentimes a project will have 100s, possibly even 1000s of backers, and a creator’s inbox can quickly become overwhelmed, resulting in it taking more time for them to get back to everyone. We recommend waiting a week or two for a response from a busy creator before sending a follow up or reporting the project to Kickstarter. If Kickstarter sees that a creator has failed to address requests for updates for a significant period, they will be contacted and their account may be restricted until they provide updates.

For many creators on Kickstarter, they ran a project to bring a passion project to life. As such they may also be working on their project alongside a full-time job or other commitments, so make sure to allow some time for the creator to get back to you, or address your concerns in a project update. If a creator hasn’t posted a new update in over 4 weeks, you can request an update via the project page.

In instances where a creator has become overwhelmed we ask that you are supportive and understanding, and avoid sending anything unkind. And most of all, be patient. Remember that there is a human being behind every project.

You can check this blog post for more information on communicating with project creators.

I no longer want the reward, can I have a refund?

It is up to each creator to decide whether they are in a position to offer refunds. If the funds have already been used to pay for production, materials, postage, or a person’s time, then a refund may not be possible.

Kickstarter is not a store and creators are responsible for the final delivery of their rewards, including deciding what their refund policy will be.

It’s important to keep in mind that requesting a refund while the project is still progressing could seriously impact a creator’s effort to bring their project to life.

What if something goes wrong after a project is successfully funded?

One of the best parts about being a backer and supporting creative projects on Kickstarter is getting a front row seat to the creative process. In exchange for your pledge, you get a window into what it’s like to bring something new to life, from concept to production. As a backer you can also opt to receive a reward in return for your financial support. However, sometimes – and despite a creator’s best intentions – things don’t go according to plan. It’s important to keep in mind that backing is not buying, and rewards are not guaranteed.

The estimated delivery date has passed and I haven’t received my reward

The first thing to note here is that the estimated delivery date is just that, an estimate.

This date is determined by project creators as their best guess for when backers will receive their rewards. It’s an estimate because it can often be tricky to determine exact dates so early on in the creative process, when a project is still in its beginning phase.

The project itself must first be completed before rewards can be fulfilled. As such, it’s not uncommon for refinements and modifications to push back a project’s estimated delivery date. In cases where a creator is delayed, we expect them to be open and honest with their backers, and properly communicate any changes to their project’s timeline. The estimated delivery date passing does not mean that you won’t still receive your reward.

I have questions or concerns about a specific project or reward

The first place we recommend looking if you have specific questions about a project is the Updates section of the project page. Creators are expected to share news of their progress and/or challenges in updates, so that’s often a good place to start.

In instances where the creator hasn’t posted a new update in the last 4 weeks, you can anonymously request a new update.

If the next update they post doesn’t address your specific question we recommend sending them a direct message through Kickstarter.

I still haven’t heard anything after reaching out to a creator

We expect creators to always keep their backers informed. However, each creator may approach their communication differently. Keep in mind that you may not receive a response right away – the creator is likely working hard to complete their project, and are communicating with backers as fast as they can. Don’t be dismayed if it takes longer than you expected to receive a response.

If you’ve already checked the Updates section, requested a new update, and reached out to the creator via Kickstarter direct messages, we recommend waiting a week or two before taking any further steps. A creator’s inbox can quickly become overwhelmed.

In some cases, creators may move their communications off of Kickstarter after their project ends, or they may be more responsive elsewhere. Check if the creator might have listed their own website, social media pages, or a direct email address in previous updates or in their creator bio. As a next step we recommend reaching out to a creator through their own website or social media. If Kickstarter sees that a creator has failed to address requests for updates for a significant period, they will be contacted and their account may be restricted until they provide updates to their backers.

What is a pledge manager and how do I complete it?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager provides creators with an easy tool to gather all the information they need in order to fulfill your reward. Using the pledge manager, a creator can collect your shipping address, reward preferences, as well as any applicable taxes and shipping fees, all in one place. It’s also a chance for you to finalize your reward and add-on selections before fulfillment begins.

When a creator launches the pledge manager you’ll receive an email with a direct link to finalize your pledge. If you’re viewing the site through a desktop browser, you’ll also see a banner at the top of the page for any pledge managers awaiting your response. To complete the pledge manager for a project, simply follow the steps below.

  • First, make sure that you are logged in to the correct Kickstarter account. Then, in the email you received, click the “Complete last steps” button to open the pledge manager.
  • On the ‘Welcome’ page, read the message the creator has written and click “Continue to pledge manager” to start submitting your information.
  • Next, you will see the reward tier you initially selected, if you backed the campaign while live or through late pledging. If the creator has configured any rewards to be available as an upgrade, you’ll see the option here to change to one of a higher tier, if any are available.
  • Note: If you pledged any amount for no reward during the campaign, this amount cannot be applied toward a reward upgrade in the pledge manager.
  • If the creator has made additional add-ons available in the pledge manager, the next page you’ll land on will be ‘Add-ons’. Here you’ll have the option to increase your pledge with any available add-ons. Simply click “Add” and adjust the quantity as needed.
    • Please note: When a reward tier is a digital one, it won’t be possible to add physical add-ons to your pledge.
    • If no add-ons are being offered by the creator, this page will not appear in the pledge manager.
  • When ready, click “Continue” and you’ll be taken to the ‘Survey’ page. Here, you can answer any questions from the creator and submit your item preferences. If you upgraded your reward or added any add-ons, the survey will automatically include questions relating to those rewards. When this section is complete, click “Continue”.
  • Next, you’ll need to confirm your address. This should be the address you would like the creator to ship your reward to. We will also use this address to determine how much tax may be due for your reward items.
    • If the creator is collecting shipping through the pledge manager, and you need to change the country your reward will be shipped to, you can do so here, provided the new country is included in the creator’s shipping zones.
  • After clicking “Continue” on the ‘Address’ page, you’ll be taken to the ‘Payment’ page. We recommend double-checking that everything is correct on this page. If there is a payment due, you can select a payment method and click “Pay” to finalize your pledge. If no payment is due, simply click “Complete” and you’re all set!

Remember: The creator will close the pledge manager when they are ready to move on to the next phase of their project.

You will receive notifications reminding you once a week while the pledge manager is open. Make sure to complete this before they close it. After the pledge manager has been closed you will need to work directly with the creator to see if an alternative arrangement can be made.

If you’re having trouble accessing the Kickstarter Pledge Manager through the email link or notification banner, another option is to access it via your Backed projects page.

  • Log in to your Kickstarter account and navigate to the Backed projects page.
  • Next, locate the specific project under the ‘Successful pledges’ section and click the round “+” button to the right-hand side of the project name.
  • On the Pledge summary page, select the ‘Pledge manager’ tab and click “Start pledge manager” to be taken to the pledge manager.
  • Follow the steps above to finalize your pledge and submit your response.

What is a Project Update Request?

We understand that it can be frustrating when a creator hasn’t posted a new update in a while, and at Kickstarter we expect creators to maintain a cadence of one new update a month. In instances where this has not happened we give the backers of that project the option to send an anonymous update request. After submitting a request, we will send an email to the creator letting them know that their backers have requested a new project update.

We encourage you to make an update request prior to direct messaging the creator, leaving a comment on the project page, or writing in to our Support team.

How can I submit a Project Update Request?

For an eligible project (one where the creator has not posted a new update in over 4 weeks and they are yet to finish fulfilling their rewards), you can make an update request from the top of the Updates section, or from the backer bar at the top of the project page after selecting either the neutral or negative feedback options.

I requested a project update, what should I do if the creator still hasn’t followed up?

While we expect and encourage creators to post an update once a month until they have finished fulfilling rewards, finding the time to write updates alongside managing a project can be difficult, especially in instances where the creator is doing everything on their own. Each creator will approach this in a different way and a lack of communication doesn’t mean that the creator has given up.

After requesting an update we recommend giving the creator at least a week or two before attempting any other means of requesting an update, such as sending them a direct message through Kickstarter. The creator may be receiving a lot of requests which may mean they are taking longer to get back to everyone.

What if the creator posts an update that doesn’t address my question?

For some creators, they may have planned their update content in advance, or have received a lot of questions that they are addressing as quickly as they can.

If the next update the creator posts doesn’t address your specific question we recommend sending them a direct message through Kickstarter.

Why can’t Kickstarter force a creator to post an update?

Every creator will have their own preference for how best to keep their backers in the loop. For some, this may be only posting an update when they feel that they have something sufficient to share. Other creators will enthusiastically post an update for their community every week without fail.

While we can’t force a creator to post an update, in instances where an update is long overdue we do reach out to creators to remind them of their obligation to their backers.

Our article “What can Kickstarter do when a creator does not fulfill their project rewards?” details further actions we may take.

Why am I being asked to pay customs fees for my reward?

When a creator sends a reward to another country they have a legal responsibility to properly declare the items being shipped, including the value of the goods, on a customs form. Any duties, taxes, VAT, or other customs clearance fees that may be levied on a reward as a result will be the responsibility of the backer. Each country will have their own rules in place with regards to imported items and we advise checking this prior to pledging.

Another thing to keep in mind is that not all creators will be shipping their rewards from the country that they launched their project from, and may instead be shipping them directly from where the rewards are being manufactured. It’s our expectation that creators are upfront about this while their project is live, but in instances where this information is not readily available we recommend checking with the creator.

It’s important to keep in mind that a project’s location does not guarantee that this is where rewards will ship from.

For EU-based backers

From 1 July, 2026, the EU has removed the existing customs duty exemption on goods valued under €150. A flat duty of €3 per item type will apply to these shipments. Some EU member states have also introduced their own national handling fees on top of this duty.

Please note that these charges will apply per item type, not per parcel. You can read more about the EU import duty changes here.

If you have questions about a specific order, we recommend reaching out to the project creator or your shipping carrier.

Why am I being asked to pay taxes for my pledge?

Kickstarter operates as a marketplace facilitator. This means we are legally responsible for collecting and remitting sales tax for items shipped to all 50 US states and Canada, as well as collecting VAT (value-added tax) on most items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK.

Creators using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager provided tax information for their reward items, and let us know where the rewards will be shipping from. This information enables Kickstarter to determine how much tax each backer is responsible to pay to ensure compliance with applicable tax regulations.

​Why is there a tariff surcharge on my order?

To help creators cover additional costs as a result of the U.S. import tariffs, we’ve made it possible for creators to add a tariff surcharge to their pledge manager. By being able to offset rising costs, we hope to make the fulfillment stage as easy as possible for creators, while encouraging them to be proactive and transparent with backers. If you have any questions for the creator about this surcharge we recommend reaching out to them directly.

Please keep in mind that during this stage of their project a creator may be overwhelmed. We ask that you remain supportive, understanding, and patient while they work to bring their creative project to life. Remember, there is a human being behind every project.

Getting started

As a Kickstarter creator, how can I comply with data privacy rules?

When you run a successful campaign on Kickstarter, you will receive some personally identifiable information (PII) from your backers, including their email address and survey responses. Under Kickstarter’s Terms of Use and Privacy Policy, all creators are required to keep backer information confidential, except as strictly necessary to communicate with backers directly and fulfill rewards. No information provided by or about backers should be used for any other purpose without first receiving consent from the backer as required by applicable data privacy laws.

To ensure that you have received backers’ consent to be added to an off-site newsletter, we recommend one of the following:

  • Add a simple yes/no question to your Backer Survey, asking your backers if they consent to being added to an off-site newsletter.
  • Post an update with a link for backers to directly sign-up to your off-site newsletter.

In addition, the European Union and United Kingdom’s General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), and the California Privacy Rights Act (CPRA), provides citizens or residents of the European Economic Area (EEA), the UK, and California, with certain rights over their personal data. This includes a right to access, correct, delete, and restrict processing of their data and requires a “lawful basis” for collecting and retaining their personal information. Any successful project on Kickstarter may have backers that are EEA, UK, or California residents and handling of their backer information would be subject to GDPR or CPRA.

All creators and any collaborators with whom creators share backer personal information are required to comply with requirements and laws set forth in Kickstarter’s Privacy Policy. This includes being prepared to respond to backer requests to provide or delete their personal information stored or used off of Kickstarter’s site, including copies of backer surveys you’ve downloaded from the creator dashboard. We also urge creators to review the GDPR and CPRA rules and consider consulting with a lawyer for guidance on how these rules and other applicable data privacy regulations could directly affect them.

Can business and nonprofit entities raise funds for projects?

Yes, business and nonprofit entities can raise funds for projects on Kickstarter. See our eligibility requirements for further details.

Nonprofits and charitable organizations can use Kickstarter to raise funds for specific projects or endeavors making something new and shareable, but not for general support for their cause or mission.

However, reward items from a project can be donated to charity, and up to 10% of campaign funds, provided this is disclosed at the time of submission. You’re also welcome to include information about donating future proceeds to charity on your project page.

As with planning any project on Kickstarter, it’s important to look at Our Rules before getting started.

​Can I make a donation to a charity?

Up to 10% of your campaign funds can be donated to charity. You are welcome to list this as a percentage or amount, however the amount must be capped at 10% (e.g. If your goal is $1,000, you can say you’ll donate $100). Please note that this must be disclosed at the time of submission.

Reward items from your project can also be donated to charity.

​Are there any restrictions for who can receive a donation?

Donations must be in the spirit of charity, with some connection to your project. While the charity does not need to be an official nonprofit or 501 (c)(3), it must not cross into personal fundraising, such as covering expenses or paying a bill.

​Are pledges tax-deductible?

Pledges on Kickstarter are generally not tax-deductible. However, some projects started by or with a 501 (c)(3) organization may offer tax deductions. If so, this would be mentioned on their project page.

In these cases, Kickstarter does not provide any official documentation for your records. Any project that states their pledges are tax deductible should be able to provide the necessary information. We recommend reaching out to the creator directly for more details.

​Please note: Kickstarter cannot provide advice about taxes, including how to provide receipts evidencing any tax deductibility of pledges. Creators should contact a lawyer or financial professional for any tax-related questions.

​If you’re part of a nonprofit organization that’s considering using Kickstarter, we’ve outlined a few case studies and best practices over on our blog.

You can also explore how other nonprofits are using Kickstarter to bring their ideas to life.

Can I choose my project’s display currency?

It’s not possible to choose a preferred display currency for your project.

By default, the currency your project will display and collect funds in is determined by the country of origin* you indicate when building your project. For example, if your banking and identity information are based in, say, Hong Kong, your project goal and reward costs will automatically be displayed in HKD. Once your project ends, the funds collected will be sent to you in HKD. If you’re in the UK, they’ll be in GBP, and so on.

Kickstarter does offer backers the option to set a preferred display currency when browsing projects. However, pledges will always be collected in the project’s native currency, not in a backer’s preferred display currency.

​*For projects launching from Denmark, Norway, Sweden, Switzerland, and Poland, creators now have the option to choose whether to run their project in their country’s native currency, or in Euros.

​

Can I launch my project on multiple platforms?

Running a successful project requires a lot of dedicated time and attention. Accordingly, we recommend focusing your efforts on managing just one campaign, on one site.

Kickstarter is all-or-nothing, and bringing all of your backers to a single place increases the chance of getting funded. Using a single platform for your project also cuts down on the confusion of sending multiple links and will help get your idea off the ground.

We see projects succeed where creators have a focused and comprehensive outreach strategy — which could be diluted by spreading your efforts across multiple funding platforms at once. While you may find your community is enthusiastic about backing you on one platform, they may be less inclined to back you in multiple places.

Can I run more than one project at once?

Generally, no. We limit this because running multiple live projects can confuse backers, split support, and dilute a creator’s attention and energy.

However, experienced creators in good standing may qualify for overlapping fulfillment privileges to be able to launch and manage a limited number of projects back to back, while fulfilling prior projects.

Who qualifies to run more than one project at once?

To qualify for an exception to run more than one project at once a creator must:

  • Be experienced: They have completely fulfilled at least four projects.
  • Be in good standing: They have demonstrated that they can abide by Our Rules and Community Guidelines, present their projects honestly and clearly, and fulfill their campaigns to a high standard. This means maintaining regular, open, and honest communication with backers and delivering rewards as promised.

Creators who fail to fulfill outstanding projects, do not maintain transparent communication with backers, or violate any other Kickstarter rules are no longer considered to be in good standing. These creators will lose their overlapping fulfillment privileges or other applicable site privileges.

How many projects can an experienced creator in good standing run back to back?

Creators who meet the above criteria will have the following limits on unfulfilled projects, based on the project category in which they have a demonstrated track record of fulfillment experience on Kickstarter.

Design and Technology Creators:

✓: Generally may launch and manage up to 2 unfulfilled projects back to back

X: Projects may not involve hardware or complex manufacturing

​Publishing and Comics Creators:

✓: Generally may launch and manage up to 6 unfulfilled projects back to back

​Creators in all other categories:

✓: Generally may launch and manage up to 3 unfulfilled projects back to back in their respective categories (e.g., Games, Food, Dance, etc.)

Creators may only use their overlapping fulfillment privileges in categories where they have a demonstrated track record of fulfillment experience.

Creators who have already reached the maximum number of unfulfilled projects will have to fulfill previous projects in order to be approved for future launches.

How can I be considered for overlapping fulfillment privileges?

There is no application process. The Trust & Safety team will assess a creator for overlapping fulfillment during submission review for their next project.

Can I schedule a reward or limit the quantity?

Can I schedule my rewards or add-ons?

Sometimes it’s useful to add more rewards as your campaign progresses, and other times you may wish to offer a particular reward for only a limited time, such as the first day of your campaign. In order to do this, you can schedule rewards to go live on your project page on a certain date during your campaign, and you can also limit the amount of time that a reward is available.

To do so, select “Custom date and time” under both the ‘Start’ and ‘End’ sections under ‘Availability’, and you’ll be able to set the start and end date for the reward tier or add-on.

How do I limit the quantity of a reward or add-on?

If you are only planning to create a limited run of an item, have a maximum capacity, or just want to offer early bird backers an exclusive reward, you can limit the quantity when creating your reward tier or add-on. To do so, select “Limited” for ‘Project quantity’ under the ‘Availability’ section and you’ll be able to set the maximum number available. For add-ons, you can also limit the amount that any individual backer may select, whether the add-on quantity is limited or unlimited. This number defaults to 10 unless you set a different value.

For example, if you want to offer 20 VIP tickets to your film’s premiere as a reward, you can limit the reward to 20. As soon as 20 people back this reward tier, it will be marked as no longer available and other backers won’t be able to select it.

Why are my rewards showing as “All gone”?

Rewards will show as “All gone” if they are set to start on a date that has not yet been reached, end on a date that has now passed, or if the quantity has been limited and pledged to already.

To make a reward tier or add-on available again, you will need to edit the availability. To do this, select ‘Build campaign’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page, and head to the Rewards tab. Select a reward and scroll to the ‘Availability’ section. Once these settings have been updated, the reward should show as available again.

Remember: If there is a reward you wish to offer while your campaign is live, but also want to make it available as a Late Pledge, we recommend setting it to start “When my campaign launches”, and to end “Beyond the end of my campaign”.

​Need to edit a reward? Take a look at our “Can I edit my rewards after I’ve launched my project?” for more information on the changes you can and can’t make while your project is live.

Further reading:
​How do I add rewards to my project?
​What can be offered as a reward?

Can I share my project before launch?

Yes! Having friends and collaborators look at what you’ve put together can be really helpful and reassuring. To share a preview of your project, simply follow the steps below:

  • Begin by clicking “Preview” at the top of your project edit overview.
  • To allow others to view your project preview, and post feedback comments, enable sharing by clicking “Enable sharing” at the top of your preview page.
  • On the modal that appears, click the toggle button to enable sharing. Then, click the link icon to copy the shareable URL.

This URL isn’t password protected, so it’s very easy to share with friends and collaborators. If they have a Kickstarter account, they will also be able to leave feedback on your project preview.

If you previously shared a link to your project preview, and don’t want people to see it or leave feedback on it anymore, you can disable sharing. To do so, click “Manage sharing” at the top of your preview page and click the toggle in the modal to disable the URL.

When sharing is disabled, any preview URLs you have distributed will no longer lead to your preview page. However, once your project has launched, your preview link will redirect to your live campaign.

​

Additionally, you can also set up a Pre-launch page for your project. A Pre-launch page is a great way to share all vital information necessary to start building hype for your campaign, as well as presenting a direct call-to-action for backers to be notified when your project has launched.

Can I use AI-generated content in my project?

It depends. Kickstarter is first and foremost on the side of creative work and the humans behind that work. You can use generative AI in your project, but you will need to tell backers how, and it cannot be the main source of your creative work. Projects that build AI technology, and Design and Technology projects, have a few additional requirements. Details about this can be found below.

Disclosing generative AI in your project and in your rewards

While building your project, you will be asked two simple yes/no questions:

  • Does your campaign page contain any generative AI?
  • Will your final project contain any generative AI?

If you answer yes to either, you will be asked to explain how generative AI was used. Be specific about which parts of your campaign page or rewards use AI, and how. Your response is required whenever you answer yes.

Your disclosure will appear on your project page with one of the following statements, along with your explanation:

  • “I am using generative AI content on my campaign page”
  • “I plan to use generative AI content in my project”

You can see how your disclosure will appear in your project preview before you launch, so you can check it before backers do.

Excessive AI is against the rules

Disclosing your use of generative AI does not mean your project is allowed. Projects must be rooted in original, human-led creative work. Projects that rely excessively on generative AI, where AI output makes up most of the work or replaces the creator’s own creative contribution, are not allowed on Kickstarter. This applies to both your campaign page and your final project.

If our Trust & Safety team determines that a project relies too heavily on generative AI, the project may be declined or, if it has already launched, suspended. Being clear and specific in your disclosure helps us review your project quickly.

Some AI projects are out of scope

Kickstarter does not allow projects that create AI companions if they:

  • Offer romantic or sexual companionship, or,
  • Primarily advertise to children, or to parents or guardians of children.

Design and technology projects must show a real prototype

Projects in Design and Technology must include a video of a real prototype of the product being made. The prototype video must be available to all backers throughout the campaign. Generative AI video, renderings, or simulations can’t stand in for a real prototype.

Note that all projects remain subject to all other rules applicable to Kickstarter projects, including those involving claims of copyright infringement.

Can I use copyrighted material as part of my project?

Do not use music, images, video, or other content that you don’t have the rights to. Using copyrighted material is almost always against the law and can lead to expensive lawsuits down the road.

When copyright disputes do arise on Kickstarter (they’re rare, but they happen), we handle them in accordance with the Digital Millennium Copyright Act of 1998. To avoid unnecessary disruption, projects in question are typically removed from public view but are not immediately canceled. This keeps pledge authorizations and lines of communication intact, and leaves room for project recovery where appropriate. The procedure is outlined in detail on our Copyright and DMCA Policy page.

The easiest way to avoid copyright troubles altogether, though, is to create all the content yourself or use content that is free for public use. For example, you may be able to use some Creative Commons-licensed music — per the terms of their licenses — that’s available on SoundCloud. Other resources worth exploring: Vimeo Music Store, Free Music Archive, and ccMixter.

Can Kickstarter be used to fund anything?

Kickstarter is a place to bring new creative ideas to life, and to find support for your project.

Projects are launched on Kickstarter to create something new; whether it be recording an album, publishing a comic, filming a short, building a gadget, or creating a work of art that will be shared with others. Your project should have a clear end goal, with a properly thought out path to getting there.

As our mission is to help creators bring new creative ideas to life, we don’t permit offering something that’s already available somewhere else as a project’s end goal. For example, if you’ve already published, and are selling, a cookbook, this shouldn’t be the end goal for your project. However, if you are looking to create and publish a second cookbook, the first one could be offered alongside this as one of your reward tiers.

We review projects before they launch to ensure that they fit this criteria, and to make sure they aren’t breaking Our Rules.

If you’re unsure whether your idea would make a good fit for Kickstarter, feel free to get in touch.

Are there any prohibited items?

We prohibit projects that are illegal, heavily regulated, or potentially dangerous for backers, as well as rewards that the creator did not make. For more information check out our Prohibited Items page.

Can creators offer investment opportunities or a monetary reward?

Offering a return on investment, or a monetary reward of any kind, is prohibited. Creators are encouraged to offer their backers a unique reward that speaks to the spirit of what they’re hoping to create, rather than holding a financial stake in their project.

Need some reward ideas? Here are 96 of them.

Can Kickstarter recommend a pledge management service?

Yes, we can! Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager can help you to collect all the information you need from your backers in order to fulfill their rewards, without the need to transfer data to a third-party platform or pay any upfront costs.

Using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, you will gather shipping addresses, ask backers’ for their reward preferences, collect taxes, charge a more accurate shipping fee and tariff surcharge (by request only), and give backers the option to increase their pledge with available add-ons or upgrades.

Take a look at our FAQs to learn more about Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager and how this tool can help you with the next stage of your project.

Can my project page be in any language?

The languages that we currently support on Kickstarter are: English, French, German, Japanese, and Spanish. If your project includes text or audio in a language outside of these, we do recommend that you include English translations or subtitles where possible.

As a creator, it’s important to build your project with your target audience in mind. If you anticipate your backers will be mostly Spanish-speaking, then you may want the main language on your project page to be Spanish. However, including an English translation as well will help your project have a wider appeal. Kickstarter is a global community, with more than 75% of backers on Kickstarter coming from English-speaking countries.

Interested in including another language on your project page? Check out our Best Practices for Bilingual Projects.

Can projects raise more than their goal?

Yes they can! Projects remain live on Kickstarter until their set funding deadline, which means it’s possible for creators to surpass their funding goal before their project ends. In these cases, the creator will receive all of the collected funds (minus our fees), and with the additional funds they may be able to increase the scope of their project, or improve the quality and variety of their rewards.

What happens if a project does not reach its goal?

Kickstarter is an all or nothing platform, which means a project must end at or above its funding goal in order to be successful. If a project does not successfully reach its goal, the creator will not receive any funds, and no backers will be charged for their pledges.

How can I change the language preference on the site?

To set your language preference, head to the bottom of any page on Kickstarter and click the current language in the right corner. This will open a drop down menu where you can select your preferred language from.

Please note: The language that appears on our iPhone and Android apps is dictated by your phone’s settings.

How can I make sure my project is included in an Open Call?

Whether you’re a first-time creator thinking about launching your first project, or a seasoned creator looking for a new challenge, Kickstarter’s Open Calls are prompts designed to encourage all kinds of creators to get involved and bring their creative ideas to life.
