Project creation is currently available to eligible creators in the following countries: Australia, Austria, Belgium, Canada, Denmark, France, Germany, Greece, Hong Kong, Ireland, Italy, Japan, Luxembourg, Mexico, the Netherlands, New Zealand, Norway, Poland, Singapore, Slovenia, Spain, Sweden, Switzerland, the United Kingdom, and the United States.

Residents and registered entities outside one of the 25 countries listed above cannot launch projects. However, you can launch a project on Kickstarter by taking steps to do business where projects are allowed. Take a look at our “I am looking to create a business in the US so I can launch a project. How do I do this?” article to find out one way you can establish a business and launch a project in the US.

We’re working hard to open up Kickstarter to project creators in even more countries. Want to be notified if your country becomes available? Tell us where you’re located!

How are there projects with locations not in the above eligible countries?

If a project’s location is set to a city outside of our eligible countries, this is likely because the project creator meets the requirements for launching a project, but they are living or launching a project abroad. In other cases the project itself may be a collaboration between international team members.

Who can use Kickstarter?

Kickstarter is open to backers all over the world. Nearly anyone, from anywhere, can support a project as long as they have a major debit or credit card and a Kickstarter account.

In order to launch a project on Kickstarter, a creator will need to meet the requirements for one of the following countries:

Australia, Austria, Belgium, Canada, Denmark, France, Germany, Greece, Hong Kong, Ireland, Italy, Japan, Luxembourg, Mexico, the Netherlands, New Zealand, Norway, Poland, Singapore, Slovenia, Spain, Sweden, Switzerland, the United Kingdom, and the United States.

Once verified, you can choose to set your project’s location to a city in a country that best describes where your project is based. You can do this on the Basics tab of your project editor. However, the physical address on your verification documents must be in one of the above countries.

Want to be notified if Kickstarter adds support for project creation in your country? Tell us where you’re located!

You may still be able to launch a project now by establishing a business where we are open, like by doing so with Stripe Atlas in the US.

The following requirements must also be met by a creator to launch on Kickstarter:

Requirement

Additional qualifications

All individuals must be 18 years of age or older.

Individuals under 18 may participate in the creation of the project under the supervision of the project creator.

If raising funds as an individual, you must be a resident of the country where you launch your project.

You also need a government issued ID from that same country.

If your project country is in the European Union, government issued IDs from other European Union countries are acceptable.

If raising funds as an entity (business or nonprofit), you must launch your project in the country where the entity is registered. The individual setting up the project on the entity’s behalf must be an owner or executive of the entity, and have authorization to represent it.

All beneficial owners of the entity, and directors as requested, must also be disclosed and verified.

You need government issued IDs and tax documents for the primary entity raising funds for the project as well as for all individuals and other entities you verify.

You need a supported bank account located in the same country you set up your project. This account must be owned by and in the name of the individual or entity raising funds.

The bank account must be a current account that supports direct deposits in the currency in which the funds are being raised.

European projects raising funds in euros have the option to use an IBAN from another country*.

While an IBAN can be used from a country we are not open in, it is important to keep in mind that the project’s country, and all other verification documents, must be for one of our accepted countries.

You have a major credit or debit card registered to the individual or entity (or entity’s owner) raising funds for this project, and in their name.

Canadian projects can only use credit cards.

*Please note: If you are a creator launching a project from one of the European countries we are open in, and your funds will be in euros, you may use an IBAN from one of these listed countries: Austria, Belgium, Bulgaria, Cyprus, Czech Republic, Denmark, Estonia, Finland, France, Germany, Greece, Hungary, Ireland, Italy, Latvia, Lithuania, Luxembourg, Malta, Netherlands, Norway, Poland, Portugal, Romania, Slovakia, Slovenia, Spain, Sweden, Switzerland, United Kingdom

However, you will need to have a home address and acceptable documents for ID verification for the country you are launching from. For example, a creator can launch from France with a Portuguese IBAN, but they will need to verify that they are a resident of France.

Why are you asking for my demographic information and what will you do with it?

We are asking creators for their demographic information to help us evaluate and improve upon our diversity and inclusion efforts, and to provide creators with relevant and useful service offerings. Learning more about our creators helps us provide better products, as well as reach our goals of creating a diverse creator community and delivering an equitable platform.

What will you do with my demographic information?

We’ll only use your information in connection with our efforts for making the Kickstarter platform equitable and available to all. We review the data from the survey in an aggregate, de-identified way to track demographic trends and identify ways that we may better be able to serve the creator community. Additionally, where we have permission, we’ll use the information in an identifiable way to communicate additional services that may be available, such as connecting creators with funding sources.

We won’t share information that directly identifies you with third parties unless you grant us permission. For information about how we protect personal information, see the our Privacy Policy.

How do I provide my demographic information?

At the bottom of the People tab of your project editor, you’ll notice a prompt to answer a series of questions about yourself and your project. This section is entirely optional and isn’t a requirement for launching a project on Kickstarter. If you choose to complete this section, you’ll have the chance to tell us about yourself and your project, which will help us evaluate the effectiveness of our diversity and inclusion efforts within the creator community.

To complete this section, simply click the “Go to questions” button. There are approximately 38 questions that ask about your campaign preparedness and demographic information.

After submitting your answers, this section will no longer appear in the People tab. You can’t edit your answers after submitting them, but you can email [email protected] if you’d like us to delete the provided information.

Interacting with backers

How can I delete comments from backers on my project?

The comments section of a project is an open space for discourse between backers and creators. As such, we try to leave this area as “on the record” as possible so as to create transparency for everyone. In light of this, it is not possible for creators or backers to edit or delete a comment themselves. However, individual comments from a backer will be removed if they cancel their pledge while the project is still live, or are fully refunded after it ends. For comments that are part of a thread, these will be hidden from view.

It is important to remember that the comments section of your project will be viewable by anyone who lands on your project page. It helps to provide an insight into the success of your project, as well as the feelings of the people who helped bring your creative idea to life. This information may be useful to potential backers if you decide to run another campaign in the future or turn your Kickstarter project into a business.

If you feel that there are comments on your project page that violate our Community Guidelines, or are not relevant to the project, please feel free to report these comments by clicking the “Report” button on the comment and selecting a reason for the report. This will temporarily hide the comment while under review by our Trust & Safety team. If after reviewing the report our Trust & Safety team deem it necessary to take action, the comment will be removed by Kickstarter. For reported comments that do not require any action, the comment will be restored and it will not be possible to report this comment again.

How can I host a livestream on my project page?

While your project is in its funding period, you will be able to embed a live video stream into your project description. Additionally, you can embed a live video stream into your project updates at any time during your project’s lifecycle.

However, it is important to note that a project description can not be edited after the campaign ends, and it won’t be possible to edit an update after it is published.

To embed a live video stream, follow the simple steps below:

​

  • Set up or schedule a public livestream on one of our supported platforms:
    • Youtube
    • Twitch
    • Facebook Live
  • When you have a direct URL to your livestream, open a project update draft and click the video icon in the toolbar. Next, paste your video stream URL into the URL box and click the check mark to confirm.
    • If adding the livestream to your project description while your project is live, open your project editor and click the “+” button to open the contextual toolbar and select the “Insert media” icon.
  • When finished, click the “Preview” button on your project update to verify that your video stream loads and is correctly embedded.
  • Once published, users can view the livestream in your update or on your project page. Third-party embeds do not currently support interactive community aspects (chat, comments, etc.) but each video player includes straightforward click-through functionality where your viewers can then participate.

I previously used Kickstarter Live, what happened to my completed stream replay?

Kickstarter Live was retired on April 8, 2019. This means creators are no longer able to host or schedule livestreams with Kickstarter Live.

How can I use my project’s backer report?

Your project’s backer report is where you can keep track of all your backers and their pledges.You can find this in the creator navigation bar, to the left of your project page.

In the backer report you can message backers, issue refunds, add free items, update the fulfillment status for pledges, and make any edits to addresses*. If you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager you will also be able to send tracking numbers and digital rewards to backers.

At a glance you will be able to see the backer number, name, pledge amount, location, status, and pledge date for each backer. To make it easier for you to find specific backers there are a number of filters you can also use. These include:

  • Rewards, Add-ons, and Items and SKUs
  • These filters can be used to view backers based on the rewards they selected, or items within their pledge.
  • Status
  • Filter backers by the current status of their pledge collection, pledge manager or survey completion, and by the fulfillment status set by you.
  • Location
  • This list can be filtered by any of the countries selected by your backers.
  • Pledge amount
  • Enter a min and max amount to be displayed.
  • Backer entry point
  • Use this filter to see which backers pledged while your campaign was live, supported during Late Pledges, or joined through the Pledge Manager.
  • Payment option
  • This filter will only be available if you enabled Pledge Over Time for your campaign.
  • Pledge date
  • Enter a date range to see only pledges made during that time period.
  • Actions
  • Any actions that may have been applied can be found here. For example, if you have issued any refunds you will be able to filter by “Refund issued”.
  • Backer email address
  • Enter multiple email addresses, separated with a comma, to view a specific group of backers.
  • Backer type
  • See which of your backers were pre-launch or project followers before pledging.
  • Previous projects backed
  • This filter shows you who is a returning backer from one of your previous projects.

Additionally, you can choose to segment a particular audience and save it as a filtered view to return to in the future. Some examples of filtered views that may be useful are:

  • Backers who selected the payment option “Pledge Over Time”
  • Saving this view will allow you to keep an eye on the collection status for these pledges.
  • Backers who followed your project during pre-launch
  • Using this view you can see which backers followed your project ahead of launch.
  • Backers who have supported your previous projects
  • See who has supported you before on Kickstarter. If you want to send a thank you message, or add a free item, this filter will help you find those backers to do so.

​*Can I edit a backer’s address in the backer report?

Yes! If a backer has submitted their address to you, either through the pledge manager or backer survey, and you need to change it for them, you can do so through the backer report. To do so, simply click the three dots under the gear icon for that backer and select “Edit address”.

Please note: The backer must have submitted an address first. If they also need to change the country this will be at your discretion. We recommend making sure that no additional shipping fee is due in the event that the country is changed.

​I’m using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, can I use segments to lock addresses?

Yes! If you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager and only need to lock the addresses for the backers receiving their rewards next, you can do this by creating a segment in your backer report.

To then schedule an address lock for the segment, select “Schedule address lock” from the dropdown menu in the top right corner. Once the address lock is scheduled, backers in that segment will be notified that they have 48 hours to make any last minute changes to their address.

​After your project ends successfully you can download your backer report. Find more information about that here.

How do I communicate with backers?

To let your backers know how the project is going you can post an update. Updates are like your project’s blog. You’ll find a link to post an update in the creator tools menu to the left of your project page. You can choose to notify all of your backers about the new update, or you can limit the notifications to backers within certain reward tiers. To do so choose the “Select tiers” option under ‘Notifications’ and select which tiers you’d like to notify.

Looking for some tips on what kinds of updates you can send to your backers? Check out these 50 ideas for sending great project updates.

You can also talk to your backers through our messaging system. To send the same message to backers in a particular reward tier, use the messaging function for that tier in your backer report, which you can access through the creator tools menu.

You can also message backers that fall into the specific search filters within your backer report (including backers that have or have not responded to their survey, backers with dropped or errored pledges, backers whose reward status you’ve marked as “Not started”, “In progress”, “Shipped”, “Delayed”, etc.). To message a segment of backers, follow these steps:

  • Apply the filters you require to narrow down your backer segment.
  • Select all of the filtered backers by clicking the checkbox at the very top of that filtered list.
  • Click ‘Message’ at the bottom of the page once those backers are selected, and compose your message in the modal. The modal will show how many backers are in the recipient list, so make sure to confirm that number matches the number of backers in your filtered list.

To message backers individually, just use the ‘Send message’ link from the backer list or their backer card. If you need to send a message to a backer who has left a comment on your project page, you can open their backer card by clicking on their name in the comment, followed by the “View pledge” button.

Backers expect to connect with you through Kickstarter, so it’s best to use our tools for sending updates, messages, and asking for information. Avoid asking people to sign up for another service as it can be confusing for backers, and difficult to keep all your information in one place.

Can I pin a comment in my project’s comment section?

Pinning a comment is a great way to highlight any information shared in the comment section that you feel would be beneficial for all backers, without them needing to look for it. For example, if a backer has previously asked a technical question about a specific reward and this has been answered by the creator, or a collaborator, you can now choose to pin this to the top.

To pin a comment, click or tap on the three dots and select “Pin comment” from the dropdown menu. You’ll be able to pin up to five comments and they will appear in descending order of when they were pinned.

If you change your mind, or no longer need to pin a specific comment, you can click on the three dots again and select “Unpin comment”.

​How can I update backers on the status of their reward?

You can let backers know what the current status of their reward is by updating the status in the backer report. From the dropdown menu you will be able to choose to set the status as “Not started”, “In progress”, “Shipped”, or “Delayed”. This can be done for an individual backer, or a selection of backers. You can also filter by reward tier and select backers within that tier.

When a status is set this will be visible to backers on their Backed Projects page, and in their pledge details on the project page.*

*Please note that the reward status information isn’t currently available when viewing pledge details through the Kickstarter app.

How do I post a project update?

Project updates are the best way to keep your backers in the loop with how your project is going, and we recommend posting regular updates both throughout your campaign and up until fulfillment is complete.

You can also share updates exclusively with project followers while your campaign is live, to help capture their attention and keep them informed while they consider pledging.

To compose an update, simply follow the steps below:

  • On the left side of your project page, click the “+ Create Update” button
  • Choose an update style, you can either post a freeform update or a Q&A update
  • Enter a title for your update and compose your post. The body of your update allows you to explore your creativity: you can include text with rich formatting options, images, videos and audio. You can also attach a poll and invite your backers to vote on available options
  • Updates are set to publicly viewable by default. If you’d like to change this to backers only, select “Backers Only” in the ‘Visible to’ drop-down menu
    • To make an update exclusive to project followers, select “Followers only” from the drop-down menu. We’ll notify your followers via email
  • You can also select which backers get email notifications within the ‘Notifications’ drop-down menu. All backers will be emailed by default, or you can choose to “Select Tiers” and un-check the box next to the reward tiers you don’t wish to notify
  • Once you’ve composed your post and selected your settings, click the “Save Draft” button at the bottom of the page

Before publishing, we recommend previewing the update by clicking the “Preview” button. This will allow you to check for typos, formatting issues, and the clarity of your messaging.

Remember: you will only have 24 hours to edit an update after hitting publish. Once the 24 hour editing window has closed you’ll no longer be able to make any changes to the update.

All set? If you’re happy with how your update looks, hit the “Publish” button to put your update live.

Not ready to send out to your backers quite yet? No worries, you can always return to your draft later.

​How do I include a poll in my update?

When creating a new freeform update you can attach a poll directly to the update. Here you can add multiple options and invite your backers to submit their vote. You could use this feature to ask backers to choose their favorite design, or to vote on which reward you should offer next.

Backers will be able to see live results as votes are submitted, allowing more interaction and collaboration among your community.

You can choose how long the poll remains open for, and who is able to vote will depend on the visibility setting of the update. After a poll has closed, backers will no longer be able to vote but they will be able to view the results.

​Can I post an update on my Pre-launch page?

Yes! Simply follow steps 1 - 3 above, but note the following for pre-launch updates:

  • Pre-launch updates will always be visible to anyone who visits your project page (there is no option to set the visibility to “backers only”).
  • When an update is published, a mail notification will automatically be sent to all project followers.

​Can I edit or delete an update after publishing?

For transparency, after the 24 hour editing window has closed it’s no longer possible to make changes to an update. This includes deleting an update. If any information was missed, or details have changed, we recommend posting a new update to let your backers know.

If you’d like to change an update’s visibility from “Public (everyone)” to “Backers only”, or vice-versa, please contact our Support team.

​What are project updates?

​What should I post an update about and how often?

​How do I include images or other media in my project description and updates?

What are project updates?

“Project updates” refer to what is essentially each project’s blog posts. They are an important tool for creators to keep their backers informed; from when the campaign is live - to let backers know a milestone has been reached or to share how preparations are going - to while fulfillment is being completed - for example, a behind the scenes look at production.

Project updates are also a great way to share any bumps in the road, or hurdles a creator has had to overcome - maintaining honesty with backers goes a long way! It is our expectation that creators will post at least one new update a month once funding is finished, to keep their backers in the loop - this could simply be a small note to check in with your backers, or a single photograph of what you are working on - just something to let your backers know you’re still working on the project.

Creators can choose to make their updates public (this means they will be visible to all viewers of the project page) just for backers, or for project followers only. Additionally, creators can opt to notify all of their backers about an update, or just backers of select reward tiers.

​Why are project updates important?

When a creator goes silent, backers assume the worst. Regularly updating your backers with information about your project helps to both keep backer’s minds at ease, but is also a great way to show your appreciation for the support you received from them.

Project updates are also a great way to share more information with the public, especially if you decide to run another campaign in the future, or turn your Kickstarter project in to a business. Project updates will help people to learn more about your working practice, as well as giving them an insight into how communicative you were with the people who helped bring your creative idea to life.

​What are follower updates?

Follower updates are a way to share information exclusively with your project followers, while your campaign is live. You can use these updates to help capture the attention of your followers, and keep them in the loop while they consider pledging to your project.

To post a follower update, select “Followers only” from the drop-down menu when drafting a new update. We will then notify your followers via email.

​Further reading:

​How do I post a project update?

​What should I post an update about and how often?

​How do I include images or other media in my project description and updates?

What is a Design & Technology Status?

For projects that successfully fund in the Design & Technology categories the creator will have an option to set a Status to keep their backers up to date as they move through the different stages of reward development, production, and fulfillment.

A Design & Technology Status is a shorter version of a project update. The latter lets you offer lengthy updates, where you can embed audio, video, and images, but a Design & Technology Status is a quick capture that’s easy for visitors of your project page to digest. Simply write a short statement to let backers know what you’re working on, and what milestone is next—you can also select a date to let them know when you plan to start.

These brief updates can cover a new phase within the production journey, whether it be a major step or simply a smaller task, it can also be used to let backers know what point you are at with fulfilling rewards. We recommend that you use the associated date feature to give your backers a better sense of when they should check in next, or when they can expect to hear from you again.

Note: This feature is currently only available for projects in the Design & Technology categories.

Do Design & Technology Statuses replace project updates?

No, we still encourage you to maintain a monthly cadence of project updates, and deliver more detailed, media-rich, behind-the-scenes snapshots of your project. The Design & Technology Status feature provides a more light-weight look at a project’s progress that can be updated more frequently without the complexity of longer updates.

Are backers notified of Status updates?

A Status will only appear on the project page—they’re not sent to backers via email and they don’t appear in a backer’s activity feed.

How do I set a Status on my project?

First, ensure that you have opted in by making sure that the top left corner of the Status update area says “Sharing on project page”. You should see the expanded module on your project page along with a pencil icon.

  • Click the pencil icon and fill out both the ‘Now’ and ‘Next’ text boxes with 100 characters or less.
  • Pick a date for when you plan on starting your ‘Next’ work.
  • Click the “Publish” button in the upper right hand corner of the editing area. Your new Status will refresh and appear on your project page.

How do I opt out of this feature?

You can hide the Design & Technology Status tool from your project at any time. When you click on “Sharing on project page,” it will change to “Hiding on project page”. This removes the Status bar from your project page. You can restore this by simply clicking “Hiding on project page” so that it says “Sharing on project page” again. Enter and publish a new Status if you have any new information to share.

What is a Q&A project update?

A Q&A project update is a feature that allows you to engage with your backers and the Kickstarter community through question and answer style project updates. You can choose which questions to answer to help you share your creative process and personal journey as your project comes to life.

Through these questions, we encourage you to share your honest and thoughtful insight with your community. Keep in mind that while Q&A updates are a great way to connect with your backers regularly, this format is not a replacement for freeform project updates. We still advise you to release frequent updates to keep your backers informed of the specific progress made on your project.

How does it work?

When creating a new project update, you’ll be able to choose between a freeform or Q&A style update. If you choose Q&A, you’ll see a list of prompts in the form of questions. Feel free to dismiss or skip any questions you’re not comfortable answering.

You can preview your update before you publish it and we’ll notify your backers via email when it’s published. Q&A updates will be visible to everyone—including visitors not logged in to Kickstarter.

Please note: You can only post one Q&A update each month, and at this time it is not possible to return and answer skipped questions once an update is published. However, we will regularly refresh these questions and you can answer as many as you’d like.

How do I compose a Q&A update?

  • Open the creator menu on your project page and click the “Post update” button
  • Choose the Q&A update style and enter a title for your update
  • Add your answers below the question prompts you’d like to answer
  • Click “X” or leave an answer field blank for any questions you’d prefer to skip
  • If you want to return to a skipped question you dismissed before publishing, simply click on the question or the “+” icon and an answer field will open
  • Click “Preview” to see your update as it will appear on your project page
  • After you have thoroughly checked everything, click “Publish” to share your answers as a project update and notify your backers via email

Why is Q&A content important?

​

Offering transparency around your project’s progress and creative process can be inspiring to others, and provides a unique way for your backers to stay connected to the journey they joined when they backed your project.

As a creator, participating in Q&A can help you create a record of your work, and help you reflect on your process as it develops over time. We might also include your answers in our educational resources, or consider placing them in our editorial features.

What is my responsibility for answering questions from backers and non-backers?

The FAQ section of each project page is the best place to address common questions. You can add Frequently Asked Questions to your project page both before you launch and while your project is live.

To add an FAQ before your project launches, navigate to the bottom of the Story tab of the project editor. Here you will see the option to “Add another FAQ”.

After your project has launched, head to the FAQ tab of your project page and click the “Edit FAQs” button. From there, you will be able to add a new question and corresponding answer. You can also rearrange or delete FAQs while your project is live.

As a creator this section is a great place to post helpful, to-the-point information about your project, and for backers to learn more about the project and rewards. For instance, you can use this section to share more information about your timeline, specifics about rewards, shipping plan, and/or a deeper dive into any technical specifications.

It’s important to note that FAQs can only be added and edited while your project is live or in draft. After your project has ended we recommend using project updates to address any common questions your backers are asking.

If you are receiving questions related to using the Kickstarter platform, you can always encourage your backers to contact us directly.

What is the Community tab?

The Community tab, located next to the Comments tab on a project page, focuses on the community that has rallied behind your project. Here you’ll be able to see:

  • How many people are supporting your project
  • The number of first time backers who have pledged
  • How many of the backers are repeat backers
  • The top 10 cities and countries of your backer community

What should I post an update about and how often?

Anything and everything that relates to your project! People who have backed your project have done so because they want to support you and your creative idea - keeping them in the loop will go a long way to show them that you appreciate their support.

​

Some creators may post an update every day, some may post more sporadically, however it is our expectation that creators will post at least one new update a month once funding is finished. Posting regular updates is the best way to keep backers informed of a project’s progress (and from our experience, a better informed backer is a happier backer).

​

For ideas on what to post an update about, check out these 50 ideas for sending great project updates.

Kickstarter 101

How can I change the email address associated with my account?

To update the email address associated with your Kickstarter account you’ll first need to login to your account using the email address currently associated with it. Once you’ve logged in:

  • Head over to your Account settings and click “Change email address”.
  • In the modal that pops up, enter the new email address you wish to use and click “Send verification code”.
  • Next, check your email inbox for the 6-digit verification code from Kickstarter and enter this on the verification screen.
  • Click “Continue” to finish updating the email address on your account.

For security reasons, we require all Kickstarter users to verify their email addresses. So keep an eye out for a verification email from us in order to finalize your email address change.

Please note: It isn’t possible to use an email address on more than one Kickstarter account. If you are seeing an error message that the email you are trying to enter is already in use, we recommend entering an alternative email address.

Check out this article for how to update your email address through the Kickstarter app.

Unable to access the email address you used to sign up for your Kickstarter account? Get in touch.

​

My email address has been changed and I’m having trouble accessing a third-party service related to my pledge

After changing your email address we recommend letting any project creators know. They will be able to see the new email address associated with your pledge in their backer report on Kickstarter.

If a creator is using a third-party service to collect any information they need in order to fulfill your reward, the creator will need to provide that service with your new email address so that they can update the information on their end.

​

  • I created a Kickstarter account with my Apple ID, how can I update my email address?

How can I download my Kickstarter data?

You can download a copy of your Kickstarter data by following the below steps:

  • Visit our Privacy Policy
  • Click the “Make a Privacy Request” button in the top right
  • Click “Download all my data”
  • Enter the email address associated with your Kickstarter account, and click “Send Email”
  • We’ll then send you an email with a link to log into the privacy center and confirm your request
  • When we have finished compiling your data, we’ll send you an email with a link to log in and download your report

The download will be a .ZIP file containing your data on Kickstarter. Keep in mind, it may take some time for us to prepare your data for download.

The information inside the .ZIP file will be in a .CSV format.

How can I manage my notifications?

You can manage which email and mobile notifications you’d like to receive by signing in to your Kickstarter account from a web browser, then going to Settings and the Notifications tab.

On this page you can control which of the below notifications you receive, and how you receive them, by toggling the corresponding icons to on or off for each one.

  • New replies in your active comment threads
  • New messages
  • Project updates
  • New projects from creators you’ve backed
  • When a creator you follow has backed or launched a project
  • When someone has followed you
  • Creator tips
  • Announcements
  • Invitations to provide feedback

If you’d like to stop receiving updates in your inbox from a specific project, simply uncheck the box next to that project under ‘Projects you’ve backed’. Please note that any new updates for that project will continue to show on your Activity feed.

At the bottom of the Notifications page you can also choose which Kickstarter newsletters you would like to subscribe to, or unsubscribe from.

​Check out this article for how to manage your notifications through the Kickstarter app.

How do I log in or sign up?

Kickstarter no longer requires a password to log in or create an account. Instead, we use a one-time verification code sent directly to your email. Enter your email address, check your inbox for a 6-digit code, and you’re in! No password needed.

This applies to all Kickstarter accounts.

  • OTP (One-Time Password)
  • OTP refers to a randomly generated code (typically 6 digits) that is sent to an external channel, such as your email, to verify your identity. Because this code is transmitted to you, it requires communication between devices. It is generally valid for a limited period and can only be used once.
  • TOTP (Time-based One-Time Password)
  • TOTP is a security method where a 6-digit code is generated locally on your own device using an authenticator app (such as Google Authenticator, Authy, or integrated password managers like 1Password). It relies on a “seed” (shared secret) generated at the time of setup.

Logging in to an existing account

  • Go to the Log in page and enter your email address.
  • Check your email inbox for a 6-digit verification code from Kickstarter.
  • Enter the code on the verification screen to continue.
  • If your account has two-factor authentication enabled (2FA), open your authenticator app and enter the current 6-digit code to complete sign in.

If you select “Remember this device for 30 days,” you won’t need to enter a verification code again on the same browser or device during that period, unless you sign out or clear your browser data.

Signing up

  • Go to the Sign up page and enter the email address you’d like to use.
  • Enter your first and last name.
  • Decide whether to opt in to our marketing emails and/or research surveys.
  • Check your inbox for a 6-digit verification code from Kickstarter. The code is valid for 10 minutes and can only be used once.
  • Enter the code on the verification screen to complete signing up.

Logging in or signing up with Apple

You can also log in or create an account using your Apple account. Click the “Continue with Apple” button on the Log in or Sign up page and complete the authentication in the window that opens up.

If you are a returning user with two-factor authentication active, you will be required to provide your authenticator code. If you are a new user, your Kickstarter account will be created automatically using your Apple credentials.

If the Apple pop-up fails or you close it, you’ll be returned to the Log in or Sign up page to continue with your email instead.

Frequently Asked Questions

I did not receive a verification code
  • First, check your spam or junk folder. The email comes from Kickstarter and has a subject line about your verification code.
  • Make sure the email address on your account is one you can currently access. You can verify this by checking your Account settings when signed in.
  • Click “Resend code” to request a new one. Each new code invalidates the previous one.
I received an incorrect verification code

Make sure you’re entering the most recent code. A previous code is invalidated the moment a new one is sent. After several incorrect attempts, you will need to request a new code.

My verification code has expired

Codes will expire after a period of time. To request a new one, click “Resend code”.

If you continue to have trouble receiving codes, please contact our Support team.

What happened to my password?

Kickstarter has moved to passwordless authentication. Passwords are no longer used to log in or sign up. If you previously had a password on your account, you won’t be prompted for it going forward, instead you will be asked to provide your OTP code, and TOTP code, if you have 2FA enabled.

Why is Kickstarter doing this?

This is a security improvement. Passwordless authentication is a widely adopted best practice, removing the password entirely means there’s nothing to steal, reuse, or expose in a third-party data breach. Your account is protected by access to your email inbox, and if enabled, a TOTP app/device.

I already have two-factor authentication (2FA) set up, does this affect me?

If you have an authenticator app (like Google Authenticator or Authy) set up for 2FA, you will still be prompted for that code as part of your log in. The full log in steps can be found under “Logging in to an existing account” above.

I can’t access the email address on my account

If you no longer have access to the email address associated with your account, please contact our Support team for help with account recovery. Account recovery is handled case by case and is not available through the standard log in flow.

How do I manage my Messages?

The Messages section of your Kickstarter profile contains any messages that you have sent or received via Kickstarter’s direct messages. You’ll be able to read and reply to messages, filter them by status, and search for specific keywords. At this time it is only possible to send direct messages between backers and creators, and not from one backer to another.

If you receive any messages that violate our Community Guidelines, please feel free to mark them as spam and our Trust and Safety team will look into this as soon as possible.

Please note that turning off new message notifications will not disable direct messages or prevent folks from reaching out.

I created a Kickstarter account with my Apple ID, how can I update my email address?

If your Kickstarter account was initially created with your Apple ID, you can update your email address via your Account settings. Please note that doing so will disconnect your account from your Apple ID and you will no longer be able to use the Sign in with Apple feature.

After clicking the link to disconnect your Apple ID, you will be prompted to enter your new email address in the modal that pops up. We will then send an email to the new email address with next steps to complete the disconnect. Once complete, you will be able to login to your Kickstarter account by entering your new email address on the Log in page.

If you encounter any issues with disconnecting your Kickstarter account from your Apple ID, please contact our Support team.

I signed up with Apple and I’m having trouble accessing a third-party survey

If you opted to hide your email address when signing up with Apple, project creators will only be able to see your private relay email in their backer report at the end of the campaign.

Please note that the unique relay address tied to your Kickstarter account can only be used for communication with Kickstarter. This means that any communication sent outside of our platform by the creator will unfortunately not reach your inbox.

If they are using a third-party survey service, such as BackerKit, the creator will need to provide that service with a valid email address so that they can update the information on their end and re-send the survey to you.

You can also follow the steps above to update the email address associated with your account. After doing so you can let the project creator know that your new email address is now available in their backer report on Kickstarter.

In what languages does Kickstarter offer Support?

Currently, our Support team is able to assist users in English, French, German, Japanese, and Spanish.

We’re working to scale up the number of languages we support and aim to have a wider reach to help users in different territories. While we don’t have a concrete timeline for implementing this just yet, rest assured that we are working on it.

If you don’t see your language listed above, please try writing in using Google Translate and we’ll do our best to respond.

What are the basics?

A project is a finite work with a clear goal that you’d like to bring to life. Think albums, books, or films.

The funding goal is the amount of money that a creator needs to complete their project.

Funding on Kickstarter is all-or-nothing. No one will be charged for a pledge towards a project unless it reaches its funding goal. This way, creators always have the budget they scoped out before moving forward.

A creator is the person or team behind the project idea, working to bring it to life.

Backers are folks who pledge money to join creators in bringing projects to life. Kickstarter is not a store, backers support a creative process.

Rewards are a creator’s chance to share a piece of their project with their backer community. Typically, these are one-of-a-kind experiences, limited editions, or copies of the creative work being produced.

If you want to know more about how it works, try browsing around and looking at some of the projects on the site — you can start here. If you’re curious about starting a project, our Creator Handbook can give you a great overview. Check out Our Rules for more information.

What are the fees?

If a project is successfully funded, Kickstarter collects a 5% fee from the funds collected for creators. Stripe, our payments processor, will also collect a payment processing fee (roughly 3-5%). The complete fee breakdowns are available here.

If a project does not reach its funding goal, no fees are collected.

We established this fee structure in 2009 and have set an industry standard for creator-friendly funding.

We use this model because we believe in paying artists as much as possible. We’re a tool for going around traditional gatekeepers like agents, dealers, distributors, fiscal sponsors, record labels, and publishers, and we accordingly take a much lower cut than they do.

In 2015, we furthered our commitment to supporting the health of the creative community by becoming a Public Benefit Corporation. This means we measure our success based on how well we achieve our mission of bringing creative projects to life, not just shareholder profits.

​

What are the fees for using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager?

The fees we will collect on payments made through the Pledge Manager are the same as the fees we collect on successful projects.

Our payments processor, Stripe, will collect a variable card processing fee (roughly 3-5%) on the full payment, including any taxes. Kickstarter will collect a 5% platform fee on all funds, except taxes.

What do backers get in return?

Backers that support a project on Kickstarter get an inside look at the creative process, and help that project come to life. They also get to choose from a variety of unique rewards offered by the project creator. Rewards vary from project to project, but often include a copy of what is being produced (CD, DVD, book, etc.) or an experience unique to the project. Backers can also opt to pledge for a project without selecting a reward.

Project creators keep 100% ownership of their work, and Kickstarter cannot be used to offer equity, financial returns, or to solicit loans.

What is Kickstarter?

Kickstarter is a crowdfunding platform where creators raise funds directly from backers to bring their creative project to life. We use an all-or-nothing model, which means pledges are only collected if a campaign reaches its funding goal by the deadline. If the goal is not reached, no money changes hands.

Kickstarter is a Public Benefit Corporation (PBC), with a stated mission to help bring creative projects to life. We support projects across art, comics, crafts, dance, design, fashion, film and video, food, games, journalism, music, photography, publishing, technology, and theater.

How Kickstarter works

  • A creator will launch a campaign for their project, with a set funding goal and deadline. The maximum campaign duration is 60 days.
  • Backers will browse campaigns and pledge funds to projects they want to see come to life. They can choose to pledge for available rewards tied to the project.
  • At a campaign’s deadline, the outcome is all-or-nothing. If the goal is met, backers are charged and the creator receives the funds. If not, no money changes hands.

See how Kickstarter works

For creators

Kickstarter is a way to raise funds for a new creative project without giving up equity or taking on debt. Every project must fit one of Kickstarter’s categories, have a defined goal, and produce something new to share. Each campaign must offer new and unique rewards related to the project, that are designed or created by the team working on the project.

All projects must meet Kickstarter’s rules

For backers

Backers pledge funds to projects they want to see come to life. In return, creators offer rewards tied to the project. This could be a finished copy of the work, early access, or exclusive experiences. Kickstarter is not a store. It is important to keep in mind that a pledge is helping to fund a creative process, not purchasing a guaranteed product.

Fees

If a project is successfully funded, Kickstarter collects a 5% fee from the funds collected. Stripe, our payments processor, will also collect a payment processing fee (roughly 3-5%).

See full fee details

Frequently Asked Questions

What types of projects can launch on Kickstarter?

Kickstarter is for creative projects only. Supported categories include art, comics, crafts, dance, design, fashion, film and video, food, games, journalism, music, photography, publishing, technology, and theater. Every project must produce something original to share with backers.

What is all-or-nothing funding?

All-or-nothing means a campaign only collects pledges if it reaches its goal. If the goal is met, backers are charged and the creator receives the funds. If not, no money changes hands.

Learn more about all-or-nothing funding

When are backers charged?

Backers are only charged if the campaign reaches its funding goal by the deadline. If the campaign falls short, no payment is processed.

What happens if a project does not reach its goal?

If a campaign ends without reaching its funding goal, no money changes hands. Backers are not charged, and the creator receives no funds. When a campaign ends short of its goal, creators are welcome to relaunch their project at any time.

What is Sign in with Apple and how can I use it to create an account?

Sign in with Apple is an iOS feature that allows you to securely create an account and sign in with your Apple ID.

To create a Kickstarter account with your Apple ID on a web browser simply click “Sign in with Apple” on the login page. You will then be prompted for a name and to choose which email address you wish to share with us; you can choose to either use the email address tied to your Apple ID or to hide it and use a relay email system instead.

This feature is also available when using Kickstarter through the app.

​

I already have a Kickstarter account, can I use the new Sign in with Apple feature?

At the moment, it isn’t possible to sign in to an existing Kickstarter account using the new Sign in with Apple feature. Additionally, if you already had a Kickstarter account and you have created a new account with your Apple ID, please note that we do not currently support merging accounts.

​

I signed up with Apple and I’m having trouble accessing the survey

If you opted to hide your email address when signing up with Apple, project creators will only be able to see your private relay email in their backer report at the end of the campaign.

Please note that the unique relay address tied to your Kickstarter account can only be used for communication with Kickstarter. This means that any communication sent outside of our platform by the creator will unfortunately not reach your inbox.

In this case, we’d recommend reaching out to the project creator to clarify the situation and to request that they update your email on their end.

If the creator is using Kickstarter’s own survey system, they should be able to update the email address on file by editing the exported backer report. They can find your pledge by looking up your backer number.

If they are using a third-party survey service, such as BackerKit, the creator will need to provide them with your new email address so that they can update the information on their end.

​

How can I disconnect my Kickstarter account from my Apple ID?

If you signed up to Kickstarter using the “Continue with Apple” option and would now like to disconnect the accounts, please contact our Support team who will be able to advise on next steps.

Please note that after your account has been disconnected from your Apple ID you will no longer be able to use the Sign in with Apple feature.

​

Can I start a project using Apple ID?

Unfortunately, at this time it isn’t possible to start a project from a Kickstarter account that is linked to an Apple ID. If you wish to create a project on an account that was created using the “Continue with Apple” option you will first need to disconnect the accounts.

Where do backers come from?

When a project launches on Kickstarter a majority of the initial funding typically comes from the creator’s existing networks. This will be your friends, family, fans, co-workers, and people you’re connected to through your extended network. If people in your network like your project, they’ll spread the word to their friends, and so on.

Press, blogs, online communities (forums, message boards, Facebook groups), and Kickstarter itself are also big sources of traffic and pledges. The Creative Independent put together a helpful guide to getting press for your creative work.

Social media is another important tool for spreading the word about your project, make sure to check out our tools to help you promote your project on social.

Why am I being asked to verify my email address?

To help keep your account secure, we request that you verify the email address that is linked to your Kickstarter account. By verifying, this also ensures that we are able to send you important account related emails.

If you haven’t verified your email address yet you will receive an email from us asking you to do so. You may also see a reminder message when you sign into your Kickstarter account.

If you encounter any issues with verifying your email address, we recommend making sure that you are fully logged out from any Kickstarter accounts before clicking the link in the email. A new verification email can be generated through your Account settings.

As a reminder, you can manage your email notifications settings at any time.

Why do people back projects?

People back projects for a number of reasons. They may be rallying around a friend’s project, or supporting a new effort from someone they’ve long admired. They might be inspired by a new idea, or simply motivated by a project’s reward.

Whatever the reason, people back projects because they want to have a part in helping to bring it to life. Backing a project is more than just pledging funds to a creator, it’s pledging support for a creative idea.

Why is funding all-or-nothing?

Kickstarter uses an all-or-nothing funding model for three reasons: to protect creators, to protect backers, and to create the urgency that helps campaigns succeed.

When a project only receives funds if it reaches its goal, creators can be confident that they have enough money to actually deliver what they promised. Backers can be confident their pledge only collects if the campaign is viable. And the goal itself creates momentum, by giving early backers a reason to recruit others and play a role in bringing something to life together.

This is also what separates Kickstarter from a store. Backers are not making a guaranteed purchase. They are funding a creative process, with the built-in assurance that if the campaign falls short, no one is charged.

How creators control all-or-nothing funding

Creators set their own funding goal and campaign deadline. Campaigns can run anywhere from one day to 60, though 30 days tends to work best. Once a campaign launches, both the goal and the deadline are locked in.

If a creator decides the timing isn’t right, they can cancel and relaunch later. They will need to bring their original backers back on board, which they can do by posting an update on their canceled project. There is no platform consequence for starting over.

See how Kickstarter works

What are the fees?

If a project is successfully funded, Kickstarter collects a 5% fee from the funds collected. Stripe, our payments processor, will also collect a payment processing fee (roughly 3-5%).

See full fee details

Frequently Asked Questions

What happens if a project does not reach its goal?

If a campaign ends without reaching its funding goal, no money changes hands. Backers are not charged, and the creator receives no funds. When a campaign ends short of its goal, creators are welcome to relaunch their project at any time.

Can a campaign raise more than its goal?

Yes! There is no limit on how much a campaign can raise, and any funds collected beyond the goal will be considered overfunding. Many creators use overfunding milestones as stretch goals, unlocking additional features or deliverables for backers.

Can backers cancel a pledge?

Backers can cancel a pledge at any time before the campaign ends. After a campaign ends successfully, pledges are collected and can no longer be canceled.

Why is there a deadline?

A deadline creates urgency. A fixed end date gives potential backers a reason to act, and gives early backers an incentive to recruit others. Without a deadline, the collective momentum that all-or-nothing funding is designed to generate would not exist.

The Kickstarter App

Can I start a project from the app?

Sorry, but it’s currently not possible to start or edit a project from our mobile app. Please get started from your computer whenever you have the chance.

Once your project is live, creators and project collaborators using the iOS app will be able to post updates, access the project dashboard, and message backers.

Dark Mode on Android and iOS

As of June 2025, both the Android and iOS Kickstarter apps will automatically match your device system theme settings - light or dark. This ensures a seamless overall mobile experience.

This functionality is available on:

  • Android OS 10+ and will apply to all app versions 3.18+
  • iOS 16.0+ and will apply to all app versions 5.26.1+

If you have any questions about this feature, please feel free to reach out to our Support team.

How can I change my pledge amount or reward selection through the app?

While a project is live you are welcome to modify your pledge amount or reward selection through the Kickstarter app.

To update your pledge on iOS:

  • Tap the profile icon in the bottom right of the screen and select the project in question.
  • Tap the blue “Manage” button and on the next page tap the three dots in the top right corner.
  • If updating your pledge amount, select “Update pledge” and change the amount you wish to pledge by adjusting the bonus support. Tap the “+” and “-” buttons to your desired amount and select “Done”. If needs be, you can also make any changes to the shipping location before tapping “Confirm” to finish.

To edit your reward on iOS:

  • Tap the profile icon in the bottom right of the screen and select the project in question.
  • Tap the blue “Manage” button and on the next page tap the three dots in the top right corner.
  • If editing your reward, select “Edit reward” and scroll across to see all of the reward tiers available. Tap “Select” on the tier you wish to change to. If you wish to keep your current reward tier, tap “Continue”.
  • If the creator has created add-on rewards for their project, you’ll have the option to add or remove these on the next page. Tap the “Continue with X add-ons” button. If you don’t wish to add anything here, simply tap the “Skip add-ons” button.
  • (Please note: if the main reward tier selected is digital, physical add-ons will not be available.)
  • Next you can choose to add bonus support, tap the “+” and “-” buttons to your desired amount and select “Done”. If needs be, you can also make any changes to the shipping location. Tap “Confirm” to finish.

To update your pledge on Android:

  • Tap the menu icon in the top left of the screen and select your profile name. Then, select the project in question.
  • Tap the blue “Manage” button and on the next page tap the three dots in the top right corner.
  • If updating your pledge amount, select “Update pledge” and change the amount you wish to pledge by adjusting the bonus support. Tap the “+” and “-” buttons to your desired amount and select “Done”. If needs be, you can also make any changes to the shipping location.
  • Tap “Confirm” to finish.

To edit your reward on Android:

  • Tap the menu icon in the top left of the screen and select your profile name. Then select the project in question.
  • Tap the blue “Manage” button and on the next page tap the three dots in the top right corner.
  • If updating your pledge amount, select “Choose another reward” and scroll across to see all of the reward tiers available. Tap “Select” on the tier you wish to change to. If you wish to keep your current reward tier, tap “Continue”.
  • If the creator has created add-on rewards for their project, you’ll have the option to add or remove these on the next page, as well as make any changes to the shipping location. When ready tap the “Continue with X add-ons” button.
  • (Please note: if the main reward tier selected is digital, physical add-ons will not be available.)
  • You can also choose to add bonus support, tap the “+” and “-” buttons to your desired amount and select “Done”.
  • If you don’t wish to include any add-ons with your pledge, simply tap the “Skip add-ons” button and on the next page you can make any changes to the shipping location, if applicable.
  • Tap “Confirm” to finish.

Remember, when changing your pledge amount you are not adding to your existing pledge, the new amount you enter will be the total amount collected if the project is successfully funded.

Once a project has ended it is no longer possible to modify your pledge amount or reward selection. If you need to seek out an alternative arrangement, we recommend reaching out to the project creator.

If you experience any issues while trying to complete your pledge, please consult this article of common pledge problems.

How can I change the displayed currency in the app?

You can change the currency displayed in the app by simply following the steps relevant to your operating system below.

iOS:

  • Select the “Profile” icon in the bottom right-hand corner of the app.
  • Tap the gear icon in the top right-hand corner of the screen to access your settings.
  • From here you’ll be able to select your preferred currency.

Android:

  • Tap the menu icon in the top left-hand corner of the app.
  • Select “Settings” and then “Account”.
  • From here you’ll be able to select your preferred currency.

Project goals, reward tiers, and pledge amounts will be shown with an approximate conversion for your preferred currency.

Please keep in mind that if a project you backed is successful, your pledge will be collected in the native currency of the project. You can read more about this here.

How can I manage my notifications on the app?

To manage notifications on iOS:

  • Select the “Profile” icon in the bottom right-hand corner of the app.
  • Tap the gear icon in the top right-hand corner of the screen to access your settings.
  • From here you’ll be able to go to your “Notifications” section to manage your mobile and email notification preferences. If the mail or mobile icon for a certain category is green, that means that you have notifications turned on.

To manage notifications on Android:

  • Tap the menu icon in the top left-hand corner of the app.
  • Select “Settings” and then “Notifications”.
  • From here you’ll be able to manage your mobile and email notification preferences. If the email or mobile icon for a certain category is green, that means that you have notifications turned on.

Check out this article for how to manage your notifications from a web browser.

How can I manage notifications for my project on the app?

To manage notifications for your project on iOS:

  • Select the “Profile” icon in the bottom right-hand corner of the app.
  • Tap the gear icon in the top right-hand corner of the screen to access your settings.
  • From here you’ll be able to go to your “Notifications” section to manage your notification preferences.
  • Under ‘Projects you’ve launched’, you’ll find options to set your digest frequency to either daily or twice a day.
  • To receive the digest as a push notification, make sure the mobile icon for “Project activity” is turned on (on setting shows as green, off setting shows as grey).

To manage notifications for your project on Android:

  • Tap the menu icon in the top left-hand corner of the app.
  • Select “Settings” and then “Notifications”.
  • From here you’ll be able to manage your notification preferences.
  • Under ‘Projects you’ve launched’, you’ll find options to set your digest frequency to either daily or twice a day.
  • To receive the digest as a push notification, make sure the mobile icon for “Project activity” is turned on (on setting shows as green, off setting shows as grey).

Note: if you would prefer to only receive the digest as an email, make sure only the mail icon is turned on for this setting. You can read more about the email notifications here.

The Project Activity Summary includes:

​

  • New Pledges
  • Pledge Adjustments
  • Pledge Cancellations
  • Comments
  • Likes

If you would prefer to receive a push notification for every comment, and like, you can adjust this by selecting the mobile icon next to each option in the notification settings.

How can I update my email address in the app?

You can update the email address associated with your Kickstarter account via the app, to do so simply follow the steps relevant to your operating system below.

iOS:

  • Select the “Profile” icon in the bottom right-hand corner of the app.
  • Tap the gear icon in the top right-hand corner of the screen to access your settings.
  • From here you’ll be able to go to your “Account”, where you can change your email address.

Android:

  • Tap the menu icon in the top left-hand corner of the app.
  • Select “Settings” and then “Account”.
  • From here you’ll be able to change your email address.

Check out this article for how to update your email address from a web browser.

I created a Kickstarter account with my Apple ID, how can I update my email address?

If your Kickstarter account was initially created with your Apple ID, you can update your email address via your Account settings. Please note that doing so will disconnect your account from your Apple ID and you will no longer be able to use the Sign in with Apple feature.

After clicking the link to disconnect your Apple ID, you will be prompted to enter your new email address in the modal that pops up. We will then send an email to the new email address with next steps to complete the disconnect. Once complete, you will be able to login to your Kickstarter account by entering your new email address on the Log in page.

If you encounter any issues with disconnecting your Kickstarter account from your Apple ID, please contact our Support team.

How can I update my profile in the app?

It’s not currently possible to edit your profile from the Kickstarter app.

If you wish to make any changes to your profile, including your profile name, profile picture, biography, location, and website, you can do so from any compatible web browser. Simply sign in to your Kickstarter account, then go to Settings and the Edit profile tab.

How do I block a user on the iOS Kickstarter app?

“Blocking” a user refers to the act of hiding their content and communications from your view on the iOS Kickstarter app. If you want to notify us of a project that you feel is in violation of Our Rules, Community Guidelines, or Terms of Use (you can also check our Copyright and Trademark policies), you can do so by clicking “Report this project to Kickstarter” at the bottom of the project page.

If you want to specifically hide another user’s content or communications from your Kickstarter experience, you can do so by “Blocking” them. If you have any active pledges on a live project, and would like to withdraw your support, you must make sure to cancel your pledge before blocking. Please keep in mind that it won’t be possible to withdraw your support from a project that has already ended. In this case, we recommend reaching out to the creator to see if a refund may be possible.

If you need to block a user, you can do so from a few different places in our iOS app.

  • Comments
    • In comment threads, you have the ability to block a user by tapping the user’s profile icon and selecting the “Block this user” option. This action will hide all new and existing comments from the blocked user within the iOS app.
  • Messages
    • Within the messaging feature, you have the ability to block a user by tapping the user’s profile icon and selecting the “Block this user” option. This will prevent the user from sending any further messages to you through the iOS app.
  • Project pages
    • On project pages, you have the ability to block a user by tapping the user’s profile icon and selecting the “Block this user” option. This will stop you seeing any projects or communications from them in the iOS app.

It isn’t possible to block a user from pledging to a project. If there is a backer you wish to remove from your project, you can submit a request to remove them by clicking on their name in a comment they have posted and tapping the “Remove backer” button.

To reverse a block and re-establish communication with a previously blocked user in the iOS Kickstarter app, you can submit a request here.

How do I follow creators through the app?

Following project creators is a great way to find out about exciting new projects to back! Not only will you receive a notification when a new project from that creator launches, but you’ll be notified of any Kickstarter projects they back too.

To follow a creator on iOS:

  • Click or tap the creator’s name on the project page to view their bio.
  • On the next page, click or tap the creator’s name again to open their profile page.
  • This will open in a new browser window, where you’ll see a button to follow them (make sure that you are logged into your Kickstarter account on your mobile browser to see this button).

To follow a creator on Android:

  • Click or tap the creator’s name on the project page to view their bio.
  • On the next page, click or tap the creator’s name again to open their profile page.
  • Here you’ll see a button to follow them (make sure that you are logged into your Kickstarter account on your mobile browser to see this button).

Check out this article for how to follow creators from a web browser.

How do I pledge through the app?

Congratulations on finding a project that you’d like to support!

Before you start, make sure that you’re logged into a Kickstarter account on the app. Then, to pledge to a project, simply follow the steps below.

To pledge on iOS:

  • Go to the project you’d like to support, tap the “Back this project” button on the page and scroll across to see all of the available reward tiers.
    • If you don’t wish to receive a reward but still want to support the project, you can select the “Pledge without a reward” tier and enter your desired pledge amount.
  • Select the reward you’re interested in and if it involves shipping, be sure to choose the appropriate country from the drop-down menu.
    • For rewards marked as in-person local receipt, you will see the location listed with the reward.
    • When a reward is digital there will be no shipping option.
  • If there are add-on rewards available, you’ll have the option to add these on the next page. To continue without adding anything here, simply tap the “Skip add-ons” button.
    • You can also add some bonus support, if you’d like, by tapping the “+” and “-” buttons.
  • Next, you can view a summary of your pledge before heading to checkout.
  • Confirm that everything is correct and, when ready, enter your payment details and tap the “Pledge” button to complete. Alternatively, you can tap the “Pay” button to pledge using Apple Pay*.

*We do not currently support “Apple Pay Later”.

To pledge on Android:

  • Go to the project you’d like to support, tap the “Back this project” button on the page and scroll across to see all of the available reward tiers.
    • If you don’t wish to receive a reward but still want to support the project, you can select the “Pledge without a reward” tier and enter your desired pledge amount.
  • Select the reward you’re interested in and if it involves shipping, be sure to choose the appropriate country from the drop-down menu.
    • For rewards marked as in-person local receipt, you will see the location listed with the reward.
    • When a reward is digital there will be no shipping option.
  • If there are add-on rewards available, you’ll have the option to add these on the next page. To continue without adding anything here, simply tap the “Skip add-ons” button.
    • You can also add some bonus support, if you’d like, by tapping the “+” and “-” buttons.
  • Next, you can view a summary of your pledge before heading to checkout.
  • Confirm that everything is correct and, when ready, enter your payment details and tap the “Pledge” button to complete.

Please note: If the reward tier selected is digital it won’t be possible to add physical add-ons to your pledge.

You should receive a confirmation email to confirm that you are now a backer of the project. If the project is successfully funded, your card will be charged when it reaches its deadline.

If you experience any issues while trying to complete your pledge, please consult this article of common pledge problems.

I’m not receiving push notifications to my device

Push notifications for the Kickstarter app, for both Android and iOS, require the use of a token. In some instances, such as when you change your device or log in to multiple devices, it will be necessary to “refresh” the token to allow notifications to be delivered successfully.

If you’ve stopped receiving push notifications, here are a few things you can try:

  • Navigate to your Kickstarter Account settings, click the “Log me out on all other devices” button, and log back into the app on your device. Doing so should refresh your token and resume the delivery of push notifications.
  • Head to the Notifications tab of your settings and check your preferences. You’ll notice an icon for email and mobile alongside each notification option. Make sure the mobile icon has been toggled to “ON” (i.e. blue) for any notifications you wish to receive.
  • Double-check your mobile device settings to ensure that push notifications have been toggled “ON” for the Kickstarter app.

If you’re still not receiving push notifications after trying all of the above steps, please contact our Support team.

My pledge was declined, can I fix this through the app?

If your pledge has been declined, you’ll need to log in to your Kickstarter account to fix the payment.

To fix the payment through iOS:

  • Tap the Activity icon at the bottom of the screen
  • You will see a project card that shows you which pledge we were not able to process
  • Tap “Fix payment” or “Authenticate card” to be redirected to the screen to fix your payment
  • In the pop-up, select to either retry the current card or try a new payment method

To fix the payment through Android:

  • Visit the project page or select “Project alerts” from the left hand navigation menu to see which pledge we were not able to process
  • Tap “Fix payment” or “Authenticate card” to be redirected to the screen to fix your payment
  • Here, select to either retry the current card or try a new payment method

There may not be a specific issue with your card, your bank or card issuer might just have flagged it to be safe. As a first step, if you’re unable to fix the payment on Kickstarter, we recommend reaching out to your bank or card issuer to ask for help.

When you call, it’s helpful to include the details of this transaction, such as:

  • The charge being from Kickstarter
  • The amount pledged for the project
  • The date when Kickstarter attempted to collect your pledge (this is the date that the project ended)
    • If the project hasn’t ended yet, you can use the date you attempted to pledge
  • Additionally, it may be helpful to request that your bank add any charges from Kickstarter to their “allow list”

Once you’ve contacted your bank or card issuer, log back into the Kickstarter app and try again.

You’ll have seven days from the date the project ended to resolve the payment issue, before you’ll be dropped from the project. We’ll automatically retry your pledge one more time at the end of the seven days.

If you continue to encounter issues fixing the payment for your pledge through the app, we recommend logging into your Kickstarter account on a web browser and trying again on our desktop site instead.

The Project Alerts Dashboard (Beta)

The Project Alerts dashboard (beta) on the Kickstarter app will allow you to manage all outstanding actions for your backed projects in one place - such as responding to surveys, resolving pledge payment issues, or messaging creators.

How can I access this feature?

  • On your device, visit the App Store (iOS) or Google Play Store (Android), to make sure you have the latest version of the Kickstarter app. If an update is available, tap to install.
  • Open the Kickstarter app on your device and head to the Project Alerts dashboard via the steps below:
    • On iOS: Tap the Activity icon at the bottom of the screen and you’ll be taken to the “Project alerts” tab if you have alerts.
    • On Android: Tap the menu icon in the top left of the screen and select “Project alerts”.
  • Scroll through your alerts to complete any outstanding actions. Please note that if there are no pending actions, your dashboard will be empty.

What does the Project Alerts dashboard do?

From this dashboard, you can:

  • Submit your response to any open backer surveys from projects
  • Fix errored payments to ensure none of your pledges drop
  • Message creators about projects with outstanding actions

What else does this dashboard do?

Over the course of 2025, we’ll be adding more functionality to this dashboard so that you can easily track all of your pledges in one place.

What are the OS/device requirements for the Kickstarter app?

The Kickstarter iOS app requires OS 14.0 or later. It’s compatible with iPhone, iPad, and iPod touch.

The Kickstarter app for Android requires Android 6.0 (Marshmallow) or higher.

What is Sign in with Apple and how can I use it to create an account through the app?

Sign in with Apple is an iOS feature that allows you to securely create an account and sign in with your Apple ID.
