To create a Kickstarter account with your Apple ID through the Kickstarter app simply tap the “Continue with Apple” button on the login screen. You will then be prompted for a name and to choose which email address you wish to share with us; you can choose to either use the email address tied to your Apple ID or to hide it and use a relay email system instead.

This feature is also available when visiting Kickstarter on a web browser.

​I already have a Kickstarter account, can I use the new Sign in with Apple feature?

​

At the moment, it isn’t possible to sign in to an existing Kickstarter account using the new Sign in with Apple feature. Additionally, if you already had a Kickstarter account and you have created a new account with your Apple ID, please note that we do not currently support merging accounts.

​I signed up with Apple and I’m having trouble accessing the survey

If you opted to hide your email address when signing up with Apple, project creators will only be able to see your private relay email in their backer report at the end of the campaign.

Please note that the unique relay address tied to your Kickstarter account can only be used for communication with Kickstarter. This means that any communication sent outside of our platform by the creator will unfortunately not reach your inbox.

In this case, we’d recommend reaching out to the project creator to clarify the situation and to request that they update your email on their end.

If the creator is using Kickstarter’s own survey system, they should be able to update the email address on file by editing the exported backer report. They can find your pledge by looking up your backer number.

If they are using a third-party survey service, such as BackerKit, the creator will need to provide them with your new email address so that they can update the information on their end.

​How can I disconnect my Kickstarter account from my Apple ID?

If you signed up to Kickstarter using the “Continue with Apple” option and would now like to disconnect the accounts, you can follow the steps to do so here.

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager

Can I charge a tariff surcharge through the pledge manager?

We understand that having to pay a surprise tariff on rewards imported to the US can have a negative impact on your budget and cause unexpected delays to your project timeline.

For creators using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, we’ve created the Tariff Manager to help you apply a tariff surcharge to backer’s orders and offset some of the costs. To enable the Tariff Manager, you’ll see a banner at the top of the pledge manager page that gives you the option to submit a form to request access to this tool. If accepted, we’ll enable the Tariff Manager for your project, and you’ll see a tariff surcharge field for each item, within the tax and tariff section of your pledge manager.

Backers will see the total tariff surcharge they are being charged at checkout and on the order confirmation email they receive.

Please note: At this time, tariff surcharges may only be applied to backers with a US shipping address.

What happens if a backer can’t pay the tariff surcharge?

If a backer chooses not to pay the tariff surcharge when completing their pledge manager, they’ll need to reach out to you directly to explore their options. This may be by adjusting their pledge, issuing a refund, or finding another resolution.

If you are not eligible to use the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, you will need to find an alternative arrangement to collect tariff surcharges from your backers.

Can I edit my backers’ orders after they’ve completed their Pledge manager order?

If using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, creators can make some edits to their backers’ orders after they’ve gone through Pledge manager checkout.

Edits that can be made:

  • Add new add‑ons to a backer’s order
  • Upgrade rewards (for example, move a backer from one reward tier to a reward tier of equal or higher value)
  • Change SKUs / item variants (e.g. size, color, region-specific variants)
  • Swap items by:
    • Refunding the original item
    • Then using the Order edit tools to add the new replacement item

All order edits can be initiated via the Backer report by clicking on the backer’s name to open the pledge detail modal, then clicking ‘Edit Order’. Once the edit has been made and submitted, backers review and pay for their updated order in a dedicated Backer order edit checkout flow.

To upgrade a reward

  • Select the reward you’d like the backer to be upgraded to. Any reward of equal or higher value will appear
  • Submit edits to the backer
  • Upon confirming edits, the backer will be able to respond to any item preference questions and be charged for difference between their original reward and the new reward, and any applicable shipping and tax. The backer will be refunded the original shipping and tax associated with their original reward

To add a new add-on

  • Select the new add-on(s) you’d like to add to a backer’s order
  • Submit edits to the backer
  • Upon confirming edits, the backer will be able to respond to any item preference questions and be charged for the new add-on and any applicable shipping and tax

To adjust survey question responses

  • Select “Edit preferences” next to any item and change the SKU selection
  • Submit the preference change

Can I edit the pledge manager after it has launched?

Yes, after you have launched the pledge manager for your project you will be able to make edits to some of the information you previously added.

To view and edit your pledge manager settings, head to the Pledge Manager page and click the “Settings” button. If a section is editable you will see an “Edit” button next to it. If a section is read-only you can click the “Review” button to view the information added but no changes can be made.

Please note: Any edits that may adjust the total a backer is charged at checkout, will only apply to backers who have not yet entered the pledge manager for your project.

For backers who have already started the process of completing the pledge manager they will see the existing settings. If you need to collect any additional funds from a backer who has already entered or completed the pledge manager, you will need to work with them directly to make an alternative arrangement.

The following edits can be made after launch:

  • Welcome message
  • Rewards & add-ons:
    • Add new rewards and add-ons using existing items
    • Edit the settings of any rewards and add-ons that have no (0) backers
    • If a reward or add-on has already been pledged to, you will only be able to edit:
      • Availability of the reward for upgrades
      • Quantity of the reward
      • Per-backer quantity of an add-on
  • Set order edit preferences
    • Choose whether to allow backers to edit their survey response, or increase their order, and for how long
  • Shipping costs:
    • For flat rate shipping:
      • Shipping costs
      • Shipping destinations
    • For weight-based shipping:
      • Item weights
      • Weight tables and costs

The following edits cannot be made after launch:

  • Backer survey
  • Tax settings for items, your tax information, and tariff costs
  • Shipping origin and main business addresses
  • Your selected shipping rule (weight-based or flat rate)

If you encounter a problem with making any of the qualifying edits, please contact our Support team.

Can I send digital rewards to my backers through Kickstarter?

Yes, if you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager you will have the option to send digital rewards to backers through the backer report. You can send rewards as a digital file, code, or URL.

To send something to a single backer through the backer report, simply click the three dots under the gear icon for that backer and select the action you wish to take. When multiple backers are selected, you will see a menu appear at the bottom of the screen showing you the available bulk actions.

Digital files

For rewards that require uploading and distributing a digital file, you can do this through Kickstarter by uploading digital files and attaching them to the relevant rewards. To start, select ‘Digital files’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page.

  • On the Digital files page, click the “Upload a file” button
  • In the modal that opens up, select your file to begin the upload
    • Your file must be a PDF, PNG, JPG, MP3, MP4, ZIP, STL, or ePub, no larger than 1 GB
    • Please note that the maximum storage limit per project is 2 GB
  • Next, select from one of the two distribution methods for your file:
    • Automatic: It will be automatically sent to the relevant backers as they complete the pledge manager. This will require selecting which items and SKUs to attach the file to.
    • Manually: You will manually send this to backers who have completed your project’s pledge manager, whenever you are ready, through the backer report.
  • Remember: If you select to distribute the file automatically, you will need to select from the dropdown menu which of your project’s items and SKUs the file should be attached to.
  • Add a name for your digital file, along with any instructions, if applicable
  • Click the “Save” button to finish adding your digital file

You will be able to keep track of all digital files, and how many backers have downloaded each one, on the Digital files page for your project.

If you need to replace a digital file you can do so at any time. When a file is replaced, backers will automatically lose access to the old version but gain access to the new one.

When a digital file is sent, either automatically or manually, each backer will receive an email notification to let them know that their files are available. They can then view and download these from the ‘Digital rewards’ tab of their Pledge summary page. Currently, backers will have access to their digital files for two years from the date of distribution.

Digital codes and URLs

If your project included digital rewards that only require a code or URL to access, you will have the option to send these within the backer report as either unique or shared access.

When sending a unique access code or URL to multiple backers at once, make sure to separate each entry with a comma, and only add one unique access code or URL per recipient. For example, if you are sending digital reward codes to 10 backers, only enter 10 unique codes. If you wish to send a unique access code to a specific backer, you will need to send this to them individually.

For a shared access code or URL, simply add this to the modal and it will be sent to all selected backers.

Once sent, we will send the backers an email with their digital reward code or URL and any instructions, if included. They can also find this on the ‘Digital rewards’ tab of their Pledge summary page.

Configuring the Pledge Manager Tax Section

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager currently supports sales and value-added tax collection in the United States, Canada, the European Union, and the United Kingdom. Creators can also choose to opt out of VAT collection through Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager, in which case VAT will not be calculated, collected, or remitted by Kickstarter.

In accordance with marketplace facilitator regulations, Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager calculates, collects, and remits applicable sales and use tax on Kickstarter Pledge Manager transactions, where a backer resides in the United States or Canada.

In accordance with applicable deemed supplier regulations in the European Union and the United Kingdom, Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager will calculate, collect, and remit value-added tax (VAT) on applicable Kickstarter Pledge Manager transactions, including:

  • Low-value imported goods; and
  • Distance sales between Member States.

Learn more about specific sales and value-added tax collection in supported jurisdictions:

  • United States
  • Canada
  • United Kingdom
  • European Union

Tax services provided by Kickstarter and its subsidiaries do not constitute tax advice and creators are encouraged to work with their tax advisor to ensure compliance with applicable tax regulations.

​Pledge Manager Tax FAQ

How do I add a free item to a backer’s order?

Is there an existing reward item you’d like to give to a backer as a gift? Maybe you reached a milestone with your goal and want to include an extra reward in every order as a thank you? No problem, if you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager you can do so through the backer report!

To add a free item, select all backers you plan to give the item to and select “Add free items” from the actions menu. Next, choose one of your existing reward items and add it to the order.

In the event that a backer’s pledge already includes a quantity of the free item, adding another will increase this quantity, unless you choose to skip that backer.

After a free item is added a backer will be able to see this when viewing their pledge details. If a free item is added before a backer completes the pledge manager for your project, they will see this free item at checkout.

To add a free item to a group of backers based on their email address, simply enter the email addresses, separated by a comma, within the backer email address filter. This will filter the backer report to only show the backers of your project that match with one of the email addresses.

Please note: At this time it is only possible to add a free item to a backer’s order if you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool.

How do I charge shipping through the pledge manager?

If you are planning to use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager to coordinate the fulfillment of your project, we recommend waiting to charge shipping in your pledge manager. We make this easy by providing an option in the project editor to charge shipping later. If this is selected, you will be able to show a shipping cost estimate during your live campaign. You can then configure the final shipping amount through the pledge manager closer to fulfillment. This helps to ensure that the shipping cost is more accurate based on the final number of orders, as well as allow for any potential price changes from shipping providers.

The shipping cost estimate backers will see at checkout will be calculated based on the estimates associated with each reward in their pledge. For example: If a reward tier has an estimate of $10-20, and the backer adds 2 x add-ons with an estimate of $5-10 each, the total shipping cost estimate for that pledge will be $20-40.

If you choose to charge shipping later, no shipping fee will be collected from backers at the time of their initial pledge, nor included in their final pledge amount paid to you, and shipping costs won’t count towards your project’s goal.

Tip: As you build out your rewards and set shipping cost estimates, note that an estimated shipping range for a precise country will always trump the cost of a region. For instance, if you offer a shipping rate estimate of $15-20 for the European Union, but only $10-15 for shipping to France, a backer based in France will be shown the $10-15 shipping rate estimate.

For more information about the shipping options available when building your rewards, as well as which countries are included in the European Union shipping option, take a look at this article.

​What options do I have to charge shipping in the pledge manager?

When setting up your project you will have the option on the Preferences tab of your project editor to either charge shipping at pledge, or charge it later. If you plan to charge shipping at pledge while your campaign is live, you can find more information about setting that up here.

If charge shipping later is selected on the Preferences tab, your backers will be shown an estimated shipping range at the time of their initial pledge, they will then be charged the final shipping cost - set by you - when they complete your project’s pledge manager.

Using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager, you will be prompted to provide the finalized shipping costs for each country, for every reward tier, and for each add-on. On the ‘Finalize shipping costs’ tab of the pledge manager, you can select from one of the two options for the shipping rule, these are:

  • Weight-based shipping
    • For this option, you’ll start by choosing which unit of measurement you plan to use. This can be either ‘Kilograms (kg)’ or ‘Ounces (oz)’
    • Next, you’ll add your shipping zones and set the shipping rates for each. You can include as many weight ranges as needed for each shipping zone.
      • For example, for France, you may decide that shipping rewards weighing between 1-2kg will cost $10, but if a reward weighs between 2-3kg, this will be $20.
    • To allow for all possible order weights, we recommend leaving the upper weight limit blank on your highest range. For example:
      • Range 1 = 1-2kg ($10)
      • Range 2 = 2-3kg ($20)
      • Range 3 = 3kg and up ($30)
        • Note: Each range is for orders up to the higher weight. As such, using this example, a backer with an order that weighs 2kg will be charged $20.
    • You then need to set a weight for each of your reward items. The set weight can have no more than 2 decimal places. Every item included in your pledge manager must either have a set weight or be checked as excluded.
      • An excluded item might be a bonus item that you included within a reward tier, but that shouldn’t be factored into the total weight of a backer’s order.
  • Flat rate shipping
    • For this option, you’ll simply add the actual cost to ship to each of your shipping destinations, for each of your reward tiers and add-ons.
      • Shipping costs can easily be duplicated from other reward tiers and add-ons to save you from re-entering the same costs multiple times.
    • You will also have the option to add new shipping destinations.

Make sure to create a shipping plan for your project, and think about how you will charge shipping on your rewards.

How do I issue a refund for funds received through the pledge manager?

After a backer has completed the pledge manager for your project you will be able to issue a refund for any funds received through the pledge manager.

To issue a refund, head to your backer report, located in the creator navigation bar to the left of your project page, and follow the steps below:

  • Search for the backer in the backer report and click on their name.
  • In the modal that opens up, click the “Issue a refund” button.
  • On the next page you will have the option to issue a full or partial refund.
    • A full refund will result in the backer receiving 100% of their order total, including shipping, fees, and any taxes.
    • When issuing a partial refund you can choose to enter a specific refund amount for each item within the pledge, or simply enter a general refund amount. You can also choose to fully remove an item.
  • Select a reason for the refund and, if you’d like to, you can include a message to be sent to the backer within their refund email.
  • Next, choose a refund source from the cards saved to your account, or enter a new personal credit card (this will be the card that the refund is issued from).
  • Lastly, check all information is correct and click the “Issue refund” button to confirm.

It’s important to note that any Pledge Manager refunds will be issued from the source card you added to your project on the Payment tab or the new card added during the refund flow, rather than from your campaign or Pledge Manager funds collected, regardless of payout status.

How do I know if I’m eligible to use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager?

All projects are eligible to use the Kickstarter Pledge Manager tool, provided your project’s rewards have all been properly itemized, you have not already sent the Kickstarter Backer Survey to your backers, and that you are not using another pledge manager service for this campaign.

​How do I access the Kickstarter Pledge Manager?

For all projects, the ‘Pledge manager’ tool will become available in the creator navigation bar, to the left of your project page, once your project has launched.

If you change your mind about using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager, you can choose not to launch it and switch to using the survey tool only via the banner at the top of the pledge manager page. In this case, you will need to coordinate with your backers an alternative way to collect any shipping costs.

Changed your mind again? No worries, as long as you haven’t already launched the Backer Survey you can opt back in to the Pledge Manager by clicking the “Use pledge manager” button at the top of the survey page.

For projects that become ineligible to use the Kickstarter Pledge Manager after answering the eligibility survey, you will instead see ‘Backer survey’. If you believe you made a mistake with the eligibility survey, you can contact our Support team and we can help to reset it for you.

​I ran a campaign on a third-party platform, can I use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager?

Unfortunately not, Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager is only available for campaigns that ran on Kickstarter and meet our above eligibility requirements.

Creators with a successful Kickstarter campaign can choose to use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool, or a third-party service.

How do I prevent double-charging my backers for VAT?

If you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager and the tax collection feature for international shipments, it is essential that your shipping labels and customs documentation clearly signal that VAT has already been collected. Failure to do this often results in the carrier mistakenly charging your backers again at the border.

To avoid this, follow these steps:

Step 1: Identify the correct Tax ID:

  • If Kickstarter is the Deemed Supplier (typically under €150/£135): You must use the Kickstarter IOSS or UK VAT ID, provided in your downloaded backer report under the ‘Transaction VAT ID’ column.
  • If you are the tax collector (typically over threshold): You must use your own registered VAT ID.

Step 2: Update your Shipping Data: When creating shipping labels or customs entries in your carrier’s portal (e.g., DHL, FedEx, UPS), ensure the Tax ID is entered in the designated “Tax/VAT ID” field. Do not rely on hand-written notes alone.

​

​Step 3: Include the “Tax Paid” Declaration: Add the following text to the “Notes,” “Description,” or “Invoice” section of all your shipping documents:

​

​TAX PAID DECLARATION

​VAT STATUS: VAT PRE-COLLECTED AT POINT OF SALE.

​VAT REGISTRATION NUMBER: [INSERT KICKSTARTER ID OR YOUR OWN VAT ID]

​REASON: TRANSACTION PROCESSED UNDER MARKETPLACE FACILITATOR / SELLER-COLLECTED VAT REGIME.

​CARRIER INSTRUCTIONS: The VAT for this shipment has been fully collected from the recipient at the time of purchase. Please utilize the provided VAT Identification number to process the customs entry as “pre-paid.” Do not collect import VAT or additional VAT-related brokerage fees from the recipient. If additional documentation is required, please contact: [INSERT CREATOR SUPPORT EMAIL].

​

​Step 4: Use Accurate HS Codes: Ensure every item is assigned a correct HS code. This allows customs to accurately categorize the goods and verify the pre-paid tax status.

​

​Step 5: Coordinate with your Carrier: Before shipping, confirm with your courier that your account is configured to pass through the IOSS/VAT ID information electronically to customs.

Commercial Invoice Note Template

Copy and paste this block into the “Notes” or “Description” section of your commercial invoice.

TAX PAID DECLARATION

VAT STATUS: VAT HAS BEEN PRE-COLLECTED AT POINT OF SALE.

​VAT REGISTRATION NUMBER: [INSERT KICKSTARTER VAT/IOSS ID OR YOUR OWN VAT ID]

​REASON: TRANSACTION PROCESSED UNDER MARKETPLACE FACILITATOR / SELLER-COLLECTED VAT REGIME.

Please do not collect import VAT from the recipient. If additional documentation is required, please contact [INSERT CREATOR EMAIL] immediately.

How do I send tracking numbers through Kickstarter?

If you are a creator using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager to manage the next stage of your campaign, you will have the option to send backers their tracking numbers through the backer report.

To send something to a single backer, simply click the three dots under the gear icon for that backer and select the action you wish to take.

When multiple backers are selected, you will see a menu appear at the bottom of the screen showing you the available bulk actions.

To help your backers know when their rewards are expected to be delivered, you can use the backer report to send them the tracking numbers for their order. You can choose to either select a single backer and send them their tracking numbers only, or select multiple backers and send all tracking numbers in one go.

To bulk upload tracking numbers, select multiple backers using the checkboxes on the left side of the backer report. You will see an option to download a CSV template* in the window that pops up. Next, open this template on your device and enter the tracking numbers for each backer in the relevant column of the spreadsheet. Save the file, upload the spreadsheet within the same window, and once uploaded click “Add tracking numbers”.

When a tracking number is added, we will send this to the backer in an email and add it to the ‘Backing details’ tab of their pledge summary page.

If we encounter any issues matching the tracking numbers, it is likely that the content in the spreadsheet needs to be re-formatted and uploaded again. If you are still having trouble after that, please contact our Support team.

*The CSV file can be opened in any spreadsheet program, such as Numbers, Microsoft Excel, or Google Sheets.

How do I sync pledge manager orders to ShipStation?

If you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager to manage the next stage of your project, you can use the ShipStation integration to connect the pledge manager directly to your ShipStation account.

Once set up, finalized orders will sync automatically to ShipStation for fulfillment, and tracking numbers will be sent back to Kickstarter so that your backers are kept up to date without any manual data entry.

Before you begin you will need:

  • An active Kickstarter Pledge Manager that is currently live or has been closed
  • A ShipStation account on the Gold tier or above (required for API access)
  • Admin/Account Owner access to your ShipStation account

Step 1: Generate your API key in ShipStation

Your API Key and Secret Key are how Kickstarter authenticates with your ShipStation account. Only the ShipStation Account Owner can generate these.

  • Log in to ShipStation and click the cog icon in the top right to go to Account Settings.
  • In the left sidebar, expand Account and select API Settings.
  • Make sure V1 API is selected in the dropdown, then click Generate API Key.
  • ShipStation will send a verification email to the account owner’s email address. Click the link in that email, then return to API Settings and click Generate API Key again.
  • Choose an expiry period (3, 6, or 12 months) and click Generate.
  • Your API Key and Secret Key will appear. Copy both immediately and store them somewhere secure. These will only be shown once. Once you navigate away from the page, they are hidden permanently. If you lose them, you will need to generate a new set.

Please note: API Keys can only be generated and viewed by the ShipStation Account Owner. If you are unsure who that is, contact [email protected].

Step 2: Connect ShipStation in Kickstarter

  • Go to your project in Kickstarter and select ‘Pledge manager’ from the creator tools menu, located on the left-hand side.
  • On the Pledge Manager overview page, find ‘Sync orders to ShipStation’ and click “Configure ShipStation sync”.
  • On the connection screen, enter your API Key and Secret Key from Step 1.
  • Click “Connect to ShipStation”.

If the connection is successful, you’ll see a confirmation. If you see an error, double-check that your API key and Secret Key were copied correctly and that your ShipStation account is on the Gold tier or above.

How the sync works once connected

Orders going to ShipStation:

  • Finalized pledge manager orders sync automatically to ShipStation
  • If a backer updates their address, that change syncs through
  • If an order is refunded (fully or partially), ShipStation is updated accordingly

Tracking numbers coming back to Kickstarter:

  • When ShipStation marks an order as shipped, tracking information is sent back to Kickstarter automatically
  • Tracking numbers appear in your backer report
  • Backers receive an email notification and can view the tracking information on their pledge summary page

Managing the integration

Once set up, you can:

  • Pause the sync to temporarily stop orders from syncing without removing the integration
  • Restart the sync to resume syncing after a pause
  • Remove the integration to disconnect ShipStation entirely. Any data already in ShipStation stays there, but no new data will sync. You would need to reconnect from scratch to resume

Who can manage this integration?

By default, only the project creator can set up and manage the ShipStation integration. To allow a collaborator to manage it, select ‘Collaborators’ from the creator tools menu, located on the left-hand side of your project page, and under ‘Configure pledge manager’, enable the ‘Manage fulfillment partner integrations’ permission for the relevant collaborator.

Troubleshooting

My API Key isn’t working

Make sure you have copied both the API Key and Secret Key correctly. These are only visible immediately after generation. If you didn’t save them, you will need to generate a new set in ShipStation.

I can’t generate an API Key in ShipStation

Only the Account Owner can do this. Check with your team or contact ShipStation support to confirm who holds that role.

Pledge manager orders aren’t syncing

Check that your pledge manager is live and that orders have been finalized. Only completed orders are sent to ShipStation.

I’m not seeing tracking numbers

Tracking information is sent back to Kickstarter once ShipStation marks an order as shipped. If ShipStation hasn’t processed a shipment yet, the tracking number won’t appear.

​Related articles
​Setting up IOSS for EU shipments via ShipStation

How do I sync pledge manager orders to Shopify?

If you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager to manage the next stage of your project, you can use the Shopify integration to connect the pledge manager directly to your Shopify store.

Once set up, you will be able to sync finalized orders in Segments to your Shopify store so that you can manage fulfillment in one place, with your existing Shopify products and workflows.

Before you begin you will need:

  • An active Kickstarter Pledge Manager that is currently live
  • An existing Shopify store
  • Products already set up in Shopify with SKUs that correspond to your Kickstarter reward variants
  • Admin access to your Shopify store

Please note: The SKU matching step is a key part of setup. Kickstarter will match your reward variants to Shopify products using SKU names. If your Shopify products do not have SKUs set, the sync will not complete. If your Shopify products are already live and you cannot change their SKUs, you can update the SKU names on the Kickstarter side during the matching step instead. Either way, the SKU names need to match between both platforms before you can sync.

Step 1: Connect your Kickstarter project to Shopify

Kickstarter connects to your Shopify store via a Shopify app. You will need to download the app and connect it to your Shopify store.

  • Go to your project in Kickstarter and select ‘Pledge manager’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left-hand side.
  • On the Pledge Manager overview page, find ‘Sync backings to Shopify (Optional)’ and click “Configure Shopify sync”.
  • You will be brought to the Shopify app store. Click “Install” on the Kickstarter app listing.
  • Next, view the data access requirements for the Kickstarter app and click “install.” You’ll then be brought back to Kickstarter to complete Step 2.

If the connection is successful, you will move on to the next step of the setup flow automatically.

Step 2: Match your Kickstarter SKUs to Shopify

Once connected, Kickstarter will display all of your reward variants alongside their SKU names. You will need to confirm these match the corresponding products in your Shopify store.

  • If a SKU is matched correctly, it will show a Shopify icon next to it.
  • If a SKU is unmatched (shown with a warning icon), click “Edit” to update it or go to Shopify and ensure the product has the correct SKU set under the product’s inventory details.
    • Note: Edits made here to SKU names will only affect the SKU names in Kickstarter, they will not update SKU names in Shopify.
  • The “Finish setup” button will only become active once all SKUs are matched.

Once all SKUs show as matched, click “Finish setup” to finalize SKU mapping.This will not sync any orders.

Step 3: Sync orders to Shopify in Segments

  • To begin syncing orders, select ‘Backer report’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left-hand side.
  • Create a Segment of backers using any combination of filters to create a group of orders you want to sync to Shopify.
  • Once a Segment has been created and you’re ready to sync, use the Segment dropdown menu to select “Schedule sync to Shopify”.
  • Once “Schedule sync to Shopify” has been initiated, backers will receive a notification that they have 48 hours remaining to make any changes to their shipping address
  • After the 48 hour notice period has passed, the orders will appear in Shopify.
  • Once the sync to Shopify has been scheduled, there will be two visual indicators:
    • The address will appear in the backer report with a lock pending icon.
    • The Shopify sync status will appear as “Pending”. The Shopify sync status can be added to the backer report view by selecting the gear icon and adding the Shopify sync status to the visible columns.
  • You can sync orders in as many waves as needed to fulfill your project.
  • We recommend syncing a small test Segment to begin.

How the Shopify data is formatted

  • The total a backer paid for each reward and add-on gets split proportionally across each item, so the item prices contained within a reward or add-on always add up to what was paid. Free items show as $0. Shipping and tax are sent as their real amounts, so the order shows as fully paid.
  • By default, Kickstarter sends orders to Shopify with the following properties:
    • sourceName = Kickstarter
    • shippingAmount = Amount backer paid
    • shipCode = Kickstarter Shipping
    • lineItems.taxLines = Amount backer paid
    • Tags = project-name

What happens after a sync occurs

If a tracking number is added to an order in Shopify that was synced from Kickstarter, the tracking number will be automatically pulled into Kickstarter and displayed to the backer and to the creator in the backer’s backer report modal.

You will see a confirmation on the Pledge Manager overview showing how many backings have been synced. From there you can:

  • Pause the sync to temporarily stop orders from syncing
  • Resume the sync to start again after a pause

Orders will appear in your Shopify store and can be managed and fulfilled from there as normal.

If your sync is paused you can also disable it entirely, requiring a new set up. This is the recommended method to set it to another store or remap the SKUs if needed.

Troubleshooting

Some SKUs are showing as unmatched

This means Kickstarter cannot find a matching product in Shopify. Check that the SKU shown in Kickstarter matches exactly what is set in the relevant Shopify product (under Products > the product > Inventory > SKU). SKU matching is case-sensitive.

The “Finish set up” button is greyed out

All SKUs must be matched before you can sync. Resolve any unmatched SKUs first.

How do I use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager?

Please note: The Kickstarter Pledge Manager will only become available after your project has launched.

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager is an easy way to gather all the information you need in order to fulfill your backers’ rewards in one place, including collecting taxes and shipping costs closer to fulfillment time. There is no up front cost to using this tool. Kickstarter will deduct our usual fees from any payments made within the pledge manager.

If your project meets the criteria to use the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, you can start setting up the pledge manager for your campaign after it launches by selecting ‘Pledge manager’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page.

The pledge manager consists of sections that need to be completed before launching it. Some are optional, and you can choose to skip these. When you’re ready to begin completing a section, simply click the “Start” button next to it.

Welcome message (Optional)

The ‘Welcome message (Optional)’ section will be automatically set up with a generic introductory message to your pledge manager, that your backers will see. You can personalize this to make it more relevant to your project, including adding images or embedding links. This space can be used to thank your backers, share additional information, or direct people to your website.

Configure rewards and add-ons (Optional)

You can use this section to configure specific reward tiers and add-ons to be available for backers through the pledge manager. This includes the option for backers to upgrade their reward, choose add-ons, and even make it possible for new backers to purchase available rewards after your campaign has ended. You can set your pledge manager to be available for new backers by toggling it on at the top of the page:

In this section you’ll see a list of your project’s existing rewards and add-ons. For each one you can select whether or not to offer it in the pledge manager, as well as set the price and quantity limits. You will also be able to add brand new rewards and add-ons to be available specifically in the pledge manager.

When a backer adds or upgrades a reward in their pledge manager order, their survey will update to include any questions relating to the items that make up the reward. As with when your campaign was live, physical add-ons will only be available for backers who pledged to a physical reward tier.

Notes:

  • If a backer pledges any amount for no reward during the campaign, this amount cannot be applied toward a reward upgrade in the pledge manager.
  • Local pickup rewards cannot currently be offered as upgrades in the Pledge Manager.

Create survey (Optional)

The backer survey helps you collect your backers’ addresses, reward preferences, and any additional information you need in order to fulfill rewards. The steps for completing this section can be found here.

Set order edit preference

Here you can set whether or not to allow your backers to change their survey response after completing their order in the pledge manager. This will include reward preferences and any backer questions. If you decide to allow for survey response edits you can choose whether to make this available for a set period of time, until you close your pledge manager, or when you manually stop it.

Finalize shipping costs

If you chose not to charge shipping during your campaign backers will have been shown an estimated shipping cost range for their pledge, but no shipping fee was charged. Now is the time to finalize the shipping amount each backer will need to pay, based on their reward tier, any add-ons, and their location.

You will have the option to charge weight-based or flat rate shipping for each reward. More information about charging shipping through the pledge manager can be found here.

If shipping was charged at pledge, you can review these costs in the pledge manager. However, please note that it won’t be possible to make any changes to the previously set costs. Backers will only be asked to pay additional shipping costs if they upgrade their reward, add available add-ons to their order, or change their shipping country before completing the pledge manager checkout.

Add tax information for your items

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager (which is operated by its subsidiary Project Partners, LLC) is legally required to calculate, collect, and remit applicable sales/use tax on your behalf. To help us accurately calculate this, we’ll need you to complete the following for each reward item:

  • Select a category. To help us understand what each item is, choose a category from our list of commonly offered items.
    • If you can’t find a category that matches your item exactly, choose the closest option from the list. When you submit your pledge manager for review, our team will review these item categories and reach out to you if they have any questions.
  • Add the market value. This amount represents the MSRP (Manufacturer’s Suggested Retail Price); or the cost you would sell it for individually in the market.
  • Add a tariff surcharge. This option will only be available for creators who have requested use of the Tariff Manager tool.
  • Configure your shipping origin.

To help us correctly determine certain (but not all) taxes owed by a backer and who is responsible for remitting those certain taxes, we need to know where you will be shipping their reward from. You can add as many shipping origins as needed.

When adding a shipping origin, you will need to enter both the ship-from address and the countries this location will be shipping to. For example, if you have a shipping origin in France, you may only plan to ship to other European countries from this location, while your shipping origin in Canada may be used for all North American countries.

Every country selected by one of your backers must be assigned to a shipping origin. If there are countries missing an assigned location, they will be displayed under ‘Not assigned’.

If you only offered digital rewards, the shipping origin should be the address where you intend to send the digital assets from. This may be your home or an office address.

Add your tax information

Please note that this section will only need to be completed if you are a creator shipping rewards to or from the UK or EU.

The pledge manager offers the ability to collect sales tax or value-added tax (VAT) on transactions for backers who reside in the United States, Canada, the United Kingdom, and the European Union.

Here you may need to enter your UK VAT ID or EU One Stop Shop (OSS) VAT Number to enable Kickstarter to calculate and collect VAT on applicable transactions. If one of these tax ID numbers is required for your situation, you’ll see a field where you can enter it.

If you do not have a VAT ID, or would prefer not to use Kickstarter’s VAT collection service, you can choose to opt-out of VAT collection. By opting out, Kickstarter will not calculate, collect, or remit VAT on your behalf, and you will be responsible for managing VAT, including any import VAT or customs charges, directly or via your fulfillment or logistics partner. Backers may be required to pay VAT and/or import charges upon delivery.

If you are a creator with multiple VAT IDs, after finalizing your shipping costs and configuring your shipping origin, we’ll automatically show you which countries require a VAT ID based on the information you have provided.

Additionally, you’ll verify your office address (the address currently associated with your project). If you need to make any changes to the address we currently hold for you, simply click the “Edit in Stripe” button to update this through our payments processor, Stripe.

To learn more about how Kickstarter is approaching tax collection, check out our VAT and US sales tax articles.

Preview pledge manager

Once all required sections have been completed, you will be able to preview what the pledge manager will look like as a backer. To do this, scroll down to the bottom of the Pledge manager page for your project, where you’ll see a “Preview” button. Click this button to begin the preview process, and make the following selections:

  • Backer entry point: Choose when the backer whose experience you’re previewing backed your project. This may be during the crowdfunding campaign, through late pledging, or as a new backer through the pledge manager after it has launched.
  • Backer location: Select where your hypothetical backer is located.
  • Reward tier: If you chose “Crowdfunding campaign,” select which reward tier the backer pledged for.
  • Add-ons: If you chose “Crowdfunding campaign” and selected a reward tier, add any add-ons that your hypothetical backer may have included in their pledge.

Once your selections have been made, click “Preview as backer” to launch the preview. In this view, you will see all of the steps your backer will go through when they complete the pledge manager. As you navigate through these steps, you will also see a running tally of the order total, including the cost of rewards, add-ons, and any shipping or tax that is due.

Please note: This is a preview only and you will not be able to complete the pledge manager in this view. Additionally, at this time it isn’t possible to preview the pledge manager as a specific backer of your project, however we may add that capability in the future.

Launch pledge manager

Once all required sections have been completed, we recommend double-checking everything is correct before launching. You can do so by clicking “Edit” or “Resume” on a section from the main pledge manager page. When you’re ready to launch your pledge manager, click the “Submit for review” button. The pledge manager review by our Trust & Safety team can take up to 5 business days. In the event that they have questions about your pledge manager during the review phase, they will contact you directly via email.

When your pledge manager is approved for launch you can go ahead and click the “Launch” button to send it to your backers. Once launched you will be able to view everything you added to each section of your pledge manager, and edit some of this information, by clicking the “Settings” button”.

After launching your pledge manager, your backers who have paid in full will be notified by email that they need to enter the pledge manager, finalize their address, review their order, and pay any applicable shipping and taxes. If you have enabled Pledge Over Time and there are any backers who have not completed all of their payments yet, they will not be able to enter the pledge manager until all of their payments are complete. Those backers will be notified via email when they are able to join the pledge manager.

You can keep track of how many backers have completed their order on the pledge manager page. After you launch, you can also see all of your pledge manager configuration by clicking the Settings button.

Close the Pledge Manager

Your pledge manager must remain open for at least 3 weeks for all backers. After that time, you can go back to your pledge manager page and click “Close pledge manager”. We will then send an email to any backers who have not yet completed the checkout process informing them that they have 48 hours to complete it. Once you close the pledge manager, you’ll then be able to lock backer addresses when you are ready to begin fulfilling rewards.

​When should I launch my pledge manager?

The pledge manager should be launched when you are ready to begin production and fulfillment of your rewards, and you need to start collecting item preferences and shipping addresses. You can configure and edit your pledge manager before that time, but only click “Launch” when you’re ready to fulfill those orders.

After your pledge manager has launched you will be able to make edits to some of the sections, such as adding new shipping locations. However, we recommend double-checking everything is correct, and that nothing crucial is missing, before you send it to your backers.

You can learn more about the edits you’re able to make to your project’s pledge manager after launch here.

​How do Late Pledges work alongside the pledge manager?

To launch your pledge manager you will first need to end Late Pledges. When you launch the pledge manager, a notification will be sent to everyone who has backed your campaign so far.

Ending Late Pledges doesn’t mean new backers can no longer join your project, though. When configuring the rewards in your pledge manager you will see an option to allow new backers at the top of this page. When this toggle is turned on, we’ll automatically activate your project’s Spotlight page with a button linking to your pledge manager. You can also share the URL of your launched Kickstarter Pledge Manager with any potential backers and they’ll be able to pledge for available rewards.

​Can I lock addresses for specific backers only in the pledge manager?

Yes! If you plan to stagger the shipping of your rewards, and only need to lock the addresses for the backers receiving their rewards next, you can do this by creating a segment in your backer report.

To create a segment in your backer report, simply select the backers to include or use the filters to help find a specific group. Click “Save segment” in the top right corner and give your segment a name. All created segments will appear above the filters on the backer report view.

To schedule an address lock for a segment, select the segment and from the dropdown menu in the top right corner, select “Schedule address lock.”

Once the address lock is scheduled, backers in that segment will be notified that they have 48 hours to make any last minute changes to their address. An address column will also appear in the backer report view for the segment. Addresses will be in the “Lock pending” state for 48 hours, changing to “Locked” once the window has closed.

How to enter item values for bundles and reward packages

When you’re setting up your rewards in the pledge manager, you’ll notice we ask for the value of each item being offered. This is important for tax collection and shipping, and is a requirement for when your rewards cross international borders.

But what if your rewards are bundles, like a paint set that comes with paints and a brush, or a board game with bonus dice and cards? You might not have a separate retail price for each piece, and that can feel confusing.

Here we share how it works (and how to keep it simple).

Why item values matter

When packages travel internationally, customs agencies require that each item in the box has a value. This helps them:

  • Classify items correctly (so the right taxes and duties are applied)
  • Prevent delays at the border
  • Ensure the total value matches what your backer paid

If you only enter “Paint Set = $50” without breaking it down, customs may reject the shipment or assign their own values, which can cause headaches and delays for you and your backers.

Important: Using arbitrary values like $1 or $0 for bundle items can trigger customs audits and delays.

How to set values for bundles

Assign a reasonable value to each item within the bundle, so the total adds up to what the backer paid.

Here are a few simple ways to do that:

  1. Use market values if you know them.
  • Example: If a brush normally sells for $5 and paints for $45, declare $5 for the brush and $45 for the paints.
  1. Allocate the price proportionally.
  • Example: A board game bundle costs $60. The base game makes up most of the value, so you might assign $50 to the game, $5 to the dice, and $5 to the card pack.
  1. Estimate based on cost or weight.
  • If items don’t have their own retail prices, split the bundle value in a way that feels fair and can be explained if asked (for example, by production cost).

How to manage import taxes for international shipping (DDP vs. DDU)

If you are shipping goods internationally and have not opted into Kickstarter’s automatic tax collection for EU imports above the high threshold value, you are responsible for how your backers encounter import VAT and customs duties. You must choose one of the following “Incoterms” (shipping terms) and communicate it clearly to your backers.

​

Option 1: DDP (Delivered Duty Paid) – “Seamless Delivery”

In this scenario, you pay the import VAT and duties on behalf of your backers. This is the best experience for the backer, as they receive their reward without any extra bills at the door.

  • How to do it:
    • Carrier Setup: Log into your shipping account (e.g., DHL, FedEx, UPS) and explicitly select “Bill Duties & Taxes to Sender” (DDP) when booking the shipment.
    • Account Requirement: You must have a carrier account configured to pay duties; otherwise, the courier will revert to billing the backer.
    • Commercial Invoice: You must include the following “Tax Paid” declaration on your Commercial Invoice (in the ‘Notes’ or ‘Description’ field):
    • ​
    • ​— CUSTOMS DECLARATION (DDP - DUTIES PRE-PAID) —
    • ​VAT STATUS: VAT PRE-PAID BY SENDER.
    • ​DUTY STATUS: IMPORT DUTIES PRE-PAID BY SENDER.
    • ​INCOTERMS: DDP (Delivered Duty Paid).
    • ​CARRIER INSTRUCTIONS: The sender has pre-paid all import VAT, customs duties, and brokerage fees.
    • ​PLEASE BILL ALL IMPORT CHARGES TO SENDER ACCOUNT: [INSERT CARRIER ACCOUNT NUMBER].
    • ​DO NOT collect payments from the recipient. If held, contact: [INSERT CREATOR SUPPORT EMAIL].
    • ​

Option 2: DDU / DAP (Delivered Duty Unpaid) – “Pay at Arrival”

In this scenario, the backer pays the import VAT and duties upon arrival. This can lead to surprise fees, so transparency is vital to avoid package refusals.

  • How to do it:
    • Transparency: You must state on your campaign page:
    • ​“International orders are shipped DDU (Delivered Duty Unpaid). You will be responsible for all local import taxes, duties, and carrier brokerage fees upon delivery.”
    • Data Requirements: You must provide the backer’s phone number and email to the courier. If the courier cannot reach the backer for payment, the package will be returned to you at your expense.
    • Commercial Invoice: You must include the following declaration:
    • ​
    • ​— CUSTOMS DECLARATION (DDU/DAP) —
    • ​VAT STATUS: IMPORT VAT/DUTY NOT COLLECTED AT POINT OF SALE.
    • ​DUTY TERMS: DDU (Delivered Duty Unpaid) / DAP (Delivered at Place).
    • ​CARRIER INSTRUCTIONS: This shipment is DDU. Please contact the recipient directly to collect all applicable import VAT, customs duties, and brokerage fees prior to delivery. Do not bill the sender for import charges.
    • ​RECIPIENT CONTACT: [INSERT BACKER PHONE NUMBER & EMAIL - MANDATORY]
    • ​

Critical Best Practices for Both Methods

  • Use Accurate HS Codes: Incorrect codes cause customs to manually re-assess your goods, ignoring your pre-paid status.
  • Digital Data Entry: Do not rely on paper notes alone. Ensure that when you book your shipment in your carrier’s portal, you select the correct duty payment setting (DDP vs. DDU). Most carriers now use digital customs data, which overrides paper invoices.
  • Match Transaction Values: The value on your Commercial Invoice must exactly match the amount the backer paid. If there is a discrepancy, customs will trigger a manual review and will often ignore your “pre-paid” status to avoid tax evasion.

​How to enter item values for bundles and reward packages

How to read your Pledge Manager Tax Report

The pledge manager tax report for your project can be found by selecting ‘Payouts & Taxes’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page. Each row represents one tax calculation for an order component (for example: an item, add-on, or shipping).

Please note: Each report shows how tax was calculated and collected through Kickstarter. Creators should use this information to support their own tax filings or reconciliation.

Key columns explained

Order & Identification

  • Order ID / Order Item ID / Shipment ID
  • Use these to match tax lines back to a specific backer order, item, or shipment
  • Backer ID
  • Internal reference for the backer who placed the order
  • (Please note: The ID for a backer will only be visible to you and is separate to the backer number assigned at pledge.)
  • Sale Date
  • The date tax was calculated (this is the relevant date for tax reporting)
  • Currency
  • Currency used for the transaction

What was taxed

  • Item Name
  • What the tax applies to (e.g. reward item, add-on, shipping)
  • Item Type
  • Item, shipping, or other charge
  • Amount
  • The taxable amount
  • Pledge Voucher Amount
  • Pledge amount

Tax details

  • Tax Category / Avalara Tax Code
  • How the item was classified for tax purposes
  • Avalara Invoice Number
  • Reference used by Kickstarter’s tax engine (not a creator invoice)

Project Partners LLC (Kickstarter) handled taxes

  • Project Partners LLC Responsible Tax Name / Rate / Amount
  • Taxes that were remitted by Project Partners LLC (Kickstarter)

Creator-handled taxes (if applicable)

  • Creator Responsible Tax Name / Rate / Amount
  • Taxes that are shown as the creator’s responsibility (not remitted by Kickstarter)

Location

  • Address / City / Postal Code / Region / Country Code
  • Used to determine the correct tax jurisdiction
  • ​

Project Partners LLC / Creator Tax Name

This shows the jurisdiction and type of tax applied.

For UK/EU VAT, many lines will say “Standard rate”. To understand which VAT this is, check:

  • Country Code (e.g. GB, DE, FR)
  • Sales Tax Rate (e.g. 20%, 19%)

Together, these identify the tax (for example: UK VAT at 20%).

If Creator Responsible Tax Amount is populated:

  • The tax is not remitted by Kickstarter
  • The creator is responsible for handling VAT/tax outside the platform

If Project Partners LLC Responsible Amount is populated:

  • Tax was collected through the Pledge Manager
  • Project Partners LLC (Kickstarter) handled collection (and remittance where applicable)

How to set tax expectations with your backers

When you’re running a campaign, transparency is key, especially when it comes to how and when your backers will be charged. One area that can sometimes catch backers by surprise is sales tax or VAT. To build trust and avoid confusion down the line, we recommend proactively communicating how taxes will be handled before your campaign ends.

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager makes it simple for you to collect any required taxes after your campaign ends, based on where your backers live and what they’ve chosen to receive.It is also important to let your backers know that they may be charged customs duties. These fees will be collected by customs authorities at the point of importation, not at checkout by Kickstarter.

Below are a few places you can include this information to help set clear expectations:

In your campaign FAQ

  • “Will I be charged sales tax?”
    • Yes, applicable sales tax or VAT will be collected when you complete your backer survey in Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager after the campaign ends. This ensures you pay only what’s legally required based on your shipping location and reward selection. However, keep in mind that depending on your location, you may also be subject to import duties charged by customs at the point of delivery. These are separate from the taxes collected by Kickstarter.

In your campaign updates

  • Consider adding something like: “A quick note about taxes: We’re using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager. If you are a backer in the United States, Canada, United Kingdom, or European Union, any required sales tax or VAT will be collected when you fill out your post-campaign survey. This ensures you’re only charged the exact tax amount required for your location and keeps us compliant with tax laws worldwide.”

In your reward descriptions

  • You can put a brief mention of the tax and shipping expectations in the bottom of your rewards description, for example:
    • SHIPPING & TAXES: Shipping and taxes are NOT included. You’ll pay those when your reward is ready to ship. (more info in the Shipping & Taxes section)
    • IMPORT DUTIES: Depending on your location, you may be charged an import fee by customs authorities at the point of importation. If you are based in the EU, import duties may apply at the point of delivery from 1 July, 2026. These are not collected by Kickstarter.

Dedicated section in the campaign story

You can make a dedicated section with “Shipping, Taxes, Tariffs and Duties” (or any collection of variable cost information) on your campaign page (usually near the bottom). Here is an example of shipping guidance creators provide in a section like this:

Pledge Manager Backer FAQs

Can I change my address in the Pledge Manager?

If shipping is being collected through the pledge manager, backers will be able to change their shipping country when providing their address, provided the country they are looking to change to is one that has been set up as a shipping zone. Please note that it is only possible to change a backer’s shipping country before they complete the pledge manager. After this point a backer can only update their address to one in the same country until the creator locks addresses.

Once a creator has locked addresses, backers are no longer able to edit their shipping address themselves. If an address change is needed, contact the creator to request an update. Note that creators can update your address on their end, but they cannot change the shipping country.

Why do I have to provide my address for some digital rewards?

Most reward items will be taxable in the pledge manager, especially if they are downloadable items. An address is required for any taxable reward so that tax can be calculated accurately.

We’ve listed some examples of digital rewards where tax should be collected below.

  • Downloadable content, such as a song, album, podcast, film, or game
  • Steam key or access to an online game
  • Digital artwork
  • A pdf of a book or zine

A digital thank you, such as an eCard, may be subject to tax, depending on a backer’s location. An acknowledgment where nothing is sent, such as a name on a thank you page, is generally not taxable.

Why am I being asked to pay taxes for my pledge?

Kickstarter operates as a marketplace facilitator. This means we are responsible for collecting and remitting sales tax for items shipped to all 50 US states, as well as collecting VAT (value-added tax) on items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK.

Creators using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager provided tax information for their reward items, and let us know where the rewards will be shipping from. This information enables Kickstarter to determine how much tax each backer is responsible to pay to ensure compliance with applicable tax regulations.

Why is there a tariff surcharge on my order?

To help creators cover additional costs as a result of the U.S. import tariffs, we’ve made it possible for creators to add a tariff surcharge to their pledge manager. By being able to offset rising costs, we hope to make the fulfillment stage as easy as possible for creators, while encouraging them to be proactive and transparent with backers. If you have any questions for the creator about this surcharge we recommend reaching out to them directly.

Please keep in mind that during this stage of their project a creator may be overwhelmed. We ask that you remain supportive, understanding, and patient while they work to bring their creative project to life. Remember, there is a human being behind every project.

What is a pledge manager and how do I complete it?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager provides creators with an easy tool to gather all the information they need in order to fulfill your reward. Using the pledge manager, a creator can collect your shipping address, reward preferences, as well as any applicable taxes and shipping fees, all in one place. It’s also a chance for you to finalize your reward and add-on selections before fulfillment begins.

When a creator launches the pledge manager you’ll receive an email with a direct link to finalize your pledge. If you’re viewing the site through a desktop browser, you’ll also see a banner at the top of the page for any pledge managers awaiting your response. To complete the pledge manager for a project, simply follow the steps below.

  • First, make sure that you are logged in to the correct Kickstarter account. Then, in the email you received, click the “Complete last steps” button to open the pledge manager.
  • On the ‘Welcome’ page, read the message the creator has written and click “Continue to pledge manager” to start submitting your information.
  • Next, you will see the reward tier you initially selected, if you backed the campaign while live or through late pledging. If the creator has configured any rewards to be available as an upgrade, you’ll see the option here to change to one of a higher tier, if any are available.
  • Note: If you pledged any amount for no reward during the campaign, this amount can only applied toward a reward in the pledge manager if the creator has made a reward tier available.
  • If the creator has made additional add-ons available in the pledge manager, the next page you’ll land on will be ‘Add-ons’. Here you’ll have the option to increase your pledge with any available add-ons. Simply click “Add” and adjust the quantity as needed.
    • Please note: When a reward tier is a digital one, it won’t be possible to add physical add-ons to your pledge.
    • If no add-ons are being offered by the creator, this page will not appear in the pledge manager.
  • When ready, click “Continue” and you’ll be taken to the ‘Survey’ page. Here, you can answer any questions from the creator and submit your item preferences. If you upgraded your reward or added any add-ons, the survey will automatically include questions relating to those rewards. When this section is complete, click “Continue”.
  • Next, you’ll need to confirm your address. This should be the address you would like the creator to ship your reward to. We will also use this address to determine how much tax may be due for your reward items.
    • If the creator is collecting shipping through the pledge manager, and you need to change the country your reward will be shipped to, you can do so here, provided the new country is included in the creator’s shipping zones.
  • After clicking “Continue” on the ‘Address’ page, you’ll be taken to the ‘Payment’ page. We recommend double-checking that everything is correct on this page. If there is a payment due, you can select a payment method and click “Pay” to finalize your pledge. If no payment is due, simply click “Complete” and you’re all set!

Remember: The creator will close the pledge manager when they are ready to move on to the next phase of their project. If there are any pledges that have not been completed in the pledge manager, these will become bonus support.

You will receive notifications reminding you once a week while the pledge manager is open. Make sure to complete this before they close it. After the pledge manager has been closed you will need to work directly with the creator to see if an alternative arrangement can be made.

Pledge Manager Troubleshooting

If you’re having trouble accessing the Kickstarter Pledge Manager through the email link or notification banner, another option is to access it via your Backed projects page.

  • Log in to your Kickstarter account and navigate to the Backed projects page.
  • Next, locate the specific project under the ‘Successful pledges’ section and click the round “+” button to the right-hand side of the project name.
  • On the Pledge summary page, select the ‘Pledge manager’ tab and click “Start pledge manager” to be taken to the pledge manager.
  • Follow the steps above to finalize your pledge and submit your response.

Pledge Manager Tax FAQ

What are the tax implications of using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager operates as a marketplace facilitator. We are responsible for collecting and remitting sales and tax for items shipped to all 50 US states and Canada, as well as collecting VAT (value-added tax) on items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK.

Separate from any sales and use tax/VAT, you may owe (and have the responsibility to report) national or subnational income, franchise, or other taxes. How much you owe can vary based on a number of factors, including the deduction of project-related expenses.

We highly recommend talking to an accountant or tax advisor to ensure you are compliant with applicable tax regulations. They can guide you through your particular tax scenario in the most advantageous way possible.

While we can’t offer tax advice, we have compiled a guide for financial professionals who may not be familiar with Kickstarter, which is intended to help such professionals understand some of the tax responsibilities you may be responsible for. Such guide includes coverage of sales and use tax/VAT responsibilities of creators that do not use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager.

How is tax collection managed in the US?

Kickstarter will automatically calculate, collect, and remit the applicable sales tax on taxable rewards. As a creator, you don’t need to manage the tax collection for US backers. However, you will be required to properly identify, and provide the cost of, the items that make up your rewards and add-ons. The cost you provide, in combination with the reward and add-on price, will be used by Kickstarter to calculate the amount of sales tax to collect from backers.

There is additional detail on US tax collection here.

How is tax collection managed in Canada?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager will collect and remit applicable Canadian indirect taxes on taxable rewards shipped to Canadian backers. As a creator, you will not need to collect these taxes yourself. However, it is your responsibility to provide complete and accurate information about each item’s value and category. This data will be used to determine the tax to charge each backer.

There is additional detail on Canada tax collection here.

How will Kickstarter approach VAT collection in the pledge manager?

As tax liability varies from country to country, providing rewards to a backer based in a specific region might create a VAT liability for you. For creators using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager, we will collect VAT (value-added tax) on items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK, when Kickstarter is the deemed supplier and, as authorized by the creator, when the creator is the responsible party.

Creators may also choose to opt out of Kickstarter’s VAT collection service, in which case Kickstarter will not collect or remit VAT on their behalf and the creator will remain fully responsible for VAT compliance.

We have additional information about VAT collection here.

Are creators based in or shipping to the EU & UK responsible for VAT?

As tax liability varies from country to country, providing rewards to a backer based in a specific region might create a VAT liability for you.

Creators may choose to opt out of Kickstarter’s VAT collection service. In these cases, Kickstarter will not calculate, collect, or remit VAT, and the creator is fully responsible for VAT compliance, including collection, remittance, and any import or customs obligations.

For creators using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool, we will collect VAT (value-added tax) on items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK, when Kickstarter is the deemed supplier and, as authorized by the creator, when the creator is the responsible party. You can read more about this here.

In some cases, as a deemed supplier, Kickstarter will be responsible for remitting VAT collected on the creator’s transactions to the relevant tax regulator. In other cases, creators will be responsible for remitting VAT, and any funds collected on Kickstarter toward value-added tax will be disbursed to the creator. Creators will be provided with tax reporting that outlines the responsible party for each VAT transaction.

VAT will be determined by using a combination of the creator’s VAT registration, business location, the shipping origin and destination, and item-based tax codes.

Please note that Kickstarter does not collect VAT for creators who choose not to use Kickstarter’s pledge manager tool. In these cases, ensuring that VAT is correctly accounted for on these transactions is the responsibility of each individual project creator.

Will Kickstarter collect EU import duties from EU-based backers?

No, the EU customs duties and national fees are entirely separate from VAT and are not collected or remitted by Kickstarter. You can read more about the EU import duty changes here.

Setting up IOSS for EU shipments via ShipStation

If you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager and ShipStation to manage the next stage of your project, you can find the steps below for setting up IOSS for your EU shipments.

As a first step, you will need to make sure that your account is on the new ShipStation layout. When you log in to ShipStation, if you see “New Layout. Try Now” at the top of the page, you will need to move to the new layout to use the settings referenced below.

Step 1: Configure IOSS number in International Settings

  • Navigate to Settings > Shipping > International Settings
  • Click on “Add a Tax Identifier”
  • Add the IOSS number provided by Kickstarter. This can be found in your downloaded backer report, under the ‘Transaction VAT ID’ column. We recommend giving the tax identifier a suitable nickname (e.g. Kickstarter IOSS)

Step 2: Use Automation Rules to apply IOSS number to applicable shipments

You can use any criteria for the automation rule, but in the below example we’re going to use the Store, on the assumption that all Kickstarter orders come through a unique Store in ShipStation.

  • Navigate to Settings > Automation > Automation Rules
  • Click on “+ Create a Rule”
  • Set a criteria (in our example a specific ShipStation store) and set the Action Type to ‘Set tax identifiers’ and ‘Bill International Duties to Payor of Shipping Charges’

Step 3: ShipStation Guaranteed Prepaid Duties and Taxes

At time of writing, this only applies to ShipStation accounts in the US or Canada. ShipStation released a feature to prepay for duties and taxes when using ShipStation Carrier accounts (e.g. UPS from ShipStation, FedEx from ShipStation & DHL Express from ShipStation).

To see if you have this feature enabled, navigate to Settings > Shipping > International Settings. If you have enabled this feature, ShipStation will automatically set all international orders to be DDP. If you want more granular control (i.e. only apply DDP to specific orders), then untick this option and use Automation Rules to apply DDP where applicable.

Until June 30, 2026, if using ShipStation Carriers with Guaranteed Prepaid Duties and Taxes to ship Kickstarter orders to the European Union under 150 euros, ShipStation will not charge any additional duties or taxes. For orders over 150 euros, ShipStation will charge duties and taxes at label print (this is in addition to the VAT collected by Kickstarter at checkout).

From July 1, 2026, de minimis will no longer apply so all orders shipped to the European Union on ShipStation Carriers with Guaranteed Prepaid Duties and Taxes will be charged for import duties and taxes at label print. Other charges may apply on a carrier by carrier basis; this is yet to be determined.

If shipping on your own carrier accounts, payment of duties and taxes will need to be handled by the shipper (if Delivery Duty Paid) or by the end customer (if DAP: Delivered at Place). This is not covered by the Guaranteed Prepaid Duties and Taxes solution provided by ShipStation
