Understanding Tax Collection in Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager

This guide outlines where Kickstarter automatically collects and remits taxes on your behalf through the Pledge Manager, versus where creators are responsible. For scenarios where creators remain responsible, Kickstarter offers an optional tax collection service to simplify compliance. Creators may also choose to opt out of VAT collection entirely, in which case all VAT obligations remain with the creator and no VAT will be collected through Kickstarter.

How Tax Responsibility is Determined

Tax collection and remittance responsibility related to the supply of goods depends on several factors working together:

  • Backer ship-to location - Where your item is being delivered
  • Creator location - Where your business is registered
  • Product ship-from location - Where items are fulfilled from
  • Product value - With specific thresholds (€150 for EU, £135 for UK)

When Kickstarter Handles Tax Collection and Remittance

The Pledge Manager automatically collects and remits taxes where the platform has an obligation to collect and remit transaction tax. This covers all backer transactions in the U.S and Canada, and any across the UK and EU where we are the deemed supplier.

These obligations are outlined under the following laws and regulations in different regions:

  • U.S.: Marketplace facilitator laws
  • Canada: Digital platform operator GST/HST obligations, and local provincial goods and services tax
  • UK & EU: Deemed supplier regulations

In these cases:

  • No additional action required from creators for tax compliance
  • Automatic collection & remittance of appropriate tax amounts
  • Built-in coverage that handles collection and government remittance seamlessly
  • The Pledge Manager will provide tax reporting detailing tax collected on your transactions for any information returns that may be required by local tax authorities or where you otherwise have tax obligations.

When Creators Remain Responsible

For scenarios where tax laws designate creators or backers as the responsible party, creators maintain tax obligations. To support compliance, Kickstarter offers an optional additional tax collection service (that you can opt-in to in the Pledge Manager set-up) where:

  • Any creator-responsible taxes will be collected on applicable purchases made in the Pledge Manager
  • Taxes are disbursed to your Stripe Connected Account along with your other Pledge Manager funds
  • You handle the remittance to relevant tax authorities
  • Once you have closed your Pledge Manager, Kickstarter will provide tax reporting detailing tax collected on your transaction denoting that tax is collected and the creator is the Tax Responsible Party to support your filing activity.

Understanding Your Specific Scenarios

The table below shows who is responsible for tax collection and remittance based on your specific combination of backer location, creator location, product shipping location, and product value. This will help you understand what to expect for each type of transaction in your campaign.

  • How to read your Pledge Manager Tax Report

When will I receive the funds from Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager?

Funds collected in the Kickstarter Pledge Manager will be paid out every 2 weeks (14 calendar days), starting 3 weeks (21 calendar days) after your pledge manager launches. Each payout reflects a 2 week period of collected funds, with a 1 week processing period.

Your pledge manager must be open for a minimum of 3 weeks, at which point a button will become available for you to close it for your project. After it has been closed, any remaining funds will be processed and the final payout will begin initiating 2 weeks from the date your pledge manager closed.

For example, if your pledge manager launches on January 1, the first payout will begin initiating on January 22, for the period January 1-15. If you were to close the pledge manager on January 31, your last payout would initiate on February 14. Please note that when a payout is initiated on a non-business day your financial institution may not show it as processing until the next business day.

After a payout has been processed, and 48 hours after the funds are cleared by your bank, you can view each payout by selecting ‘Payouts & Taxes’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page.

Similar to all other project payouts, please allow up to a week after a payout is initiated for the funds to appear in your account.

​Where can I find the amount of tax collected for each Kickstarter Pledge Manager payout?

You’ll be able to find this information by clicking on a Pledge Manager payout, located on the ‘Payouts & Taxes’ page. Here you will see the breakdown of funds (shipping and taxes) and deductions (Kickstarter fee, payment processing fee, taxes to be remitted by Kickstarter) for that payout. To access an invoice from this view click the “View invoice” button at the top and print the web page.

Launching your project

Can I get my project’s URL before I launch?

Yes! After your project has been submitted for review, you will have the option to generate your project’s URL on the Promotion tab of your project editor. After clicking the “Generate my URL” button, you will be asked to confirm your project’s URL. Once confirmed you can share this with potential backers ahead of launch.

Please note: If you have activated the Pre-launch page for your project, the generated URL will take backers to this page. Once launched, this same URL will lead people to your project page. If you didn’t generate your URL before launching you can find your project’s URL in the search bar of your web browser.

It’s also important to note that your project’s URL will be generated based on your project’s title. If there are any keywords that you feel are important to have in the URL, make sure to include these in the title before generating the URL.

Can I customize my project URL?

Here’s an example of what a project URL generally looks like:

​kickstarter.com]/[yourProjectTitle]

The [yourVanityURL] part of the URL will show your vanity URL. If you haven’t set a vanity URL, then your user ID will take its place (it will look like a string of numbers). You can read more about how to set a vanity URL here.

The [yourProjectTitle] part of the URL will be each word of the project title separated by a hyphen. For example, if the title is “Your Project Title”, the end of your project’s URL will look like “/your-project-title”.

If you need to change the URL after generating it, our Support team can help. Just reach out to us here.

Please keep in mind that resetting the vanity URL, or changing the project URL, will break any links you’ve already shared, so you will need to share the new/updated URL with your network of followers again.

Does Kickstarter curate which projects are allowed on the site?

Absolutely not. It doesn’t matter if we don’t think a project will succeed, or if someone on the Kickstarter staff doesn’t like it. We would never reject a polka album just because we don’t like polka. (Note: We have no strong opinion on polka.) However, we do expect all projects to adhere to Our Rules and Community Guidelines.

How do I launch my project after it’s been approved?

After your project has been approved, you can go ahead and launch it whenever you’re ready by following the steps below:

  • Click on the “I’m ready to launch” button.
  • Review the details on the following page and make sure you’ve read our Terms of Use, Privacy Policy, and Kickstarter Rules.
  • Once you’ve read them, confirm this by clicking the checkbox.
  • After you’ve double-checked that everything is in order and you’re ready to launch, click on the “Launch project” button.
  • Congratulations, your project is now live! You’ll see a confirmation page and will receive an email confirming that your project has launched.

Please note: Launching a project is a manual process, it isn’t possible to schedule an automatic launch for a predetermined date. After clicking the “Launch project” button your project will be live.

If your project has been approved but you’re not ready to launch it just yet, you can activate your pre-launch page in the meantime by going to the Promotion tab of your project editor.

How do I prepare to launch my project?

Before you can launch your project you’ll first need to submit it for review, you can do this by simply clicking the “Send to review” button. Your project may then be auto-approved, or sent for a manual review.

If your project is auto-approved, an “I’m ready to launch” button will appear straight away. If your project is not auto-approved, our Trust & Safety team will need to manually review it first.

After a project has been reviewed and approved, creators will have the flexibility to launch whenever they’re ready.

Which projects are auto-approved?

We use an algorithm incorporating thousands of data points to check whether a project can be auto-approved — things like the project’s category, description, rewards, funding goal, and whether the creator has previously launched a project.

The algorithm analyzes the characteristics of an individual project, and compares them to how we’ve handled similar projects in the past.

What happens if a project isn’t auto-approved?

If your project isn’t auto-approved, it will be sent to our in-house Trust & Safety team for a manual review. They will make sure the project is in line with Our Rules, and verify that it’s a project with a specific, finite goal. This process can take up to 3 business days, or longer if additional revisions are needed.

Please note that all Design & Technology projects are subject to manual review.

If your project has been submitted and is awaiting a review, you can find more information here.

How long does it take to build and launch a project?

The amount of time it takes to build a project page may vary depending on your project’s scope and size. You can take as long as you need to build your project, and even save it as a draft to revisit again at a later time. Keep in mind that all tabs of your project editor need to be fully completed before you will be able to send it for review.

Once you’ve finished building your project and have submitted it for review, you will need to wait for it to be approved by our Trust & Safety team. After it’s been approved, you can go ahead and launch whenever you’re ready.

​How do I submit my project for review?

Upon finishing your project draft, and provided all tabs show as completed, you’ll be able to submit for review by clicking the “Send to review” button. If you’re not seeing this option, it’s likely that one or more of the tabs requires some further editing. As a first step, check that everything is in order with your project end date and any set time limits for rewards.

If the People tab is showing as incomplete, it’s possible that there is information missing from your profile. To complete your profile, make sure that you have a profile name, location, biography, and an avatar added.

​Can I edit my project after I’ve submitted it for review?

You’re free to make edits to your project after it’s been submitted for review. In some instances, our Trust & Safety team may even suggest some changes are made following your first review.

You can also continue to edit your project after it’s been approved, provided any changes made are in compliance with our guidelines. Remember, after your project has launched, there are only a few areas that you can continue to make changes to.

My project is awaiting review, how does this work?

Before a project can launch it may need to be reviewed by our in-house Trust & Safety team. They will make sure that the project is in line with Our Rules, and verify that it’s a project with a specific, finite goal. Additionally, they may offer helpful feedback on ways to structure or present your project. If your project is queued for review this process can take up to 3 business days.

Our team may request that changes be made to a project draft, in which case it can take an additional 3 business days, or longer if additional revisions are needed, to review the changes after you’ve responded to our request.

Please keep in mind that during periods of high volume project submissions our turnaround time may be slightly longer than usual. In light of this, we recommend planning ahead and building in extra buffer time when scheduling your project’s launch, to ensure you’re ready to go on your Target Launch Date.

What is Target Launch Date?

Target Launch Date is an optional section that you can fill in on the Basics section of the project editor. To use it, simply enter the date that you plan to launch and we’ll recommend when you should confirm your identity, provide payment details, and submit your project for review by.

Don’t worry, setting a Target Launch Date won’t automatically launch your project!

I’m a repeat creator, why has my latest project not been auto-approved?

We use an algorithm incorporating thousands of data points to check whether a project is suitable for auto-approval. While a previous project may have been auto-approved, the algorithm analyzes the characteristics of each individual project, and therefore there is no guarantee that future projects will encounter the same result.

Please note that all Design & Technology projects will be subject to a manual review.

Pledge Over Time FAQ

Important Note: For projects that received their first Pledge Over Time payout before January 15, 2026, the funds will still be sent in two payouts. The funds from the next 2 charges will still be combined and sent approximately 12 weeks after the campaign deadline.

What is Pledge Over Time?

Pledge Over Time is a flexible payment option that allows backers to split their pledge into 3 payments, making it easier for them to support your campaign. By reducing the upfront cost, Pledge Over Time can help to attract more backers and encourage pledges to higher tiers by making premium rewards more accessible.

There are no additional fees, third-party signups, or interest-bearing loans—just a seamless way to offer backers more flexibility while keeping the pledge process simple and accessible.

You can find the answers to common Pledge Over Time questions from backers here.

Important considerations before enabling Pledge Over Time on your project:

  • Your first campaign payout will only include the 1st charge of your Pledge Over Time backers’ pledges. The remaining Pledge Over Time charges are collected over time and paid out in two additional payouts, approximately eight weeks and twelve weeks after your project ends. Please note: This only applies to projects that receive their first Pledge Over Time payout on or after January 15, 2026.
  • Pledge Over Time backers will not be able to access your project’s Pledge Manager until they have completed all 3 charges of their payment plan.
  • Pledge Over Time is currently unavailable for Late Pledges.

​How does Pledge Over Time work?

Pledge Over Time allows backers to split their pledge into 3 equal payments. The first charge will occur when your project ends successfully. The remaining 2 payments are then collected automatically each month until the pledge total is fully paid.

The first collected payment will be paid out in a separate payout, close to the time that your project’s first payout initiates. The second and third payments are paid out in two additional payouts, approximately eight weeks and twelve weeks after your project ends. Please note: This only applies to projects that receive their first Pledge Over Time payout on or after January 15, 2026.

​How do I enable Pledge Over Time for my campaign?

You can choose to enable Pledge Over Time for your campaign on the Basics tab of the project editor, before you launch your campaign. If enabled, backers who pledge the minimum amount, or higher, in your project’s currency, will be able to select to split the payment for their pledge.

​What is the benefit of Pledge Over Time and how will it work for my backers?

Pledge Over Time can reduce the cost barrier to pledging and attract backers who prefer payment flexibility. By allowing backers to split payments on premium rewards this could potentially raise your average pledge amount. There are no additional fees for using Pledge Over Time and no 3rd party signups or interest-bearing loans.

For projects with Pledge Over Time enabled, after a backer has selected their reward and proceeded to the checkout page, they will see the option to “Pledge in full” or “Pledge Over Time”. If they select “Pledge in full” the total amount of their pledge will be collected when the campaign ends successfully.

For backers who select “Pledge Over Time” they will be shown a payment schedule, what is due at the successful completion of your campaign, and what they will be charged over the next 2 collected payments. Backers can view the status of their Pledge Over Time pledge at any time by viewing the pledge summary for your project on their Backed projects page.

​What’s the criteria for a pledge to be eligible for Pledge Over Time?

Backers can access Pledge Over Time if their pledge total is the minimum amount, or higher, that is required for your project’s currency. If their total pledge value is below the minimum, and the campaign is enrolled in Pledge Over Time, the option to “Pledge Over Time” can not be selected. The backer will be shown a tooltip letting them know that Pledge Over Time is only available for pledges above a specified amount.

Below you can see which currencies we currently support for Pledge Over Time, along with the minimal pledge amount required to use this payment option.

​How can I see which backers have selected Pledge Over Time?

On your backer report page, you’ll be able to select ‘Pledge Over Time’ under either the ‘All backers’ or ‘Payment Option’ drop-down (your backer report version will depend on whether you’re using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager).

​How will I be paid?

Pledge Over Time allows backers to complete their pledge in three payments spread across three months. The first payment is collected when your project ends successfully. The second payment is collected one month after your project ends, and the third payment is collected two months after your project ends.

Each payment is paid out to you separately. The first payment is paid out around the time your project’s first payout is initiated (approximately 14 days after your campaign ends). The second and third payments are paid out in two additional payouts, approximately eight weeks and twelve weeks after your project ends. Please note: This only applies to projects that receive their first Pledge Over Time payout on or after January 15, 2026.

​What happens if a backer’s payment fails for one of the payments?

If a payment fails, Kickstarter will notify the backer that they need to fix their payment within 7 days for charge 1, and 14 days for charges 2 and 3. If the backer is not able to fix a payment in time their pledge will be dropped and any payments collected may be considered bonus support. The refund of any successfully collected charges will be at each creator’s discretion.

​How do I issue a refund for Pledge Over Time?

If you wish to issue a refund for a backer who selected to pay using Pledge Over Time, you can do so via your backer report. A refund can be issued at any point after your campaign has ended successfully.

For backers who still have upcoming charges for their pledge, you can choose to offer a full or partial refund. To issue a full refund and cancel any scheduled payments you will simply proceed with issuing a full refund.

If you plan to issue a partial refund, the amount you refund will determine if any scheduled charges are also canceled. We’ve outlined some scenarios below to help:

  • To issue a partial refund before all charges are collected and cancel any scheduled payments, you will need to add the amount that is yet to be collected to how much you plan to refund from what has been paid so far.
    • For example: If $100 of a $150 pledge has been collected, and you plan to withhold $10, you will enter $140 as the amount to refund. This ensures that the final $50 charge is canceled and $90 is returned to the backer.
  • To issue a partial refund before all charges are collected without canceling any scheduled payments, you will enter the amount you wish to refund and, provided it is less than the upcoming charges, these will not be canceled.
    • For example: If $100 of a $150 pledge has been collected, and you plan to refund $35, you will enter $35 as the amount to refund. This will then adjust the next scheduled payment to $15 to reflect the refund.

It’s important to note that any Pledge Over Time refunds will be issued from the source card you added to your project on the Payment tab or the new card added during the refund flow, rather than from your campaign or Pledge Over Time funds collected, regardless of payout status.

If you encounter any problems with issuing a refund to one of your Pledge Over Time backers, please contact our Support team.

What are the rules for hardware and product design projects?

We expect creators on Kickstarter to bring an exceptional level of honesty, openness, and candor to both how they present their ideas and how they run their campaigns.

That’s why, in addition to following our rules, hardware and product design creators are required to show working prototypes of their projects. We do not allow photorealistic renderings. We also ask creators in these categories to clearly detail for their backers how they’ll produce their product, and whether they’ve produced anything like it before.

For creators planning to run a project within the hardware and product design categories, we recommend taking some time to check out the below Kickstarter-created resources:

Prototyping guide

​Read our rules and best practices for visually presenting your project and showing backers the development of your product, both honestly and transparently.

Kickstarter Creator Tips: Design & Technology

​Resources to guide you through planning, promoting, launching and fulfilling your campaign.

Design creator basics

​Design creators share their advice on setting your funding goal, planning rewards, telling your story, building your community, and anticipating obstacles and delays.

Environmental Resources Center

​Start thinking about sustainability from the get-go with this guide to reducing the environmental impact of your product.

And lastly, take a moment to explore our “I’m looking to run a Design & Technology project” article, for creator resources, campaign tips, and project inspiration.

What should projects do if they don’t meet Kickstarter’s rules?

If for any reason your project does not meet our rules, feel free to refine and improve it. Check out our Creator Handbook and Our Rules for tips. We also suggest browsing live projects on the site, for inspiration.

Otherwise, there are hundreds of general crowdfunding sites out there.

Why do creators need to add a credit card when setting up their project?

When completing the Payment tab of your project editor, you will be required to add a Payment source. The Payment source must be a Visa, Mastercard, or American Express credit or debit card. Other card types such as Discover, JCB, Maestro, and Visa Electron cannot be used.

A credit or debit card is necessary in the rare case that a backer disputes the charge for their pledge with their card issuer. This card must be registered to the individual or entity (or entity’s owner) raising funds for this project, and must be in their name.

​Can I change the credit card I added?

Yes, but only after your project has ended successfully. After adding a Payment source this section will be locked until after a project ends and is successfully funded, at which point you can update this card in the Disputes section of your Account settings.

Why does Kickstarter need to know about my company’s owners? What information do I need to provide?

In certain countries, Kickstarter is required to collect, verify, and maintain information on a company’s owners — known as “additional owners”. These requirements come from our payments processor, Stripe, and regulators. This information promotes transparency, and prevents individuals from using complex company structures to hide terrorist financing, money laundering, tax evasion, and other financial crimes.

Why review projects at all?

Every site has rules for how it can be used. While many sites wait until after something is posted to apply their rules — commonly through user reporting or algorithmic scan — we apply ours before projects launch. (We have user reporting and algorithm scans too, of course.) We take our review process seriously — because we have to. It’s a big deal for a project to be taken down after it’s launched – much bigger than taking down a YouTube video.

Inevitably, any line we draw might leave out some great projects. It’s a bummer, but we’re trying to make the best possible tool for funding and building a community around creative projects, not the biggest possible funding site.

If you’re unsure whether your idea would make sense for Kickstarter, get in touch with us.

Managing pledges

Can backers update their shipping address?

For creators using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager or Backer Survey, backers will have the option to update the address they submitted, until addresses are locked.

When you’re ready to start shipping rewards you can lock addresses on the ‘Pledge manager’ page or ‘Backer survey’ page. After addresses are locked we will alert backers that they have 48 hours to make any last minute changes to their address. At the end of this 48 hours, any backers who have submitted their pledge manager or survey will no longer be able to change their address.

​

​

Pledge Manager

Backers will only be able to make changes to their address after completing the pledge manager and if the creator hasn’t yet locked addresses. If a backer needs to adjust their answer to any other details within the pledge manager, such as survey questions (for example, reward size or color), and you are not currently allowing for edits to survey responses, they will need to contact you directly and any important edits can be left as a note in your backer report.

Please note that once the pledge manager has been closed, any backers yet to submit their address will no longer be able to do so. In the event that a backer doesn’t respond in time, their pledge amount will become bonus support and you will need to work with them to see if an alternative arrangement can be made.

Can backers change their shipping country through the Kickstarter Pledge Manager?

If shipping is being collected through the pledge manager, backers will be able to change their shipping country when providing their address, provided the country they are looking to change to is one that has been set up as a shipping zone. Please note that it is only possible to change a backer’s shipping country before they complete the pledge manager. After this point a backer can only update their address to one in the same country until the creator locks addresses.

​Backer Survey only

Backers will only be able to make changes to their address after submitting their survey response. If they need to adjust their answer to any other survey questions (for example, reward size or color), they will need to contact you directly and any important edits can be left as a note in your backer report.

Please note that locking addresses for the survey does not stop any backers who are yet to submit their survey response from still doing so. As such, we recommend waiting until all backers have completed their survey before locking addresses.

If I am not using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, can backers change their shipping country?

Unfortunately, no. Backers cannot change their shipping country through Kickstarter after the project has ended if the creator is only using the survey tool or a third-party service. If the incorrect shipping fee was collected due to the wrong country/region being selected, you will need to work with the backer directly to find a solution outside of Kickstarter.

As the creator, can I edit a backer’s shipping address in the backer report?

Yes! If a backer’s shipping address needs to be edited, and addresses have already been locked, you can do this directly through the project’s backer report. Simply click the three dots under the gear icon for a backer and select “Edit address” from the actions available.

How can I access my expired backer report?

Kickstarter is dedicated to protecting the privacy of our users’ information, as such, a creator’s access to their backer report expires two years from the project’s latest estimated delivery date. For example, if a project has three reward tiers, with estimated delivery dates of July 2020, August 2020 and October 2020, access to that project’s backer report will expire September 30, 2022.

At the expiration date, a creator will no longer be able to automatically download and export their backer report in full, nor will they be able to view any of their backer’s personally identifiable information (or PII), such as email address or survey responses.

If access to your backer report has expired, you will need to request access by filling out the “Submit a ticket” form, and following the steps below. A member of our Trust & Safety Team will then review the details of your request and grant access accordingly.

  • Enter your Kickstarter account email address (Note: this must be the same email address used on the account tied to your Kickstarter project)
  • Choose “I am a current or past creator”, then select “Accessing my expired backer report”
  • Copy and paste the link of your project’s URL
  • Respond to the following questions:
    1. What is your full name?
    1. What is your role in this project (e.g., creator, collaborator)?
    1. What is your project’s fulfillment status at this time? Approximately what percentage of rewards have you already sent to your backers?
    1. Why are you requesting access to your backer report at this time?
  • Which of the following documents can you provide with this form?
    1. Passport
    1. Driver’s license
    1. Other government issued identity document (e.g., birth certificate, SIN or City ID card)
    1. I don’t have any of these, but still require access to my backer report for fulfilling rewards
  • Attach a copy of your proof of identity to the ticket

As a reminder, we expect creators to adhere to all of the following guidelines when handling their backers’ personal information:

  • I will only use this information to fulfill the rewards in this Kickstarter project and nothing else. I will never abuse, sell or share details from the backer report.
  • I will only share this information with my collaborators when it’s absolutely necessary to fulfill this project’s rewards. I understand that I’m accountable for their actions.
  • I will never download or view the backer report on a shared computer, and I will delete the file once I’ve delivered all of the rewards in my project.

Avoid requesting access to your entire backer report if you only need information from a few individual backers. You can contact them directly using our messaging platform.

Take a look at these additional tips for communicating with your backers.

How can I get my backers’ information to fulfill rewards?

Kickstarter has tools that can help you to collect all the information you need in order to fulfill your backers’ rewards. If your project is eligible to use our Pledge Manager, you can find a guide for how to set that up here.

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager is also home to the Backer Survey, which can be used to gather information such as mailing addresses and item variant preferences (e.g. size, color), as well as an easier way to collect any applicable shipping and taxes from your backers.

You can start building your backer survey at any time after your project has launched. However, you will have to wait until after your project has successfully ended and pledges have been collected before sending it. Your survey will also be sent to backers when you launch your project’s pledge manager.

For more information about tools to help you with fulfillment, please visit the Creator Handbook.

I’ve launched my Kickstarter Pledge Manager or Backer Survey, what should I do if there are backers who haven’t responded with their information?

If you are still waiting to receive information or shipping and taxes from some backers, and you have made attempts to contact them, we recommend moving forward with the next stage of your project.

When using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager or Backer Survey tool, we will email backers when this launches, followed by a regular reminder* email until this is closed, for anyone who hasn’t responded yet. For backers using the Kickstarter mobile apps, they will receive push notification reminders. Additionally, when a backer logs in to their Kickstarter account they will see a notification banner at the top of their screen alerting them that they still need to provide their information in the pledge manager or backer survey.

*A reminder email will be sent every 7 days for the pledge manager, and every 2 days for the backer survey.

You can also message backers that fall into a specific filtered view in your backer report, including backers that have not yet responded to the pledge manager or survey.

If a backer is unable to submit their response due to the pledge manager or survey being locked, you will need to work with them directly to see if an alternative arrangement can be made.

Some of my backers are using private relay email addresses, how can I collect their information?

When backers sign up to Kickstarter with their Apple ID and opt to hide their email address, you will only be able to see their private relay email address in your backer report at the end of the campaign. The unique private relay email address tied to their Kickstarter account can only be used for communication within Kickstarter. This means that any communication you may send outside of our platform – including via a third-party survey tool or pledge manager service – will unfortunately not reach their inbox.

If you need an alternative email address from these backers in order to fulfill your project, we recommend reaching out to them directly via the Kickstarter messaging system to request that information. To message backers individually, click on their name in your backer report and open the ‘Send message’ tab.

Once you obtain this information, you can update the email address for the backer by editing your backer export. It isn’t possible to change the email address on the platform, however you can leave a note to yourself in the backer report. Alternatively, you can also advise them to change their email address on Kickstarter. Doing so will update the information in your backer report. However, it’s important to note that this will disconnect their account from their Apple ID.

If you are using a third-party survey service, you will need to update the information there too, and re-send the survey link to the backer using their new email address.

By using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool, any backers with a private relay email address will receive the pledge manager emails sent by Kickstarter in their inbox, and will be able to submit their response by logging into their Kickstarter account.

How do I see how many backers have completed the Kickstarter Pledge Manager?

After launching your pledge manager, the ‘Pledge manager’ page will change to show you the current status. This will include the percentage of backers who have finalized their order, how many are still remaining, and how much has been collected for shipping, add-ons, and taxes.

If your backer report access has expired, you can request an extension by following a few simple steps.

How can I use the downloaded backer report?

After a project ends successfully you will have the option to download your backer’s data as a CSV file that can then be opened in Excel, Numbers, or any spreadsheet program. If you don’t have one yet, Google Drive is available to all, and has a free, compatible spreadsheet program. Check out their help section to get started.

The downloaded backer report will include each backer’s profile name, backer number, email address, and their pledge details, including their shipping country. If you used Kickstarter’s backer survey tool to collect information for fulfilling rewards, such as their shipping address and reward item preferences, this information will populate in the exported backer report too.

For creators using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool, the exported backer report will also include any additional items backers have added to their order, as well as any shipping and taxes they may have paid.

Please note: Data in the backer report is likely to change for 7 days after your campaign ends successfully, while we work to collect pledges. After that period concludes, the backer report will continue to change as backers submit their survey or pledge manager responses, edit their shipping address before this is locked, or if a backer is refunded.

The downloaded backer report allows you to organize and sort the information to meet your needs, you can:

  • Sort and reorder by backer number, rewards, add-ons, pledge amount, shipping country, date of pledge, etc. This makes it easy to count and view different rewards, such as how many signed copies of your book you’ll need, and so on.
  • Make changes to backer information. If you need to update their reward preferences, or something else outside of Kickstarter, make a note of it in the spreadsheet so that all the information is kept together. You can update addresses within the backer report.
  • Add additional fields for any other data you may want to store. For example, you could create fields to note when you have packed and shipped rewards.

These are just a few examples of what you can do with the exported backer report, but you can organize and edit it to work for you.

How will my backers’ information be displayed in the download?

The downloaded backer report will list each backer alongside their pledge details, with one backer per row. Column A will show you their backer number, and in column E you’ll see their shipping country.

Column F is the base reward tier the backer pledged for, with column O onwards listing any additional information collected. This may include any pledge management details, tax information, and add-ons that may have been added to their pledge. This will be followed by the items associated with each reward tier and add-on. An item could appear within multiple reward tiers, as well as in an add-on, so this is essentially checking off each item, and what quantity needs to be included with each pledge.

For example, a backer may have selected a base reward tier that included the item “Vinyl album”, and then also opted to add an extra two of this item as an add-on. As this backer’s pledge now includes three of the item “Vinyl album”, that number will be listed in the relevant item’s column on the backer export.

Survey response time stamps and shipping address, if collected, will be available after the add-on and item counts. Item customization questions will appear in a column for each SKU. All SKUs appear after the survey response columns for visibility into which SKUs each backer selected.

How can I use the Fulfillment dashboard to manage reward production and fulfillment?

The Fulfillment dashboard is a tool to help creators track and visualize the fulfillment status of their project after it successfully funds. This feature links to both the backer report and surveys where, as a creator, you can keep track of fulfillment signals like the number of backers, response rate for surveys, and the percentage of rewards that have shipped.

The Fulfillment dashboard also provides a custom overview of important logistical numbers relating to the fulfillment of your project, such as:

  • Number of pledges per country for international shipping
  • Number of backers per reward tier and add-on for budgeting
  • Reward item inventory breakdown for manufacturing
  • Downloadable reports for planning production and fulfillment

Additionally, if using Kickstarter’s Backer Survey or Pledge Manager tool, you’ll also see the automatically generated SKUs for your reward items, along with the count for each. This number will be final once all backers have submitted their response.

By placing this information side by side on the same dashboard, we hope that this will help you as a creator to get a better idea of your project’s overall fulfillment progress.

Pledge Breakdown

The pledge breakdown at the top of the Fulfillment dashboard can give you a clearer understanding of the final backer number for your campaign, and how this relates to fulfillment. You’ll see the total number of backers, alongside the number of pledges with rewards and the number of pledges without a reward.

If your campaign had any dropped pledges, a count will appear alongside the other pledge numbers. If there are no dropped pledges, this information will not be displayed.

Rewards Graphs

Rewards fulfillment completion rate

Fulfillment completion shows the percentage of rewards that you have sent to your backers and is directly tied to the ‘Status’ column in the backer report - which you must manage manually as you ship rewards. An up-to-date visualization will be generated as more pledges have their status changed to “Shipped”.

If you have already started to ship out rewards, but have not kept track of the ‘Status’ column on your backer report, the text below the visualization provides a link to the backer report where you can select specific pledges and use the “Quick status” button in the bottom right corner to mark them as “Shipped”.

Rewards & Fulfillment

The main overview of the Rewards & Fulfillment section shows the percentage of funds raised specifically for shipping. Here, shipping costs collected from pledges with a shipping option are taken into account. For example, if a backer pledges to a $50 reward tier with a $10 shipping fee ($60 total pledge), the $10 shipping fee would count towards the shipping funds that appear in this display.

All displays and charts in this section pull logistical reward data specified when you build out your rewards - shipping funds by country, by rewards tier, and itemization of the reward inventory. The more information included in your reward tiers, including itemization and costs, the more accurate these visualizations will be around fulfillment expenses.

Shipping destinations

This table shows you where your backers are located around the world, and can help you to begin to plan for international fulfillment by specifying collected shipping funds for each country. This feature uses the per-country shipping costs specified per-reward tier when you set up your project.

Please note: If you did not specify shipping fees on a per-country basis on your rewards, this table will be hidden from your Fulfillment dashboard.

Reward tiers

This table shows which reward tiers your backers selected, and provides a better snapshot of what you will need to plan for fulfillment by specifying the number of pledges made under each reward tier. This information is also available on the campaign page and backer report.

To get a summary of the number of rewards you need to produce and fulfill for each reward tier, click the “Full report” button* at the top of this section. The downloaded report will include the number of backers confirmed, partially refunded, and dropped (fully refunded), in addition to the reward type, name, reward tier description, including which items and how many are included in the reward tier.

Add-ons

This table provides information about add-on rewards, including the number of unique backers who selected the add-on and a total count (in the event that some backers added multiples of an add-on to their pledge). This table will only appear if you created add-ons for your project.

To get a summary of the number of rewards you need to produce and fulfill for each add-on, click the “Full report” button* at the top of this section. The downloaded report will include the number of backers confirmed, partially refunded, and dropped (fully refunded), in addition to the reward type, name, add-on description, including which items and how many are included in the add-on.

Reward items

This table helps you to plan how much inventory you’ll need to produce for each unit that will be shipped. This feature uses the reward data you provided in the reward itemization and quantity specifications when creating your rewards.

To get a summary of the number of reward items you need to produce and fulfill, click the “Full report” button* at the top of this section. The downloaded report will include the number of backers confirmed, partially refunded, and dropped (fully refunded) for each reward item.

*Please note: Data in these reports is likely to change for 7 days after your campaign ends successfully, while we work to collect pledges. After that period concludes the only changes would be if a backer is refunded by the creator, if Late Pledges are active, or if your project’s Pledge Manager is open.

SKUs

When using Kickstarter’s Backer Survey or Pledge Manager tools to collect item variant preferences from your backers, you will see a SKU table at the bottom of your Fulfillment dashboard. Here is where you can see all of the automatically generated SKUs for your reward items and how many backers have selected each specific SKU as part of their survey response.

The SKU count will continue to update as backers submit their survey or pledge manager response.

How do I track Backer Survey or Pledge Manager completion?

When using Kickstarter’s Backer Survey or Pledge Manager tools, you can see what percentage of your backers have completed this by selecting either ‘Pledge manager’ or ‘Backer survey’, from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page. You should aim to reach 100%.

If you’re ready to ship rewards, but still haven’t received all responses, you can message the backers who haven’t responded yet by using the “Not responded to survey” or “Pledge manager incomplete” filters in the backer report.

How do I issue a refund to one of my backers?

If you need to issue one of your backers with a refund, you can do so through your backer report. Your backer report can be accessed through the creator tools menu to the left of your project page, you can then issue a refund by following the below steps:

  • Search for the backer in the backer report and click on their name.
  • In the modal* that opens up, click the “Issue a refund” button.
  • On the next page you will have the option to issue a full or partial refund.
    • A full refund will result in the backer receiving 100% of their original pledge amount, including any fees.
    • When issuing a partial refund you can choose to enter a specific refund amount for each item within the pledge, or simply enter a general refund amount. You can also choose to fully remove an item from a pledge.
  • Select a reason for the refund and, if you’d like to, you can include a message to be sent to the backer within their refund email.
  • Next, choose a refund source from the cards saved to your account, or enter a new personal credit card (this will be the card that the refund is issued from).
  • Lastly, check all information is correct and click the issue refund button to confirm.

*If the backer has left a comment on your successfully funded project page, you will also be able to navigate to the modal by clicking on their name in the comment, followed by the “View pledge” button.

For refunds issued before your project’s payout occurs, Kickstarter directly refunds the backer our 5% fee + payment processing fees. You are responsible for covering the cost of fees for any refunds issued after the payout.

If a backer has been issued a full refund (100% of their pledge amount), it’ll be reflected in your backer report. Their status will be changed to “Dropped” and they’ll no longer be able to post comments, but you can continue to communicate with them through direct messages.

If a partial refund has been issued, the backer will remain on your backer report and will still be able to post comments on your project page. If you do not wish for the backer to gain access to your third-party pledge manager post-campaign, you will need to manually remove them from your downloaded backer report. We recommend creating a file with the names of all partially refunded backers so that you can easily find and remove them later as needed.

You can find information about refunding funds received through Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager here.

In instances where the backer’s original payment method has expired, or is no longer active, please refer to this article. If you receive an error message when trying to issue a refund, you may need to issue it outside of Kickstarter.

Please note: If a backer has an active dispute filed with their card issuer you will be unable to issue a refund through Kickstarter. We also recommend waiting until an outcome of the dispute has been reached before issuing a refund outside of the Kickstarter platform.

For help understanding disputed charges you can find more information here.

​How do I issue a refund for backers who selected to Pledge Over Time?

You can issue a refund for a Pledge Over Time backer at any point during their payment schedule. For guidance on issuing one of these refunds head to our Pledge Over Time FAQ article.

​How do I refund a Late Pledge?

For a Late Pledge refund issued before that particular pledge’s payout occurs, Kickstarter directly refunds the backer our 5% fee + payment processing fees. After the payout, you will be responsible for covering the cost of fees for any refunds issued.

​How do I refund a Pledge Manager order?

Any refunds of Pledge Manager orders (either pre- or post-payout of that particular pledge) will be refunded as a post-payout refund, and thus subject to Kickstarter fees. Applicable taxes to be refunded will be calculated according to the amount being refunded, and the tax obligor for the original transaction.

For example, if you issue a 50% refund to a backer whose order had taxes that were collected and remitted by Kickstarter, the funds from their main order items would be refunded from your payment method, and the relevant taxes would be refunded from Kickstarter directly.

​Can I issue multiple refunds at once?

Unfortunately not, at this time it is only possible to refund one backer at a time.

​I need an invoice for a refund I issued

We are unable to provide invoices or receipts for refunds. If you need a detailed breakdown for your records, we recommend reaching out to your card issuer to get this for each transaction.

​Can I remove a reward item from a backer’s pledge?

Yes! If a backer has changed their mind about an item in their pledge, or you are no longer able to ship a specific reward to a backer, you can remove it from their pledge with a partial refund. To fully remove it, make sure you also toggle the “Remove” button to the check mark.

More information about Kickstarter refunds in general can be found here.

​If you need a list of all the refunds that have been issued for your project, please contact our Support team. They will be able to provide a report of all refunds processed by you to date.

How do I manage backer information?

Your project’s backer report has all the tools you need to work with your backers during and after your campaign. You’ll find a link to it in the creator navigation bar, to the left of your project page.

From the backer report you can:

  • See a list of all your backers and sort them by various filters, including reward tier, add-ons, pledge date, who has responded to the survey, etc.
  • Communicate with your backers and view message history.
  • Request information from your backers (mailing address, T-shirt size, etc.) to fulfill rewards.
  • View backers’ information, edit shipping addresses, and leave yourself a note for special requests.
  • Track fulfillment by updating the reward status for each backer’s pledge.

If you are using the Kickstarter Pledge Manager, your backer report will also include the ability to:

  • Save a view to help you keep track of a specific group of pledges. For example, any Late Pledge backers who selected a certain reward tier and are based in France.
  • Send tracking numbers and access to digital rewards.

If your backer report access has expired, you can request an extension following a few simple steps.

How do I set a status for my project?

After a project ends successfully, you’ll see the option to set a “Project status” at the top of the creator tools menu, located on the left side of your project page. You may also see a pop-up asking for an updated Project status once you have a completed project.

This status is a quick and easy way to let folks know how your project is progressing since it ended.

The default project status will be “No rewards made”. To change this, simply choose one of the options from the dropdown menu, and click “Save”. After a status is intentionally set for a project, a fulfillment indicator label will be added to the overview of that project that is visible on the Creator tab of a project page for repeat creators. Once you’ve marked your project as ‘Fulfillment complete’, you’ll no longer see the Project status dropdown in your creator tools.

Below you can see which fulfillment indicator label will be added to a project based on the status that has been set. We’ll encourage you from time to time to review your project’s status and confirm it’s up to date, which in turn helps to make sure a project’s fulfillment indicator is accurate for potential backers.

  • Marked as in production
    • No rewards made
    • Some rewards made
  • Marked as in fulfillment
    • All rewards made
    • Fulfilling some rewards
    • Fulfilling all rewards
  • Marked as fulfilled
    • Fulfillment complete

It’s important to remember that when you set a status for your successfully ended project, this is intended to act as a quick overview of where you are with production and fulfillment, and should not be seen as a replacement for project updates. We still encourage you to post regular project updates to give your backers a more detailed insight into the work you are doing to bring your project to life.

How do I ship rewards with Easyship?

Whether you need to budget for shipping, or figure out what countries you can ship your rewards to, Easyship’s dedicated crowdfunding team can help you. Through Kickstarter’s affiliation, you will benefit from waived membership fees, free consulting, greatly discounted shipping rates and upgraded options for your backers, as well as shipping credits for qualifying campaigns.

You can reach out to Easyship at any stage of your project to feel confident about your shipping options. When logged in to your Easyship account, simply select “Book an Onboarding meeting” to schedule a consultation with an account manager. You can also click the “Support” button at any time to email, call, or live chat with their team. Additionally, check out their help center for more information.

Their team is experienced in supporting crowdfunding campaigns of all sizes. We recommend you check out their Collect feature, which allows you to confirm shipping address and charge backers for shipping options of their choice whenever you are getting ready to ship.

Once your campaign has successfully concluded, here are the steps to get your backers into their system easily. Keep in mind that Easyship has dedicated account managers to help you through every step of the way, don’t be shy to reach out if you have any questions.

Step 1: Get your pledges ready for shipping

  • Send out your Kickstarter survey to collect addresses, and anything else you may need from your backers. Once shipping addresses have been collected, you are ready to transfer orders to Easyship following a couple of quick steps.

Step 2: Transfer pledges to Easyship

  • For the smoothest process, Easyship recommends adding your rewards and add-ons to the Product Listings page on your Easyship Dashboard prior to uploading your pledges. This way your orders will be mass updated when importing your pledges with all relevant information needed. Important: For ‘Product Description’ use your reward or add-on name.
  • Once you have collected shipping addresses, go to the backer report page, and click on “Export backer report”.
  • The first filter should remain on the default option ‘All reward tiers - one file’, or if not all of your rewards require shipping, you can also download reports for the rewards you’d like to ship and upload them individually.
  • For the format, select ‘Easyship - quotes/shipping labels’.
  • Once you have successfully downloaded the report, head over to Easyship via our affiliate link to receive free access: app.easyship.com
  • Complete the signup flow and once on your Easyship Dashboard, click on “Create Shipments” on the left hand navigation.
  • Next, select ‘Import from file’ on the ‘Add shipments’ options on the header of the shipments page, or simply click here to get there directly: app.easyship.com
  • Drop your report(s) on the upload field and you’re done with the transfer. Congrats, your pledges are now in Easyship ready to be processed! Please note that the upload can take a moment if you have a large number of backers.

Step 3: Getting ready for shipping

  • Make sure that all of your pledges have been synced with Easyship by following the steps above.
  • You can then easily bulk update your reward dimensions once your pledges are loaded into your Easyship dashboard. Click on the ‘Filling-in your product dimensions’ prompt on your shipment page to add.

How does Easyship work for backers?

When a reward item is shipped, backers will automatically receive an email with the tracking information for their shipment directly from Easyship or the carrier, which will allow them to keep track of when their reward is estimated to arrive. If your backers are reporting they did not receive tracking, you should be able to reference their individual tracking number via your Easyship dashboard.

If you are running a large campaign, with 500 backers or more, and you’d like to offer more options for your backers, such as the ability to upgrade their shipping selection, pre-pay import taxes, or add insurance to their shipment, you can do so by registering to use Easyship Collect, this feature is currently available for qualifying campaigns with 500 or more backers. We are working on extending this benefit to all creators soon, stay tuned for updates! To get access to this feature, reach out to your Easyship’s Account Manager who will guide you through every step of this process.

​

How to build your backer survey

When you’re ready to begin collecting your backers’ information to fulfill their rewards, simply follow the steps below to build your backer survey:

  • First, select either ‘Pledge manager’ or ‘Backer survey’, from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page. If you are eligible to use our Pledge Manager, but wish to only send a backer survey, you can click the “Switch to survey only” button at the top of the Pledge manager page.
  • ​
    • If you will be using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager, click the “Start” button within the ‘Create survey (Optional)’ section.
    • If you will be using Kickstarter’s Backer Survey only, click the “Start” button to start building your survey.
    • ​
  • Under the ‘Item questions’ tab, you will see all of the items that were offered as part of your campaign, including any that were available as add-ons.
  • ​
  • To create questions and variants for each item, click “Create questions” next to a specific item.
  • ​
    • If there are any variants for the item, such as different colors or sizes, that you’ll need a backer to select from, you can add these by clicking “Create variant”.
      • You can add more than one variant for an item, such as color and size, but we recommend adding no more than three if possible.
      • For each variant, you will need to add a selection of values (e.g. the different sizes available). The values are what the backer will select from when completing their survey.
      • Your SKUs will automatically generate for each item based on the item name and variant values added. For example, “Reward 1”, in size small and the color blue, will be generated as “Reward-1-Small-Blue”. You can edit the SKUs later via the SKU table.
    • If you need further information for an item, you can collect this by adding an open text question under ‘Item customization’.
    • For any items where there are no variants created in the backer survey, the default SKU will be the first three words of the item name.
    • ​
  • Next, head to ‘Backer questions’ to add any general questions you’d like your backers to answer, such as how they heard about your campaign.
  • ​
    • For each question you will have the option of making it an open text, multiple selection, or single selection answer. You can also choose to segment questions and set them up to be available for all backers, or specific to certain rewards only. These questions can be arranged in your preferred order.
      • By default a question will require an answer. Any questions that aren’t required in order to fulfill your campaign should be marked as optional.
      • If there is a question that may not apply to everyone but an answer is required, make sure to provide them with the option to say “Not applicable”.
      • ​
  • When you have added all of the information you’ll need from your backers, you can go ahead and preview the survey as your backers will see it.
  • ​
    • At the top of the page, select different reward tiers and add-ons from the dropdown menu to see how the survey will look for specific backers. The backer survey will automatically ask the questions relevant for each item within a pledge, including any add-ons.
    • ​
  • If you’re happy with your survey, and no further information is needed, you can save it to be sent to your backers when you launch your pledge manager, or if you selected to only use the survey tool, you can send it whenever you’re ready.

Backer survey keywords

  • SKU = This stands for “Stock Keeping Unit”, and will be the name assigned to each item that makes up your inventory to help you keep track of quantities.
  • Variant = This is the way in which the reward item will differ. For example, ‘Size’ and ‘Color’.
  • Value = The values are the options available for each variant. For example, if the variant is ‘Size’, the values may be ‘Small’, ‘Medium’, ‘Large’.

How many surveys can I send my backers?

The survey can only be sent once, so make sure that you are asking for everything you need in order to fulfill backers’ rewards. We highly recommend previewing it before launching your survey or pledge manager, to double-check that nothing crucial is missing.

What should I do if I need additional information not included in my survey?

If you realize after launching your survey or pledge manager that something was missed you can message your backers directly through the backer report. You can also filter the report to a specific reward tier and send a message to a smaller group of backers.

Additionally, if there is information you need from your backers before your campaign ends, you may want to collect this through direct messaging.

Can I send my Kickstarter Backer Survey while still collecting Late Pledges?

If you opted to use Kickstarter’s survey tool only, you can send your backer survey while Late Pledges are active on your project. When a backer joins your project through Late Pledges they will instantly receive their survey alongside their pledge confirmation.

After the survey has been sent out, however, we recommend that no further reward items are created for your Late Pledges, as it won’t be possible to edit the survey to include questions specific to any new items.

If you opted to use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool, you will need to end Late Pledges before the survey can be launched. The backer survey will be sent out to backers as part of the pledge manager and should only be launched when you are ready to begin production of your rewards and need to start collecting item preferences and shipping addresses.

Further reading:

  • ​How can I use the backer report?
  • ​Where can I find my Backer Survey responses?
  • ​How can I use the Fulfillment dashboard to manage reward production and fulfillment?

How to issue a refund if the backer’s original payment method has expired?

As backers are unable to update the payment method for pledges that have been collected, any refunds processed through Kickstarter can only be sent to the original payment method.

If the card on file is no longer active, one of the following typically happens:

  • If the original card used was attached to an account, as most debit and credit cards are, the bank will see the incoming credit on an expired card and roll it into the appropriate account
  • Our payments processor uses an updater service that will know if a card number is no longer valid and has been replaced with a new number. In this case, the credit should be issued to the new valid card number

If neither of these two options apply to the backer’s situation, you will see an error message when trying to issue the refund. In such cases, the refund will need to be issued outside of Kickstarter. After a backer has been refunded via a third party, you can contact our Support team to assist in removing the refunded backer from the project.

If backers are having trouble locating a refund that has already been issued to an expired card, they should reach out to our Support team.

I’m ready to fulfill, why do I need to end Late Pledges?

When you’re ready to begin fulfilling and are starting to collect backers’ information, we will require that Late Pledges are ended at this point. This is to ensure that there are no gaps in your backer data as you move your project into the next phase.

Can I re-enable Late Pledges after I finish fulfillment?

No, once all Late Pledge rewards have been ended for your project it won’t be possible to re-enable Late Pledges.

Third-Party Pledge Manager Data FAQs

Kickstarter’s offering of Late Pledges will provide creators with the opportunity to collect pledges after their crowdfunding campaign has ended. Backers who pledge within this Late Pledge phase will be added to the backer report. For creators who use a third-party pledge manager, there are some things to know!

Where will I see information in Kickstarter about Late Pledges?

New pledge information in your Late Pledge phase will be reflected in your backer report. The ‘Pledged’ column will show the date the pledge was made for easy reference.

When should I set up my pledge manager?

We recommend setting up your pledge manager once you’ve finished collecting Late Pledges on Kickstarter. This is so you have an easy, one time transfer of pledge information.

Will Late Pledges be sent to my pledge manager?

Kickstarter won’t automatically send any new pledge information to third-party pledge managers, which is why we recommend waiting until you’ve finished collecting pledges on Kickstarter.

What do I do if I have set up my pledge manager already and have started collecting Late Pledges?

Some platforms use an API to get backer information, which is a one-time import. Others allow for multiple manual imports. See table below:

What do I do if I miss my estimated delivery date?

The estimated delivery date is intended to set expectations for backers on when they will receive rewards. Setbacks are possible with any project — creative ones especially. When the unforeseen occurs, creators are expected to post a project update explaining the situation. Sharing the story, speed bumps and all, is part of the Kickstarter experience.

Creators can also keep their backers informed on the status of their reward with the ‘Status’ column of the backer report. In the event that fulfillment is delayed, creators can choose ”Delayed” from the dropdown menu for all backers that are still waiting for their reward. This can then be updated to “In progress” or “Shipped” when things are back on track.

Creators who are honest and transparent will find backers to be far more forgiving. We’ve all felt the urge to avoid things when we feel bad about them, but leaving backers in the dark makes them assume the worst. It not only reflects badly on the project, it’s disrespectful to the support that community has given and to other Kickstarter creators. Regular communication is a must.

What information can I see about my backers?

While your project is live

As folks pledge to your project, you will be able to see more information about them in your project’s backer report. You can access the backer report via the creator navigation bar, located on the left-hand side of your project page.

While your project is live you will be able to see a backer’s name, pledge amount, reward selected, and their shipping country, if applicable. If a backer has left a comment on your project page you will be able to quickly view this information by clicking on their name in the comment, followed by the “View pledge” button.

A backer’s email address won’t be visible in the backer report until after a project ends and is successfully funded. This means that while a project is still live, or if a project is canceled or unsuccessful, backer email addresses will not populate in the backer report.

After your project ends successfully

After your project has ended successfully, and if you are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager or Backer Survey, you will be able to see all information collected through these tools in the backer report, such as shipping addresses, item variant preferences (e.g. size, color), and any other details you requested. If you notice any information is missing, the chances are that the backer hasn’t completed their pledge manager or survey yet.

You can export the backer report as a CSV file, this will help with managing your project’s fulfillment. You can read more about using the backer report here.

If you choose to use a third-party survey service, you will still be able to view the default backer information in your backer report (backer’s name, pledge amount, reward selected, and their shipping country, if applicable). You can also use it to leave a note to yourself for each backer, and keep track of which rewards have been shipped, or are in progress, by updating the status.

Please note: For backers who have opted to hide their email address when signing up to Kickstarter with their Apple ID, you will only be able to see their private relay email address in your backer report at the end of the campaign. The unique private relay email address tied to their Kickstarter account can only be used for communication within Kickstarter. This means that any communication you may send outside of our platform will unfortunately not reach their inbox.

If your backer report access has expired, you can request an extension by following a few simple steps.

What information can’t I see about my backers?

Credit card information will not be shown in the backer report. Creators are required to keep backer information confidential, except as strictly necessary to communicate with backers directly and fulfill rewards.

As a creator you should never ask a backer for information that isn’t necessary for fulfilling their reward, or request sensitive personal information such as their Social Security number or payment information. You can read more under the “Information that’s shared with creators and collaborators” section of our Privacy Policy.

What is Spotify for Authors and how does it work?

What is Spotify for Authors and how can it help me?

Spotify for Authors’ helps authors publish and distribute their audiobooks globally. Kickstarter authors offering audiobooks as a backer reward can request free Spotify redemption codes once their project has been successfully funded.

When your audiobook is ready, you can publish it to Spotify through Spotify for Authors. You’ll receive as many redemption codes as you need for your backers on Kickstarter and you’ll receive another 100 complimentary redemption codes for each published title to use as you’d like in your marketing and promotions. Plus, publishing your audiobook on Spotify puts your work in front of their audience of millions of listeners and eligible premium subscribers.

How can I distribute the audiobook redemption codes to my backers?

Once your project is successfully funded on Kickstarter and you are ready to fulfill, the next step is to create a project on Spotify for Authors, upload your audiobook, and distribute it. To get started, fill out the backer code submission form. Someone from Spotify for Authors will respond within 5 days with your free redemption codes.

The Spotify for Authors team is available to help at any step, email them at [email protected] for assistance.

Can anyone find my audiobook once it’s on Spotify?

Yes! Your audiobook will be findable on Spotify. Users can purchase it to listen to, or eligible Spotify Premium subscribers in select markets can play it using their 15 listening hours a month.

If I take advantage of this service, would I be able to publish my audiobook on other platforms or would I have to publish exclusively on Spotify?

This partnership provides authors with free redemption codes for their Kickstarter backers on Spotify. Spotify does not have an exclusivity option, so you are welcome to also publish your audiobook on other platforms as well.

Will Spotify take a cut of any of my pledges?

Spotify will not take any cut of your pledges for providing free redemption codes.

What if my project doesn’t get successfully funded? Can I still work with Spotify for Authors?

The redemption codes are only available for backers of successfully funded projects. If your project was not successfully funded on Kickstarter, but you’re interested in putting your audiobook on Spotify, please visit them at authors.spotify.com.

For my project, I want to make more than one audiobook available to my backers. Will I receive redemption codes for each audiobook?

Yes, each successfully funded Kickstarter project, even ones with multiple titles, receives free redemption codes for their backers. In addition, every title receives an extra 100 redemption codes that can be used to distribute audiobooks to whomever you choose.

I remember this partnership being with Findaway Voices, not Spotify for Authors. Is this the same program?

Yes, this is the same program. Redemption codes have moved from being a feature of Findaway Voices, to a feature of Spotify for Authors, but the program otherwise remains the same.

What is the ‘Status’ column on the backer report and how does it work?

The ‘Status’ column on the backer report allows you to track the status of individual backers’ rewards in a way that’s visible to them.

You can apply a status to an individual backer, or a selection of backers, by checking the box to the left of their backer number. You can also filter by reward tier and select backers within that tier. To set a reward status for each pledge, select from one of the following options within the dropdown menu:

​

  • Not started
  • In progress
  • Shipped
  • Delayed

You can update the reward status as often as you need to to keep backers in the loop. Hit an unexpected bump in the road? Update the status for each pledge to “Delayed” in the backer report. Remember, transparency goes a long way toward building trust with your backers.

Will backers be notified when the reward status is updated?

No, Kickstarter does not send backers a notification when their reward status changes. When you update the status of a reward, this information will be visible to the backer on their backed projects page, and in their pledge details on the project page*.

In addition to updating the reward status we also recommend posting an update to let your backers know what stage you’re at and how your project is progressing.

*Please note that the reward status information isn’t currently available when viewing pledge details through the Kickstarter app.

I already marked rewards as sent. What do I need to do next?

You’ll need to opt in to share the reward status with backers. The ‘Status’ column of the backer report replaces the ‘Rewards sent?’ column. As such we’ll need to migrate the information over so that you can continue to track your project’s fulfillment.

Until this information is migrated, or if you haven’t set a status for a pledge, the status will default to “Not provided” in both the backer report and in the backer’s pledge details.

You can choose to migrate the information at any time. To do so, click the “Yes, share” button in the “Share reward status with backers?” box that appears in the bottom right corner of the page.

Once migrated, all pledges previously marked as sent will show as “Shipped” in the backer report and this information will be visible to the backer.

When should I mark a reward as “Shipped”?

We recommend waiting to mark a reward as “Shipped” until it is on its way to the backer. This ensures that the status the backer sees is accurate and backers know to expect their reward soon.

Where can I find my backer’s pledge manager or survey responses?

You can view the responses to your pledge manager or survey in your backer report, including any information you requested, such as backer addresses, SKU selections, and the responses to the item and backer questions. The backer report can be easily downloaded as a CSV file to be opened in Excel, Numbers, or any spreadsheet program.

On your project’s Fulfillment dashboard you will also be able to see the automatically generated SKUs for your reward items, along with the count for each. For any SKUs without variants that don’t require a survey response to finalize, the SKU count will match the item count. For SKUs that require backer selections, the counts will be updated as backer responses to the survey are collected. This number will be final once all backers have responded to the survey and can help to determine quantities needed for production.

Please note: The backer report export contains personal information about backers, and it is your responsibility to protect it. Do not share it with services or third parties you do not trust. If you do use backers’ information with another service, like a shipping partner, make sure to read their Terms of Use and Privacy Policy to see how they handle private information.

Can I personalize the SKUs in the SKU table?

Yes! While SKUs are automatically generated based on the item name and/or variant values added for each when building your Backer Survey, you will be able to make any changes to them by going to the SKU table of your Fulfillment dashboard.
