Please keep in mind that this will only be editable after you’ve sent your Pledge Manager or Backer Survey.

If you choose to edit these, a SKU will be required for each reward item and that every SKU must be unique.

SKU example:

If one of your item names is “Reward 1”, and in the Backer Survey you add both a ‘Size’ and ‘Color’ variant with a selection of values that a backer can choose from, when a backer selects the values “Small” and “Blue”, the SKU that would go with their selection will be: Reward1-Small-Blue.

If your item name is longer than three words, the SKU name will be truncated to the first three words of your item. Following the example above, if your item name was “Awesome and Cool T-Shirt,” the SKU would be AwesomeandCool-Small-Blue.

For items where there are no variants created in the Backer Survey, the default SKU will be the first three words of the item name.

Managing your pledge

Can I cancel a pledge?

While a project is still live you can cancel your pledge by visiting the project page and clicking the “Manage your pledge” button on the right-hand side. From there, click “Cancel Pledge” next to the “Change your pledge” link.

Remember, a pledge can only be canceled while a project is live. If the project has ended and pledge collection has started it is no longer possible to cancel.

Additionally, it is not possible to decrease or cancel a pledge during the last 24 hours of the campaign if doing so would drop the project below its funding goal.

Can I cancel or modify a Late Pledge?

As funds are taken immediately when Late Pledging it won’t be possible to modify or cancel your pledge. If you need to cancel a Late Pledge you’ll need to reach out to the creator directly to see if this is possible.

How can I change my payment method before funding ends?

To change your payment method for a live pledge, please follow the below steps:

  • Visit the project page and click the “Manage your pledge” button located at the top of the page.
  • On the next page, click “Update payment method” and from there, select “Add a new card” to update your payment information.
  • Enter your new card details and click “Pledge” to confirm.

If you make any changes to the Payment methods tab of your Settings it’s important to keep the following in mind:

  • Adding or removing a saved card from your account does not automatically update the payment method on any live pledges.
  • Removing a saved payment method from your account will not cancel any live pledges.
  • Updating your payment method for one pending pledge will not update the payment method for other pending pledges you may have. If you have multiple pending pledges, you will need to update the credit card for each pledge.
    • We recommend selecting the “Remember this card for future pledges” option when entering your new card in order to avoid having to enter these details again.

To change your payment method from the app, please follow the below steps:

Android

  • Tap the menu icon in the top left of the screen and select your profile name.
  • From here, tap the project in question and then “Manage pledge”.
  • On the next screen, scroll up and then select “Change payment method”.
  • Select “Use a new card” and add your credit card details.
  • Confirm via the “Pledge” button.

iOS

  • Tap your profile icon in the bottom right of the screen.
  • Select the project in question and tap on “Manage your pledge”.
  • Select “Change payment method”.
  • Select “Use a new card” and add your credit card details.
  • Confirm via the “Pledge” button.

How can I change my pledge amount or reward selection?

While a project is live you can adjust your pledge by visiting the project page and clicking the “Manage your pledge” button. On the following page, select “Change your pledge”.

From here, you’ll be able to adjust your pledge amount or switch to a different reward tier (changing reward tier will automatically update your pledge amount). Additionally, you’ll have the option to add any add-on rewards, or give a little extra with bonus support.

If you’d prefer not to receive a reward, simply change to the “Make a pledge without a reward” tier and enter your new pledge amount.

Remember, when changing your pledge amount you are not adding to your existing pledge, the new amount you enter will be the total amount collected if the project is successfully funded.

You can also change your pledge amount or reward selection through the Kickstarter app.

Once a project has ended it is no longer possible to modify your pledge amount or reward selection. If you need to seek out an alternative arrangement, we recommend reaching out to the project creator.

If you experience any issues while trying to complete your pledge, please consult this article of common pledge problems.

My refund was sent to an expired card, what do I do?

With Stripe, our payments processor, refunds issued through Kickstarter can only be applied to the card originally used to pledge to a project. If the refund was already sent to an expired card, one of three things will have happened:

  • The bank will realize that the card number is no longer valid, but will see the account it’s tied to, at which point the refund should be transferred to the new card number/account automatically.
  • Stripe’s card updater service will recognize your new card number that replaced the old card and the refund will be applied automatically to the new card number.
  • The bank will reject the refund to the outdated card, and the funds will be returned to our payments processor.

If your card is no longer valid (having expired or been canceled), we strongly recommend reaching out directly to your bank. When speaking with them, you’ll want to mention the date of the refund, as well as the amount refunded.

If you still don’t see the funds in your account, please allow 30 days to pass from when the refund was issued by the creator, then contact our Support team for further trace information. Refund trace details only become available after the 30 day period.

What is the ‘Your Voice Matters’ feature?

After a campaign has successfully ended, backers of the project will see a banner at the top of the project page with a ‘Your Voice Matters’ section. Here, backers can select from one of three smiley faces in response to the question, “How do you feel about this project so far?”.

This feedback feature allows backers to share their backing experience with us for any successfully funded projects they have supported on Kickstarter, and their responses may be shared anonymously with the project creator.

I selected the wrong feedback option, can I change this?

The ‘Your Voice Matters’ section resets after 24 hours, so if you return to the project page after this time you will be able to select a different smiley face.

Where can I keep track of my pledges?

You can keep track of your pledges through your Backed projects page.

In the active pledges section, you’ll be able to see your current live pledges, the amounts you’ve decided to pledge, the rewards you’ve selected, and more important details. If you’d like to change a pledge amount or reward selection, just click on the project title next to the corresponding pledge and select edit next to your pledge amount.

The successful pledges section lists all pledges for which you’ve already been charged. This will include any Late Pledges made through Kickstarter. Under the ‘Status’ column you will be able to see the latest status for your reward, if that information has been provided by the creator*. You can also keep track of received rewards by checking the “Got it!” box next to each project. If the creator has used Kickstarter’s survey tool you can view your responses by clicking the round “+” button to the right-hand side of the project name.

The unsuccessful pledges section documents all of your pledges that won’t be charged, in addition to any pledges that were refunded. This includes projects that were not successfully funded, pledges that you decided to cancel, as well as pledges for suspended projects.

To locate your backer number for a successful project, you can do so by either clicking the round “+” button to the right-hand side of the project name, or by selecting “View pledge” from the blue banner at the top of the project page. This will take you to a pledge summary page where you can review the details of your pledge and find your backer number at the bottom of the ‘Backing details’ tab.

If you’ve backed a project that you can no longer find under your active or successful pledges, you’ll want to look for it within the unsuccessful pledges section as the pledge may have been refunded by the creator or suspended by our team.

*Please note that the reward status information isn’t currently available when viewing pledge details through the Kickstarter app.

Will I receive a receipt for my pledge?

Once your pledge to a project has been collected, we’ll send you an email that includes the details of your pledge and your reward selection. The subject line will be: “We’ve collected your pledge for [Project name]”. You can also find these details listed in your profile, on your Backed projects page.

While we don’t provide formal invoices, you’re welcome to contact the project creator directly for help.

If you did not receive a pledge confirmation email, please try checking your spam folder first. If you’re still having trouble locating this email, please get in touch.

Payments and Taxes

Are creators based in the EU and UK responsible for VAT?

As tax liability varies from country to country, providing rewards to a backer based in a specific region might create a VAT liability for you.

For creators using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager tool, we will collect VAT (value-added tax) on items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK, when Kickstarter is the deemed supplier and, as authorized by the creator, when the creator is the responsible party. You can read more about this here.

In some cases, as a deemed supplier, Kickstarter will be responsible for remitting VAT collected on the creator’s transactions to the relevant tax regulator. In other cases, creators will be responsible for remitting VAT, and any funds collected on Kickstarter toward value-added tax will be disbursed to the creator. Creators will be provided with tax reporting that outlines the responsible party for each VAT transaction.

VAT will be determined by using a combination of the creator’s VAT registration, business location, the shipping origin and destination, and item-based tax codes.

Please note that Kickstarter does not collect VAT for creators who choose not to use Kickstarter’s pledge manager tool. In these cases, ensuring that VAT is correctly accounted for on these transactions is the responsibility of each individual project creator.

Tax services provided by Kickstarter do not constitute tax advice and creators are encouraged to work with their tax advisor to ensure compliance with applicable tax regulations.

If a creator chooses to opt out of VAT collection through the Pledge Manager, Kickstarter will not collect or remit VAT on these transactions, and full responsibility for VAT compliance remains with the creator.

​Will Kickstarter collect EU import duties from EU-based backers?

No, the EU customs duties and national fees are entirely separate from VAT and are not collected or remitted by Kickstarter. You can read more about the EU import duty changes here.

Do you store any of my verified information?

Kickstarter does not store full SSN information. When the user submits the form, the information is tokenized immediately– the full details do not pass through our server at any point, and are securely stored by Stripe.

How will I be paid out for Late Pledges?

Once the payout for the original campaign has been sent, the first Late Pledge payout will be issued 7 days later. Subsequent payouts will send on a 7-day cadence for the pledges in the Late Pledge phase:

  • Original campaign payout: 14 days after original campaign’s deadline (includes all campaign pledges).
  • First Late Pledge payout: 21 days after the original campaign’s deadline (includes all Late Pledges made during the first 14 days after the deadline).
  • Second Late Pledge payout: 28 days after the campaign’s deadline (includes all Late Pledges made 15-21 days after the deadline).
  • This cadence will continue every 7 days after the campaign ends, until Late Pledges have concluded.

The time a payout begins initiating is based on your campaign’s original deadline. For example, if your campaign ended at 6:00 PM EST, each subsequent payout will begin initiating at 6:00 PM EST. If a payout is initiated on a non-business day, your financial institution may not show it as processing until the next business day.

Please note: In the event that the funds have not yet settled at the time a payout is set to initiate, that payout will be deferred until the settlement is complete. This process typically takes anywhere from a few hours to a few business days, depending on processing times.

If there are no Late Pledges collected during a given payout period, a payout will not be issued. Additionally, for some project countries, if the collected amount is below a certain threshold (typically ~$1 USD), a payout will not be issued. Instead, this amount will be bundled into the next Late Pledge payout.

​Will I be able to change my bank account between payouts?

Payouts of Late Pledges will be sent to the same bank account used for your crowdfunding campaign. We highly recommend taking extra care to ensure that you have input the correct details before your Late Pledge period begins to avoid any payout issues. In certain cases, we may be able to help with updating the bank account—please contact our Support team to see if this is possible.

​Note that if any one payout was already initiated, we can no longer update the bank account for that payout.

How will Kickstarter approach VAT collection in the pledge manager?

As tax liability varies from country to country, providing rewards to a backer based in a specific region might create a VAT liability for you. For creators using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager, we will collect VAT (value-added tax) on items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK, when Kickstarter is the deemed supplier and, as authorized by the creator, when the creator is the responsible party.

Creators may also choose to opt out of Kickstarter’s VAT collection service, in which case Kickstarter will not collect or remit VAT on their behalf and the creator will remain fully responsible for VAT compliance.

​Recent changes to VAT in the EU

In 2021, the role of electronic interfaces in terms of reporting, record-keeping and VAT duties changed drastically with the introduction of Article 14a of the VAT directive.

​Reason for the changes

Sales made to consumers within the EU, both as part of the trading between Member States and in terms of import from third countries, are, to a large extent, effectuated via online marketplaces. The EU has been long struggling with effective collection of VAT on such transactions, especially in the case of sellers from outside the EU.

​Kickstarter’s Role in VAT Compliance

Under the new guidance, there are certain instances where Kickstarter will act as a deemed supplier and is therefore obligated to collect VAT from EU-based backers, including:

  • Instances where rewards are shipped directly to EU based backers from outside the EU and the value of the parcel, inclusive of shipping and duties, is less than 150 EUR.
  • Instances where creators are from outside the EU and use EU-based hubs to ship rewards to EU based backers.

VAT Collection Process

  • Kickstarter will calculate and collect VAT for instances where it is considered the deemed supplier in accordance with the EU VAT Directive.
  • In instances where Kickstarter is not considered the deemed supplier, Kickstarter will charge tax on your behalf and remit funds to the creator in order for them to meet their VAT obligations.
  • Creators may opt out of Kickstarter’s VAT collection service. Where this option is selected, Kickstarter will not calculate, collect, or remit VAT, and creators are responsible for ensuring VAT and any import or customs charges are handled correctly.

Tax Reporting

  • Creators will receive detailed reports outlining VAT collected and remitted to authorities to help you manage compliance and recover VAT paid at import if applicable.

UK VAT

  • There are certain instances where Kickstarter can be considered the deemed supplier in accordance with UK VAT laws, including when:
    • Rewards are delivered to UK individuals or non-VAT registered businesses from creators outside of the UK and,
    • The consignment is worth 135 GBP or less OR is already located in the UK
  • In instances where Kickstarter is operating as the deemed supplier and is required to collect VAT, Kickstarter will collect and remit to the UK authorities (HMRC).
  • Kickstarter will supply reporting to creators to assist with VAT reclamation, if applicable. Creators should consult with a tax advisor for this process.
  • In instances where Kickstarter is not considered the deemed supplier, Kickstarter will calculate the taxes and remit to the creator during the pledge manager.

Disclaimer

This guidance is for informational purposes only and does not constitute tax advice. For specific questions about your VAT obligations, consult a qualified tax advisor.

Is the financial information that I share on the Payment tab secure?

The financial information that you provide when completing the Payment tab of your project will never be shared publicly. This information is used to ensure that you’ll be ready to receive funds if your project is successfully funded.

Stripe, our payments processor, meets and exceeds the most stringent industry standards for security. Additionally, this page is protected by an industry-standard software protocol called Secure Sockets Layer (SSL), which encrypts your personal information.

At Kickstarter, we are committed to respecting and protecting the privacy and security of our users. We invite you to read our Privacy Policy for more information.

I’ve received my project payout, how can I get an invoice breaking down the fees?

You will have access to a payout’s funding summary about 48 hours after your payout has been successfully processed by your bank. Here you will also have access to a print-friendly invoice, listing the fees deducted by Kickstarter and our payments processor, Stripe.

To view the funding summary and invoice for a payout, first make sure that you are signed in to your creator account. Next, select ‘Payouts & Taxes’ from the creator navigation bar, located on the left side of your project page.

On the ‘Payouts & Taxes’ page you will be able to view all payouts initiated for your project so far. If late pledging is active for your project, you will see these payouts listed until Late Pledges have ended. Additionally, for creators who enabled Pledge Over Time, or are using Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager, the payouts associated with these funds will also be available on this page.

Clicking on a payout will display the breakdown of funds (gross pledges, dropped pledges, refunds) and deductions (Kickstarter fee and payment processing fee). To access an invoice from this view, simply click the “View invoice” button at the top and print the web page.

For most purposes, the printable invoice provided here will suffice in listing fees associated with your project. However, if you require a more extensive report of the disbursements, you can refer to the breakdown directly within the funding summary that lists a description of funds raised and deductions. Please note that we do not provide any further type of invoice.

If you need to make a correction to the information that appears on your printable invoice, please reach out to our Support team.

​My payout has been initiated but I’m not seeing it on the ‘Payouts & Taxes’ page yet?

The funding summary for a payout will only become available about 48 hours after the funds have been successfully processed by your bank. If the payout is initiated on a non-business day, please allow some additional time for this to be completed.

Recent Changes to EU Import Duties (Payments and Taxes)

Changes to EU customs rules came into effect in July 2026. These changes will affect low-value shipments (under €150) sent from outside the EU to backers in the EU. You can read more about these changes below.

What is changing?

From 1 July, 2026, the EU is removing the existing customs duty exemption on goods valued under €150. A flat duty of €3 per item type will apply to these shipments. This is a transitional rate that will remain in place until 1 July, 2028.

Separately, some EU member states have introduced their own national handling fees on top of this duty:

  • France: €2 per goods type, effective 1 March, 2026
  • Italy: €2 per goods type, effective 1 June, 2026

Other EU member states are expected to follow with their own fees. Full details have not yet been published in all countries.

These charges apply per item type, not per parcel. A package containing two different product categories would attract €6 in EU customs duty, plus any applicable national fee.

Who pays these charges?

These costs are collected by customs authorities at the point of importation, not at checkout. Who bears the cost depends on the shipping terms agreed between the creator and their fulfilment partner:

  • If the creator ships on a duties paid basis (DDP), they or their fulfillment partner cover the charges.
  • If the creator ships on a duties unpaid basis (DDU), the backer may be asked to pay before or at delivery.

What does this mean for creators?

If you are shipping physical rewards to EU backers, we recommend:

  • Reviewing your shipping costs and reward pricing to account for these additional charges.
  • Checking how your fulfilment partner handles customs duties and declarations.
  • Ensuring your shipments use accurate product classifications, as duties are assessed per item type.

What does this mean for backers?

If you are an EU-based backer receiving rewards shipped from outside the EU, you may see additional charges applied at the point of delivery, depending on how the creator has arranged shipping. If you have questions about a specific order, we recommend reaching out to the project creator or your shipping carrier.

What about VAT?

Kickstarter collects VAT at checkout for eligible shipments under the IOSS scheme. These new customs duties and national fees are entirely separate from VAT and are not collected or remitted by Kickstarter.

We will continue to monitor developments and share further guidance as more details are confirmed by EU authorities. If you have questions, please reach out to your fulfillment partner or shipping carrier.

Tax Collection in Canada

What is a Distribution Platform Operator?

In Canada, a Distribution Platform Operator (or equivalent) is typically defined as a business that facilitates the sale of goods or services by third-party sellers through an online platform. This includes managing payments, fulfillment, or listing products on behalf of sellers.

If you use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager to fulfill tangible or digital rewards to Canadian backers, Kickstarter (via its subsidiary Project Partners, LLC) may be considered a Distribution Platform Operator (or provincial equivalent) for Canadian goods and services tax (GST) purposes.

​How does Kickstarter support tax collection in Canada?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager uses Avalara to collect the appropriate Canadian sales tax—such as GST/HST, QST, or PST—on qualifying transactions shipping to Canadian backers. Kickstarter collects these taxes from the backers and remits them to the appropriate Canadian tax authorities on your behalf.

Collected amounts will appear in the backer report. A detailed breakdown of the applicable federal and provincial taxes per transaction will be made available within 30 days.

​What are creators responsible for?

Creators must provide accurate information about their rewards, including item categorization, description, and market value. This information is essential for correctly determining the applicable Canadian sales tax rates.

Note that some provinces may apply tax to digital goods or services in addition to physical goods. You can find further guidance on how to enter tax details for your items in this article.

Sellers registered for GST/HST or QST agree to enter into the election pursuant to Subsection 177 (1.1) of the Excise Tax Act (Canada) and section 40.0.1 of an Act respecting the Quebec sales tax to jointly elect for Project Partners, LLC to be responsible to collect, report and remit GST/HST and QST on all of the taxable supplies made by you as seller through the Kickstarter Pledge Manager. The election is effective until it is jointly revoked by Kickstarter and you. By continuing to use the Pledge Manager, you agree to these terms.

Registered sellers should complete, sign and return Form GST506 to Kickstarter:

  • Sellers registered for GST/HST only should email [email protected] to request a pre-signed election. Please return the completed form to the same email address.
  • Sellers registered for both GST/HST and QST should complete the form here.

Please contact us at [email protected] for your copy of our jointly executed election form, which you should retain with your tax files.

​What are backers responsible for?

Canadian backers will be responsible for paying the applicable GST/HST, PST, or QST on the value of their rewards and the cost of shipping, based on their province or territory of residence. Kickstarter calculates and collects this tax during checkout.

​What types of transactions may be subject to goods and services tax?

In Canada, goods and services tax (GST) may apply to both physical and digital goods, as well as shipping, depending on the destination province. Tangible items (e.g., merchandise, printed materials, or artwork) are generally taxable. Digital products and services are also often subject to GST/HST and provincial taxes. Tax rules vary by province.

​How will sales tax be managed for my campaign?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager will collect and remit applicable Canadian indirect taxes on taxable rewards shipped to Canadian backers. As a creator, you will not need to collect these taxes yourself. However, it is your responsibility to provide complete and accurate information about each item’s value and category. This data will be used to determine the tax to charge each backer.

​Will I receive reports on collected taxes?

Yes. Kickstarter will issue reports summarizing the tax collected and remitted on your behalf for Canadian backers. These reports can assist you with your own accounting and tax compliance obligations, including when determining whether you need to register for GST/HST or other provincial tax programs.

​Do I need to include collected sales tax in my tax filings?

Goods and services tax collected and remitted by Kickstarter on your behalf is not part of your revenue and should not be included as income. However, you should consult a Canadian tax professional to ensure you are meeting all federal and provincial tax obligations—particularly around income tax and possible registration thresholds for indirect tax purposes.

​Disclaimer

This content is for general informational purposes only and does not constitute legal or tax advice. For guidance specific to your situation, please consult a qualified Canadian tax advisor or accountant.

Tax Collection in the United States

What is a Marketplace Facilitator?

A Marketplace Facilitator is generally defined as a business that contracts with third-party sellers to facilitate the sale of physical and digital goods and services through an online store or marketplace.

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager (through its subsidiary Project Partners, LLC) operates as a marketplace facilitator.

What will Kickstarter be responsible for?

As a marketplace facilitator, Project Partners, LLC has the responsibility to collect and report sales and/or use tax. Project Partners, LLC utilizes the tax rate provider Avalara to calculate sales and/or use tax on qualifying orders shipping to backers in the United States, collect the amount from the backer, and remit the tax to the appropriate taxing authority.

All amounts collected will be available to view in the backer report, and you will receive a breakdown of item-level state and local tax collected from your backers orders within 30 days.

What will creators be responsible for?

Creators are required to provide accurate information about their items as may be requested, including the item categorization and value of the reward.This information will be used to calculate the applicable sales and use tax, if any. You can find more guidance for adding tax information for your items in this article.

What will backers be responsible for?

The backer will be responsible for paying sales and/or use tax on the value of the reward. Project Partners, LLC calculates the amount of tax to impose and processes the collection.

What types of transactions may be subject to sales tax?

Tax could apply to the tangible goods you offer to backers, such as products, printed materials, or art pieces. Non-physical rewards like digital content or services may or may not be subject to sales tax, depending on local laws.

How will sales/use tax be managed for my campaign?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager will automatically calculate, collect, and remit the applicable sales and/or use tax on taxable rewards. However, you will be required to properly identify, and provide the cost of, the items that make up your rewards and add-ons. The cost you provide, in combination with the reward and add-on price, will be used by Kickstarter to calculate the amount of sales tax to collect from backers.

Will I receive reports on collected taxes?

Yes, you will be provided with reports that show the amount of sales and/or use tax collected on redeemed rewards, along with any add-ons or upgrades purchased through Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager.

Do I need to include collected sales/use tax in my income tax filings?

The sales/use tax collected though Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager and remitted by Project Partners, LLC does not count as income to you. However, we recommend consulting with a tax professional to ensure you are handling any applicable taxes, such as income taxes on your campaign funds, correctly.

Disclaimer

This guidance is for informational purposes only and does not constitute tax advice. For specific questions about your tax obligations, consult a qualified tax advisor.

What are the tax implications of using Kickstarter?

Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager operates as a marketplace facilitator. We are responsible for collecting and remitting sales and tax for items shipped to all 50 US states and Canada, as well as collecting VAT (value-added tax) on items shipped to backers in the EU and the UK.

Separate from any sales and use tax/VAT, you may owe (and have the responsibility to report) national or subnational income, franchise, or other taxes. How much you owe can vary based on a number of factors, including the deduction of project-related expenses.

We highly recommend talking to an accountant or tax advisor to ensure you are compliant with applicable tax regulations. They can guide you through your particular tax scenario in the most advantageous way possible.

While we can’t offer tax advice, we have compiled a guide for financial professionals who may not be familiar with Kickstarter, which is intended to help such professionals understand some of the tax responsibilities you may be responsible for. Such guide includes coverage of sales and use tax/VAT responsibilities of creators that do not use Kickstarter’s Pledge Manager.

What is the 1099-K Form and will I receive one?

The IRS requires Kickstarter to file a 1099-K tax form for each successful project that meets the following requirements:

  • The project creator’s verified entity (company or individual) and bank account are based in the United States
  • The project collected at least $20,000 USD in gross volume in more than 200 transactions

The 1099-K is a tax form that includes all reportable payment transactions associated with your project and it is designed to assist you in reporting your taxes. It is provided to you and the IRS, as well as to some US states, depending on their requirements.

For more help understanding your 1099-K, go to IRS.gov. You can also download a blank 1099-K form here.

​State threshold requirements:

Alabama, California, Connecticut, Florida, Georgia, Hawaii, Kansas, Maine, Mississippi, New York, North Carolina, Oregon, and Tennessee require 1099-Ks to be filed for each unique tax identification number meeting all IRS requirements.

Arkansas requires 1099-Ks to be filed for each unique tax identification number with at least $2,500 USD in gross volume.

The District of Columbia, Maryland, Massachusetts, Montana, Vermont and Virginia require 1099-Ks to be filed for each unique tax identification number with at least $600 USD in gross volume.

Illinois requires 1099-Ks to be filed for each unique tax identification number with at least $1,000 USD in gross volume and more than three charges.

New Jersey requires 1099-Ks to be filed for each unique tax identification with at least $1000 USD.

Rhode Island requires 1099-ks to be filed for each unique tax identification with at least $100 USD.

​If you received a 1099-K form, or multiple forms, and are not sure why:

Kickstarter files a 1099-K for each unique tax identification number (TIN), such as a Social Security Number (SSN) or Employer Identification Number (EIN), that meets the IRS processing threshold. If you received a 1099-K and didn’t expect to, it might be because:

  • You have two or more successful projects verified with the same TIN; one meets the IRS threshold and one does not. Each will receive a 1099-K.
  • You have two or more successful projects verified with the same TIN; neither are over the IRS threshold individually but, when combined, the threshold is met. Each will receive a 1099-K.
  • ​

​How will the tax year be determined?

​

Calculations for 1099-Ks are based on when a successful project’s backings are collected by our payment processor, and are not based on a project’s initial payout date, or on any subsequent payout dates for Late Pledges.

  • For campaign pledges, collection occurs at the project’s deadline date, or up to 7 days after the project’s deadline date, for any backings that errored during initial collection attempt.
  • For Late Pledges, collection occurs right away, at the time of pledge. Late Pledges can occur anytime after a successful project’s deadline date, as long as late pledging is enabled by the creator.

This is especially important to keep in mind if your project deadline occurs at the end of the year, with your initial campaign payout date occurring early the following year; or, if your project deadline date occurs late in the current year, but you are accepting Late Pledges into the following year.

In these cases, please note that your 1099-K will report earnings based on all pledges that were collected in the current tax year only.

If your project has collections occurring in the following year (either campaign or Late Pledges), and these meet the State or IRS thresholds, you will receive a 1099-K in the following tax year as well.

​How will my 1099-K form be delivered?

Kickstarter will deliver forms electronically, or by mail, if requested, via Stripe Express. In January you will receive an email from our payment processor, Stripe, Inc., requesting consent to e-deliver your 1099-K tax form. This message will be sent to the email address associated with your Kickstarter user account, unless a different email address was provided during project verification.

If you consent to e-delivery, you will be taken to a secure portal where you can view and download your 1099-K. If you do not consent to e-delivery, a hard copy of your 1099-K will be sent in the mail. This will be mailed to the address provided during project verification.

You may be contacted by Stripe, Inc. prior to tax season with a request to confirm the tax information we have on file. If you do need to make any updates or corrections, you can do so safely and securely by following the prompts within the email.

​When will I receive my 1099-K form?

You should receive your 1099-K form by January 31st if, in the prior calendar year, you ran a successful US project that met all IRS requirements.

If you are expecting a 1099-K and have not received it by January 31st, please reach out to our Support team with the subject, “Question About My 1099-K.”

​How do I request a correction to my 1099-K form?

If you have a question about the gross amount calculation shown on your form, or if you would like to provide an updated physical address, please reach out to our Support team with the subject, “Question About My 1099-K.”

Please note that Kickstarter cannot make changes to a project creator’s verified entity (company or individual) retroactively. The PAYEE’S name on your 1099-K must match the taxable entity provided during project verification.

​What happens if I receive an unexpected 1099-K from Kickstarter?

By law, Kickstarter is obligated to send 1099-Ks to the verified entities associated with all Kickstarter projects whose collections meet State and Federal thresholds. We are unable to make any changes to the 1099-K tax form you may have received, or remove you as the verified identity without another party taking over the project and account.

If you believe you have received a 1099-K in error, we encourage you to message the project creator directly for clarification and for a resolution.

While we cannot provide legal or financial advice, this IRS article may be helpful:

irs.gov

If your information was provided to Stripe without your knowledge and/or consent, you may want to report it:

identitytheft.gov

​This guide is not intended to and does not constitute legal or tax advice. If you have questions regarding your tax obligations, please contact an accountant or tax advisor.

​Further reading:

What are the tax implications of using Kickstarter?

What should I do if I need to edit some of my verified information?

If you need to edit any of the information submitted during the verification process, and your project hasn’t launched yet, please contact our Support team and we can look into this for you.

In the event that our payments processor, Stripe, identifies an error with the information submitted, you’ll receive an email from us and be prompted to re-submit your details.

What type of bank account can I use?

The bank account used for your project must be owned by and registered in the name of the verified individual or entity raising funds. You must also be authorized to link it to your project.

What type of bank account do I need to receive a payout?

Your bank account must be a checking account that supports direct deposits in the currency you’re raising funds in. We don’t support wire transfers, savings accounts, or virtual bank accounts, and using them may lead to payout failure.

Some countries have additional things to be aware of:

  • In the United States, your bank account must be able to receive ACH direct deposit transfers
  • In Japan, only “Futsuu” bank accounts are accepted
  • European projects raising funds in Euros can use an IBAN in eligible countries. For example, if your project country is Denmark and you’re raising funds in Euros, the IBAN can be in: Austria, Belgium, Bulgaria, Cyprus, Czech Republic, Denmark, Estonia, Finland, France, Germany, Greece, Hungary, Ireland, Italy, Latvia, Lithuania, Luxembourg, Malta, Netherlands, Norway, Poland, Portugal, Romania, Slovakia, Slovenia, Spain, Sweden, Switzerland, or the United Kingdom

If you have questions about whether or not your bank account is set up for these types of transfers, we recommend checking with your financial institution before linking it to your project.

Where can I find my bank account number?

You can find your bank account number and routing details on your bank statement or in your account details online. For U.S. account holders, please list a bank account that is ACH-enabled. When listing your routing number, be sure to use the one designated for paper and electronic transfers (not wire transfers). If you can’t find it, ask your bank — they’ll definitely know!

Please note that our payments processor, Stripe, only supports checking accounts at this time. More information about ACH transfers is available via their website. You can also find payout account types here.

My bank account is not being accepted when I enter it into the Payment tab

In some instances, bank account details will be formatted by issuing banks with a combination of placeholder zeros as extra digits, dashes, and spaces. If you’re entering your bank details into the Payment tab of your project and they are not being accepted, you’ll want to try to enter the same bank information with no dashes, spaces, or placeholder zeros, just digits — particularly when it comes to entering the bank account number itself.

This should address the issue, however if you continue to experience difficulty adding a bank account to your project, please contact our Support team.

Where can I double-check the bank account details for my project?

If your project is live, you can find the last four digits of your submitted bank account by selecting “Build campaign” from the creator tools menu, located on the left side of your project page. From here head to the Payments tab where you will find the last four digits of the bank account provided.

If your project has already ended, you can find your Funding Summary by going to your project’s Dashboard. From here, click on the blue “View funding summary” link to see a breakdown of the funds raised and the last four digits of the bank account associated with your project.

I need to change the bank account associated with my project, is this possible?

When entering your bank account information, we highly recommend taking extra care to ensure that you have input the correct details. Failure to enter accurate and supported information can lead to payout failure.

In certain cases, we may be able to help with updating the bank account.

  • If your project has not yet launched, contact our Support team to see if they can help with resetting your bank account information
  • If you’ve already launched your project, contact our Support team to see if they can assist with updating your bank account
  • If your project’s payout was already initiated, we can no longer update the bank account or beneficiary details on your project

Why was I asked to verify my information after my project ended?

Our payments processor, Stripe, has notified us that they need some additional information from you. This is required, and not doing so may block pledges, collections, payouts, or other activity on your project. Please follow the link you received and their instructions; if you run into any issues, don’t hesitate to get in touch with us for help with further troubleshooting.

Profile settings

How can I change my profile name or verified identity name on my project/account?

To change your profile name, simply head to Settings and the Edit profile tab, then enter your new chosen account name in the Name box. This name will appear on the site when you post a comment, so make sure you’re not sharing any personal information that you’re uncomfortable with others seeing.

Once you’ve launched a project from your Kickstarter account, the associated profile display name can no longer be edited. This is to maintain transparency across projects. If you have additional context to provide in support of a change in your profile name, please reach out and our team will gladly take a look.

Our policies prevent us from facilitating a name change in all circumstances, but certain scenarios (gender transition, marriage, etc) supersede this policy. If this is something you would like to request, please submit the request by filling out the “Submit a ticket” form, and following the steps below.

  • Enter your Kickstarter account email address (Note: this must be the same email address used on the account tied to your Kickstarter project)
  • My question / comment is about: Changing my Profile/Verified Name
  • Copy and paste the link of your profile’s URL
  • Respond to the following:
    1. Are you the verified identity tied to the project(s) on this account?
    1. What are you requesting to change? (Profile Name, Verified Name, Both)
    1. Why are you requesting a profile/verified name change?
    1. What would you like your profile/verified name changed to?

Once the ticket is submitted a member of our Trust & Safety team will review the details of your request.

How can I enable two-factor authentication?

Enabling two-factor authentication (2FA) on your Kickstarter account adds an additional layer of security to ensure that only you can access your account.

To enable 2FA, head to your Account settings, select “Set up two-factor authentication” and follow the steps to set up your phone to generate a code via an authentication app.

With two-factor authentication enabled, you’ll get a verification code whenever you try to access your account from an unfamiliar device and you’ll need to enter that code in order to continue.

​

​If you are unable to log in after changing devices, or you are having trouble generating a code, please contact our Support team.

​

How can I keep my account secure?

Enabling two-factor authentication (2FA) for your Kickstarter account ensures that only you can access it. With this feature enabled, you will get verification codes via an authentication app, whenever you try to access your account from an unfamiliar device. You will be prompted to enter that code before you can access your account.

How do I delete my account?

If you’d like to delete your Kickstarter account, you can do so by visiting our Privacy Policy and clicking the “Make a Privacy Request” button in the top right, followed by selecting “Delete all my data”. Keep in mind: account deletion is not reversible and any information deleted will no longer be retrievable, so be sure to double-check before proceeding.

When deleting your Kickstarter account, you will be removing yourself as a backer from any projects you have previously supported, as well as deleting your entire backed projects history. This means that creators will no longer be able to send you updates about a project, or have access to the information needed to ship any rewards you may be waiting on.

Deleting your account will not:

  • Automatically hide or delete any project you’ve created on Kickstarter.*

  • Remove your name, comments, or updates from any project you’ve created on Kickstarter.

  • Delete all of your information. We may retain certain information as required by law or for necessary business purposes.

  • Contact our Support team to ask about hiding or deleting a project. We won’t be able to accommodate all requests.

Deleting your account will:

  • Delete the email you use to sign in, and any previously set passwords.
  • Delete your profile.
  • Remove your name as a backer from all successful projects you’ve supported. Please note: this will likely make it impossible for creators to send you rewards.
  • Stop all notifications to your email address.
  • Cancel any projects you’ve started but not launched.
  • Cancel your pledges to any live projects.
  • Remove your name from comments on projects and project updates—so long as they are not your own projects. Your name will remain on any project you’ve created.

What should I do before deleting my account?

Before deleting your Kickstarter account we recommend doing the following first:

  • Make sure to cancel any pledges to live projects. Keep in mind that a pledge can not be canceled if a project has already ended.
  • If there are any projects that are still being fulfilled, we recommend reaching out to the creator to coordinate off-site communication. Deleting your account will remove your name as a backer and the creator will no longer have access to your information.
  • Download a copy of your Kickstarter data, you can find the instructions for how to do this here. The information downloaded will include all of your pledge details, should you need this again in the future.

How do I unsubscribe from emails or adjust which emails I receive?

You can manage which emails you receive by signing in to your Kickstarter account and going to the Notifications tab of your Settings. On this page, you’ll see icons for email and mobile next to each notification type. You can turn these on or off by toggling the corresponding icon (a blue icon means it is set to on).

You can also manage your newsletter subscriptions from this section. If you’re looking to unsubscribe from a newsletter you’ve received, you can do it directly via the “Unsubscribe” link in the footer of the newsletter.

If you’ve received unwanted notifications after updating your notification settings, please get in touch with us and we’ll be happy to take a look.

Using the Kickstarter app? Click here to learn how to manage notifications in the app.

What is a vanity URL?

A vanity URL is a personalized URL for your Kickstarter account. Instead of a series of numbers (e.g., /profile/1234567890), your profile can be recognized with a name or word of your choosing (e.g., /profile/ralphwiggum). If you’d like to create one, you can do so on the Edit profile tab of your Settings.

As a project creator, you’ll also see the option to create a vanity URL for your Kickstarter account when completing the People section of your project editor.

If you set a vanity URL prior to launching your project this will change your project’s URL. Instead of the default series of numbers (e.g., /projects/1234567890/project-title), the URL will include your chosen name or word (e.g., /projects/ralphwiggum/project-title).

When setting a vanity URL remember to make sure it’s relevant to you as a creator, and not specific to the project. The vanity URL you enter will be used for all other campaigns launched from your creator account.

Can I reset my vanity URL?

If you made a mistake when entering your vanity URL, or would like to change it to something else, you can reset your vanity URL on the Edit profile tab of your Settings. After clicking the “Reset” button, simply enter your new vanity URL and click “Save settings” to confirm.

It’s important to keep in mind that resetting the vanity URL may cause existing URLs to break. For instance, if you are linking to your Kickstarter profile from somewhere else on the web, it would be a good idea to update this after resetting your vanity URL.

​Interested in getting your project’s final URL before launching? Find out how in this article.

What’s my profile page, and what appears on it?

When you create a Kickstarter account, a basic profile page is created for you. It displays your chosen account name, the date the account was created, a list of projects you have backed or launched, and whether you are a Superbacker. You can also add as much or as little information as you want — like a profile picture, a bio, a link to your website or social media, and a personalized vanity URL.

For fun, each profile includes a colorful “pie” that shows which categories you’ve backed projects in. Back a project in every category to fill your pie with colorful slices!

Your profile page isn’t searchable on Kickstarter, and it can’t be found via search engines like Google. But whenever your account name appears on the site (for instance, when you post comments or send messages), people can click that account name to see your profile. Make sure anything you add follows our Community Guidelines, and don’t share personal information that you’re uncomfortable with others seeing.

Can I make my profile private?

Profiles on Kickstarter are private by default. To check the status of yours, go to the Edit profile tab under Settings and scroll to the bottom of the page. There you will see the heading “Privacy” with a checkbox underneath, make sure this box is checked to set your profile to private.

For transparency, creators will not be able to set their full profile to private.

Promoting your project

How can I spread the word about my project?

Once you’ve planned your promotional efforts for your project, you’ll want to start thinking about how you’ll spread the word. Finding people beyond your immediate circle to share your project can be very helpful in getting the word out.

Start by thinking about who could help amplify your project to your community, such as fans, friends, followers, and draw up a list.

Additionally, you may want to reach out to some press outlets to see if they might be interested in writing about your project. Think about sites that may be relevant to your project. For example, are there any publishing blogs that would be interested in covering your 21st century dystopian romance novel?

Keep in mind, reaching out to small publications, blogs, and local outlets can be just as valuable as larger news sites, where your story might get lost in the shuffle.

Next, think about your messaging. Take some time to think about your communication plan and carefully craft what it is you want to say about your project before approaching anyone on your list.

Don’t forget to also plan your strategy for promoting your project on social media. Think about how you’ll build a strategic approach to social and make the most of Kickstarter’s social badges.

You can share your project to social channels directly from your project page. Beneath the “Back this project” button, you’ll see a link to share it on Facebook, Twitter, through Email, or to embed. The Embed icon (“</>”) will give you your project’s short link, as well as a link to embed the project on an external site.

And finally, many creators will be contacted by marketing and promotion services that promise backers. For most projects, these services aren’t necessary. However, if you are considering working with one, make sure to thoroughly research them first.

My funding has stalled after a few days. What should I do?

We know all about the dreaded feeling that comes after the initial excitement around the launch of a campaign slows down. But it’s important not to panic. This “plateau” is normal for most campaigns and there’s a lot that you can do continue spreading the word:

  • Ask friends and family to share your project with their own networks
  • ​Getting your project shared beyond your immediate audience can really help.
  • Activate any connections that you have with press or bloggers
  • Make sure to only focus on outlets relevant to your project.
  • Spice up your promotion strategy
  • You could host a Q&A session, add a new reward, or just change your messaging around your project. This post can help with ideas.
  • Learn from what a fellow creator tried
  • ​Many creators have experienced a funding plateau, check out how others handled it.
  • And most importantly, stay calm!
  • ​No one can tell your project’s story and get people more excited about it than you can.

Setting up your project’s pre-launch page

Being able to share your project prior to launching is a vital step when planning your promotion strategy. As a creator you can now set-up a pre-launch page for your project, to share the vital information necessary to start building hype for your campaign and present a direct call-to-action for potential backers to be notified when your project has launched.

You can continue to keep followers and potential backers in the loop and excited for your project’s launch with pre-launch updates. When you publish an update to your pre-launch page, your followers will receive an email notification.

Your pre-launch page, when viewed on desktop, will automatically include the following information about your project:

  • Your project’s title and subtitle
  • Your project’s image
  • Your project’s category
  • Your project’s location
  • An editable pre-launch project description*
  • Your creator name** and profile avatar
  • Any pre-launch updates

*Please note that pre-launch pages viewed on the Kickstarter app will not include the information added to the project description section of your pre-launch page.

**When a backer clicks on your creator name this will open up your creator bio page. The number of created projects displayed in your creator bio will reflect launched projects only, and will update once your new project goes live.

For first-time creators, after your project has been submitted for review and approved, you can set up your pre-launch page by heading to the Promotion tab of your project editor. For repeat creators, you will be able to access the Promotion tab ahead of review. You’ll be prompted to activate your pre-launch page after you’ve confirmed your project URL. Your project URL will serve as your pre-launch page URL until you’ve launched your campaign.

Once your pre-launch page is active you can continue to edit your project, provided any changes made are in compliance with our guidelines. When you’re ready to launch, head back to your project editor to prepare for launch.

Please note: Launching a project is a manual process, it isn’t possible to schedule an automatic launch for a predetermined date. After clicking the “Launch project” button your project will go live.

Pre-launch pages will be searchable alongside all other projects when browsing our Discover pages. You can also view all pre-launch pages under the “Upcoming Projects” section. We generally recommend setting up and sharing your pre-launch page at least one week ahead of your intended launch date.

​How will potential backers be notified, and as a creator can I contact them directly?

When shared with your community, potential backers will have the option to follow your pre-launch page and be notified when your campaign launches by clicking “Notify me on launch”. We’ll then send a launch notification email to your followers when your project goes live.

Once 10 people or more opt-in to be notified of your launch, we’ll reflect that number of followers on your pre-launch page.

It isn’t possible to directly message your pre-launch page followers, however you can share information about your upcoming campaign with followers by posting a pre-launch update. A pre-launch update will be visible to anyone who visits your project page, and a notification will be sent to all current followers when it’s published. You can find the steps for posting a pre-launch update here.

​Will my preview page URL and pre-launch page URL be the same?

The URLs for your project’s preview page (when sharing is turned on) and pre-launch page will be different, meaning you’ll be able to share both links. For instance, you may wish to share the project preview with a limited group of trusted peers so that they can offer feedback on the project before you launch, and then at the same time share your pre-launch page more publicly to help build excitement. The “Notify me on launch” functionality works the same for both pages, so when someone follows your project from either page, they’ll get the launch notification email.

​Deactivating your pre-launch page

If you decide that now is not the right time for your campaign, you can deactivate your pre-launch page (any project followers will still be followers if you re-activate that same pre-launch page later on). To do so, simply head to the Promotion tab of your project editor and click “Deactivate”.

What about marketing and promotion services that promise backers?

Kickstarter doesn’t explicitly endorse or have an affiliation with any advertising, promotional, or marketing services. If someone makes you think otherwise, please let us know.

BEWARE: Scammers may pose as genuine backers by pledging a large amount to your project and messaging you with promises to increase their pledge further and/or bring more backers to your project.

The activity starts with a high dollar pledge followed by a direct message expressing interest in your project. The goal of this scam is to gain your trust so that they can sell you marketing or promotional services and send seemingly legit backer accounts to your project.

Please keep in mind, these type of accounts are often not backed by actual money. When it’s time to collect the pledges, their payments often fail and get dropped.

Lots of creators have approached us about getting messages from marketing or promotion services that promise backers. Bottom line, we think that for most projects, these services aren’t necessary. However, if you’re considering working with one of these services, make sure to thoroughly research them first, and check the following:

  • Get details on the exact services that they provide. Are these things that you and your team could do on your own?
  • Check their track record. Look up reviews, and if possible talk to other creators that have used these services to ask them what worked and what didn’t.
  • Check our Partner Directory. Find approved partners with experience supporting creators on Kickstarter.

If you feel that a marketing or promotion service might be spamming you, please let us know by marking these messages as spam or by contacting our Support team.

Please note: Creators are not allowed to directly, or indirectly through the use of a third party, inflate or otherwise misrepresent the number of or amount pledged by backers. Paying a service to manufacture fake backers for your project, or violating our rules against self-funding in any other capacity, will lead to project suspension, account restriction, or more.

Under Kickstarter’s Terms of Use and Privacy Policy, users may not distribute unsolicited or unauthorized advertising or promotional material, or any junk mail, spam, or chain letters.

If you receive a message from a user asking to communicate off platform, please remember that you do so at your own risk. We don’t control or endorse those sites, and are not responsible for their content or their privacy and marketing practices. As a reminder, Kickstarter employees will never contact you via direct messages with unsolicited offers.

​I received a direct message from someone claiming to be a Kickstarter employee offering promotion and marketing services, is this legitimate?

No, Kickstarter employees will never contact you via direct messages with unsolicited offers. This also extends to Kickstarter’s Community Advisory Council. Any contact made by our team members will be done via email, from an @kickstarter.com email domain.

We encourage you to be wary of users who ask you to send them money off platform, especially in exchange for marketing services. Scammers use urgency to get you to send money; Be wary anytime someone wants you to act now. With that said, if you’ve sent money to someone else and believe you could be a victim of a marketing service scam, please contact your card issuer immediately.

Creators should exercise healthy caution when accepting large pledges from unknown backers, especially those pledged without selecting a reward, or selecting a reward far below the contribution amount. If you’re ever feeling unsure about a backer or pledge, please don’t hesitate to reach out and we’ll be happy to investigate. Remember – you can always say no!

Don’t take any actions in reliance on collecting any of the money pledged until you actually have the ability to withdraw it from your account and spend it. For example, you should be cautious about entering into binding contracts for manufacturing your rewards until after your project has successfully funded and you’ve received the payout.

Lastly, if you receive a message through Kickstarter that appears to be spam, or that violates our Community Guidelines, please make sure to mark it as spam by clicking the “Report spam” button on the message. This helps to flag any spam messages to our Trust & Safety team for investigation. Our Trust and Safety team may take proactive action on suspected spam accounts, and their associated pledges, to prevent pledges that may inflate or otherwise misrepresent the number of or amount pledged by backers.

​Pledges that were referred to my campaign by a marketing partner are coming through then subsequently being canceled. What’s happening?

Kickstarter’s Trust & Safety team proactively monitors pledges, to ensure these pledges are sincere. If our team determines a backer has been creating pledges in bad faith, we will take action on their account and may cancel their pledge(s). If you have questions about the legitimacy of a pledge, please contact our Support team.

What does the Kickstarter Team badge in Messages mean?

When you receive a message in your inbox on Kickstarter, you may see a Kickstarter Team badge next to the sender’s name. If you see this badge, you can safely assume that the person messaging you is a current Kickstarter employee.

While we may not message you directly on the platform often, this badge is the source of truth for knowing if the message sender works at Kickstarter. This means that if you receive a message from someone claiming to work at Kickstarter, but they do not have the Kickstarter Team badge, you should not engage with them – even if their profile has the name of a real employee.

Unfortunately, like many other platforms, we’ve recently seen an influx of spam messages from dishonest users targeting creators. We are working hard to improve our spam detection and block these users from the platform. In the meantime, we hope this update helps creators differentiate between spammers and real Kickstarter team members.

You can read more about spam messaging here.

When and how should I start planning my promotion strategy?

You should start planning your promotion strategy as soon as you decide that you want to run a project on Kickstarter!

As a first step, think about who your existing community is—friends, family, social media followers—and organize their information into an actionable contact list. This is something other project creators have found to be helpful for staying organized ahead of their project’s launch.

Next, consider how you’ll message your community. Who do you want to reach by email? How will you use social media? Do you need to plan a press release ahead of time? These are important things to think about when organizing your communication plan.

Check out our one-sheet on Spreading the Word About Your Kickstarter Project for more tips on getting the word out about your upcoming launch.

Don’t forget to generate your project’s URL before launching too. Once generated, you’ll be able to share your project’s final URL with any media, or add to any promotional material you have planned ahead of your launch.

Being able to share your project prior to launching is another important step when planning your promotion strategy. After you submit your project for review and you’ve generated your project’s URL, you’ll be given the option to set up a Pre-launch page for your project—this can be used to share the vital information necessary to start building up hype. The Pre-launch page will also give backers the option to be notified as soon as your project launches. We recommend identifying at least 10 potential backers you can count on to be a part of your project and encouraging them to follow the Pre-launch page. That way, they can pledge the second your project goes live and help get your campaign off to a great start!

Read more about how the Pre-launch page works here.

Reporting a project

How can I report something to Kickstarter?

We appreciate you bringing anything out of the ordinary to our attention. We’ve outlined how you can help to keep Kickstarter a safe and fun place to launch and back projects below.

​How do I report a project?

If you need to notify us of a project that you feel is in violation of Our Rules, Community Guidelines, or Terms of Use (you can also check our Copyright and Trademark policies), you can do so using the following steps (Note that these steps are pictured as they appear on a desktop browser; the steps will look slightly different but follow the same flow in the Kickstarter app):

  1. Scroll to the bottom of the a pre-launch or campaign page—you’ll see a “Report this project to Kickstarter” button at the bottom of the page:

  2. Choose the category under which your report falls—concerns about project completion, rule breaking, spam or abusive behavior, or intellectual property violation:

  3. This will open up sub-categories depending on your choice. Choose the appropriate options based on your report. For example, if you choose “This project breaks one of Our Rules”, you’ll be presented with these options:

  4. Choose any further options as applicable, then enter a summary of your report in the message box. Once entered, you’ll click the blue “Report Project” button:

Once a member of our Trust & Safety team has reviewed your report, you should receive an email to let you know if they’ve taken any action. For reports about rewards or communication issues after a project funds, we cannot follow up on each individual report. However, in these cases we may use your report as a basis for taking action or closely monitoring the project in question.

Please note: At this time the “Concerns about rewards, project completion or creator communication.” option is not available to select when reporting a project via the Kickstarter app.

​How do I report spam messages?

If you receive a message through Kickstarter that appears to be spam, or is outside of our Community Guidelines, please make sure to mark it as spam by clicking the “Report spam” button on the message. This helps to flag any spam messages to our Trust & Safety team for investigation.

​How do I report comments on a project?

If you notice any comments on your project page that appear to be violating our Community Guidelines, are spam comments, or irrelevant to the project, please click the “Report” button on the comment and select the reason for your report. This will temporarily hide the comment from view and flag it to our Trust & Safety team. If they deem it necessary to take action, the comment will be removed by Kickstarter. If no action needs to be taken the comment will be restored and it will not be possible to report it again. The comment will now show as “Reported” on the project page.

Please note: Only creators can report comments on their project.

​How do I report a backer that is violating Community Guidelines?

If you believe a backer is violating our Community Guidelines, please feel free to report them. Our Trust & Safety team will review your report and take action if they deem it necessary.

To report a backer, head to the bottom of the project page and click “Report this project to Kickstarter”. This will provide you with the option to “Report spam or abusive behavior”.

If you are the project creator, you can report a backer by clicking the “Report” button on the backer’s comment.

​Please note that it is only possible to submit a report for a specific project once. Creators will be able to report multiple comments on their project, but each comment can only be reported one time. If you’ve tried to report a project, a comment, or an abusive backer, for the first time and received an error message, please feel free to contact our Support team.

How do I report a copyright violation?

If you’re not sure whether material on Kickstarter infringes your copyright, please talk with a lawyer before filing a complaint with us. Misrepresenting that material infringes your copyright may subject you to liability for damages, including attorneys’ fees and costs incurred by project creators or other parties.

If you’ve spoken with a lawyer and think your material is being infringed, try contacting the project creator directly. If that doesn’t work, you can submit a complaint using this form.

For more information, please take a look at our Copyright and DMCA Policy.

How do I report a trademark violation?

If you believe a Kickstarter project is infringing on a trademark you hold, you can report a trademark violation by emailing [email protected]. We’ll evaluate and respond to every report we receive.

Please review our Trademark Policy for more information about what details to include in your report.

Troubleshooting

Can I pledge to my own project?

Pledging to your own project is not allowed by either Kickstarter or our payments processor. Doing so violates our guidelines and may lead to suspension of your project.

Common Account Issues

I created my account with Facebook, how do I log in?

We no longer support logging in through Facebook. For accounts created with Facebook it will now be necessary to log in using your email address.

  • Go to the Log in page and enter the email address originally tied to your Facebook account.
  • Check your email inbox for a 6-digit verification code from Kickstarter.
  • Enter the code on the verification screen to continue.
  • If your account has two-factor authentication (2FA) enabled, open your authenticator app and enter the current 6-digit code to complete sign in.

If you are already logged in, you will need to make sure that you are fully logged out before following the above steps.

Please note that the email address you will log in to Kickstarter with is the same as the one associated with your Facebook account.

Can I consolidate multiple accounts?

Each Kickstarter account must be linked to a unique email address. As such we don’t support merging accounts. However, there are a few options that can help you to consolidate your accounts:

  • If you have any live pledges, you can cancel those and re-pledge on your preferred account. This would help to keep all of your pledges in one place for the future.
    • Please note that it is not possible to transfer a pledge from one Kickstarter account to another. If a pledge has been collected already it will remain on the account that made the pledge.
  • Alternatively, you can delete any accounts you no longer wish to use to free up an email address.
    • Please note that when a Kickstarter account is deleted this will also delete the entire backed projects history associated with that account.

I forgot my password, how do I log in?

Kickstarter no longer requires a password to log in. Instead, enter your email address on the Log in page then check your inbox for a one-time verification code. Once this code is entered on the verification screen you will be logged in.

My email address is not recognized when I try to log in?

If your email address is not recognized when trying to log in, it’s possible you may have used a different email address when creating your account.

For accounts created using an Apple ID, you will need to select the “Sign in with Apple” button on the Log in page, then enter your Apple ID in the window that opens up.

If you are certain that you are entering the correct email, and your account was not created with an Apple ID, it could be that the email address you entered previously had a typo or variant spelling.

In this case, you will need to get in touch with our Support team who can help to locate your account. Please have the details of your most recent pledge to hand when getting in touch, in order to answer a few security questions.

I’m not receiving emails from Kickstarter

If you’re having difficulty receiving emails from Kickstarter for a project you’ve backed, we recommend first double-checking your email notification settings. To do so, sign in to your Kickstarter account and go to the Notifications page. Here you’ll see icons for email and mobile notifications that can be turned on or off by toggling the corresponding icons.

I received a notification stating emails from Kickstarter have been bouncing

As a first step, follow the link provided on the notification in order to check the email address that is currently tied to your Kickstarter account and update it if necessary.

If everything is in order, meaning the email address is correct (i.e. free of typos) and active, we encourage you to add [email protected] to your contacts list. This helps to ensure that our emails to you are not being intercepted by a spam filter.

If you’re still not receiving emails from Kickstarter, it’s possible that there may be an issue with your email provider. Please try reaching out to them next, to see if anything might be going on on their end.

In instances where you have tried all of the above and are still experiencing email delivery issues, please contact our Support team for further troubleshooting.

I’m unable to complete the CAPTCHA challenge to access Kickstarter

If you are having difficulty completing the CAPTCHA challenge, the first step you should take is to report the issue directly to PerimeterX by clicking the “Report an issue” button in the footer. Make sure to have the reference ID ready (this appears on the challenge page).

Please note: The challenge page requires Javascript and cookies to be enabled, additionally some browser extensions, including ad blockers, may block it from loading.

How do I remove a backer from my project?

While your project is live

To remove a backer while your project is live, you can do so by either clicking on their name or the actions menu (the three dots) in any comments they have left on your project page, or by clicking on their name in your backer report.

This will open a modal with a button to remove the backer. After clicking this and confirming the action, the backer will be removed and their pledge canceled. Any comments they have left on your project will also be removed from view.

The backer will be notified via email that their pledge has been canceled and they have been removed from your project.

After your project has ended

If your project ended successfully, you can choose to remove a backer from your project by refunding the backer in full. When a full refund is issued to a backer, they will be dropped from your project and any comments they have left on your project will be removed from view.

​Please note that only a creator can remove a backer or issue a refund. Collaborators of a project will not have access to these actions.

I can’t find an answer to my question, what should I do?

Our Kickstarter Support team is on hand and ready to answer your questions. As a first step, we recommend exploring our Help Center to find articles with information that may resolve your issue.

We don’t currently offer phone support, but you can contact our Support team and one of our specialists will be happy to help. These resources are easily accessible and free.

I’m having trouble verifying my business information, what should I do?

If you are having trouble verifying your business’s information, make sure that the name, address, and tax information matches exactly what is shown on your government issued documents. Typos are the most common reason for verification failures, so we recommend double-checking everything.
